# VOOL — Apple / Linux Lane Capability Matrix

## 2026-09-11 Last-Run Repair Checkpoint

Source-only candidate: non-destructive response recovery and request-sized planner
capacity. No new packaged build or native acceptance. Full portfolio arithmetic,
complete presentation, computational coverage and hard provider deadlines remain
open. The trial single-clause widening was withdrawn after a lost-demand probe.
Evidence: `validation-logs/structured-output-build-20260911/LAST_RUN_REPAIRS.md`.


**Lane:** Apple (macOS 26.5.1, Apple M4, Darwin 25.5.0) — the Unix side (`.sh` / `.command` / `install_vool.sh`).
**Date:** 2026-07-21
**Against:** `main` after merging the 16-commit Windows-lane batch (incl. the paid-cloud lane `06fca0a`, version `0.4.3`).
**Method:** every row was driven live on this box — tool intents through `execute_runtime_tool` with an isolated `VOOL_HOME`, HTTP handlers through the in-process `dispatch_get`/`dispatch_post`, and chat against the running daemon (`127.0.0.1:11435`, an older build `105fa4a`, noted where it matters). Nothing here is a test-suite inference.
**Status of the product itself:** closed test. Nothing here is third-party security reviewed, and no component of this lane is on mainnet.

This is the Apple counterpart to `WINDOWS_CAPABILITY_MATRIX.md`, driven to 1:1 against every row KAS marked VERIFIED there. **KAS reads this; KAS does not edit it. I read theirs; I do not edit theirs.**

---

## How to read this

Same four statuses as the Windows matrix, meaning exactly the same things: **VERIFIED** (ran it here, watched it work, evidence inline) · **BROKEN** (ran and failed) · **PARTIAL** (ran, part worked, boundary stated) · **UNVERIFIED** (not exercised — never "probably works"). Reachable ≠ working. A green test suite is not VERIFIED for a runtime row, and a path exercised with a mocked transport says so.

**Environment this box has that the Windows box did not** (so some Windows-UNVERIFIED rows are VERIFIED here): `sandbox-exec` present; `qwen3` models installed (thinking-capable); real `nomic-embed-text` embeddings.

---

## Out of scope: Windows — results that do NOT transfer

- **`credential_store` at-rest permissions are POSIX-only** (`_save_raw` `chmod`s on POSIX; Windows inherits the profile ACL). Our posture does not transfer — KAS checks theirs.
- **`sandbox.run_command` uses the macOS `sandbox-exec` backend.** It WORKS here (see Execution); the Windows matrix records it hard-blocked there for lack of a kernel backend. Working here says nothing about there.
- **Path/denylist results do not transfer** — macOS APFS is case-insensitive by default; the Windows guard relies on `os.path.normcase` for NTFS; Linux needs its own case-sensitivity check.
- **Packaging is a macOS `.app`/`.dmg`** (`build_macos_app.sh`), not Inno Setup / WebView2 / `.cmd` / `.vbs`.
- **Build identity now carries a platform tag** — the runtime `build_id` is `…+macos+<commit>` on this lane and `…+windows+<commit>` on KAS's, from one runtime-detected marker (no per-lane config edit). KAS: re-verify your build_id now ends `+windows+…`.

| `workspace.list_files` / `list_tree` | VERIFIED | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane (`54c05e6`)** | The old row said VERIFIED on "real repo listings", which is exactly the reachable-≠-working trap this matrix warns about: the tool returned a listing, the listing was a lie by omission, and the row could not tell. Measured 2026-07-31 on the `openclaw-skills` workspace (682 eligible files, 275 under `tests/`): the 200-path clamp plus a `sorted(rglob("*"))` walk cut the inventory mid-alphabet at `plugins/`, so `tests/` was never enumerated and the audit reported **"No tests discovered — workspace-wide" as a P1 finding on a repo with 275 test files**. The `truncated` flag existed but sat in `details`, which the answering model does not read. The clamp saved ~5.2k input tokens and cost a full second audit run — net ~9× negative on the single incident — while saving **zero I/O**, because the walk and sort complete before any cap applies. Fixed: ceilings 200→5000 for both tools (a path is ~43 bytes), the walk decoupled to a 50000 scan ceiling so the reply knows the true total and exact drop, the truncated body now states **in the body** that it is an alphabetical prefix that cannot support a conclusion of absence, and `list_tree` no longer reports `status: executed` when it truncated. **Driven, not inferred:** 682/682 listed with `status executed` and all 275 test files present; at `limit=200` the body carries "…and 482 more file(s) not listed (682 eligible in total)". Suite 8673 passed; restoring the 200 ceiling fails `test_a_real_project_sized_tree_lists_in_full_by_default`. **Cross-lane: shared Python — KAS gets this and should re-verify on Windows.** |
| A turn produces a deliverable, not internal state | UNVERIFIED | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane 2026-08-01** | Driven against `~/Desktop/openclaw-skills` and compared with a competitor that finished the same job. The reply to a build request began `["security.py", "test_security.py", "README.md"]import hmac…` — one raw model completion. Cause was NOT a missing formatter: `extract_path_list` used `rfind("]")`, which lands inside `list[str]`/`Optional[bytes]` in the Python the model went on to generate, so `json.loads` got the array PLUS a slab of code, failed, the line fallback rejected every line for containing spaces, and the build reported `handled=False` — dropping the turn into a verbatim-passthrough point. Fixed with a balanced forward scan that ignores trailing prose. `looks_like_internal_payload` is the backstop: a filename array welded to non-JSON, an unclosed `<think>` block, or a fabricated `<function_results>` is treated as NO answer so the wording fallback runs. Build replies are now RUNTIME-composed receipts — file table with line counts, exact command, real exit code, ✅/❌ per outcome, subject stated — because a model having a bad turn cannot be relied on to format its own receipt. **Sabotage:** restoring `rfind` fails `test_a_file_list_survives_the_model_continuing_past_it`, nothing else. |
| Prove-it-before-you-fix-it reaches a lane that can | BROKEN | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane 2026-08-01** | Asked to prove a bug with a failing test, the lane produced a suite that exits 0 and reported success — a fabricated proof. Two causes. The fix loop regenerates source up to `MAX_FIX_ROUNDS` **specifically to turn a failing test green**, so the deliverable was being repaired away; and the exit code was discarded (`sandbox.run_command` reports "executed" even for a failing suite, so the verdict was scraped from stdout while `returncode` sat unread in `outcome.details`). `expected_test_outcome` now travels with the build: when it is `"fail"` the fix loop is skipped entirely and a PASSING run sets `proof_failed`, so `build_finished_successfully` returns False and the reply leads with "❌ The bug is not proven." The builder also received only the raw sentence — which is why a request about `api/apache/liquefy_apache_repetition_v1.py` produced an unrelated `security.py` — so the audited paths now travel with it. This is the gate that catches a model that is confidently wrong WITH correct evidence, which the claim verifier structurally cannot: it checks whether claimed tests, deps and symbols EXIST, never whether a claimed BUG is real. |
| An audit citation points at the code it names | BROKEN | **VERIFIED — FIXED and driven live 2026-08-01** | Asked for the highest-risk bug in `api/apache/liquefy_apache_repetition_v1.py` under the explicit rule *"Cite the exact file and line range"*, the reply cited **147-160** for "the loop iterating while `p_ip < len(c_ip)`" and "the helper `next_v` (line 152)". In the real 210-line file that loop is at **132**, `next_v` at **137**, and 147-160 is a different block — the `raise ValueError` arms of the RLE expander. The mechanism described was real code; every coordinate was invented. Cause: the target excerpt is read with `verbatim=True`, which is precisely the branch of `_read_file` that renders **without line numbers**, while the prompt said "cite line content". The model had no line to cite and guessed, and `audit_claim_verifier` cannot catch it — it only range-checks (`line > total`), and 147 < 210 passes. Fixed by numbering at RENDER time only: `_numbered_excerpt` prefixes each row `N: `, truncates by line so the last number belongs to a whole line, and the instruction now demands a `path:line` coordinate **copied from the prefix**. The stored `sources` stay verbatim deliberately — `audit_claim_verifier.line_at` indexes them with `splitlines()[n-1]`, so numbering at the source would shift every coordinate it checks. **Driven against the real file:** all 210 lines carried, and lines 132/137/147/160/210 in the excerpt each match the real file byte-for-byte. **Sabotage:** restoring the raw character slice fails `test_the_line_the_model_is_told_to_cite_is_a_line_it_can_see`, nothing else. **Cross-lane: shared Python — KAS gets this.** **Boundary: PARTIAL, not VERIFIED.** The defect measurements are from the live daemon at `5156bea` and its runtime event ledger, and the citation fix is driven against the real 210-line file on disk. The FIXES are exercised in-process — the build-lane cases script `generate_fn`/`run_tool_fn` rather than calling a provider, which is a mocked transport. The running daemon is still on `5156bea` (pre-fix); no live chat turn has exercised any of this yet. **LIVE EVIDENCE (daemon 8fb1a25, same file, same request):** the free cloud model produced 27 distinct line citations and ALL 27 land on the code it described — `line 122` is `pos += l_idx`, `line 128` is the `for _ in range(10)` chunk loop, `lines 74-76` are the run flush, `line 69` is the pattern key, `lines 38-106` and `116-169` are exactly `compress` and `decompress`. A later pinned `qwen3:8b` run cited `160-162`, which is exactly the `pid > len(pat_list)` guard it was describing. Compare the pre-fix run: `147-160` for a loop at 132 and a helper at 137. **Boundary:** the COORDINATES are now checkable; the REASONING is still the model's own — the qwen3:8b answer correctly pointed at 160-162 and then claimed `pat_list` is built from `unique_tokens`, which is false (it is built from `pat_key` tuples at 72/140; `unique_tokens` feeds only the search index at 87-88). This fix makes a finding checkable; it does not make a small model right. |

| Per-project approval, low-risk only | UNVERIFIED | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane 2026-08-01** | The operator had to approve **every single write** on a multi-step task until they disabled permissions globally — the worst outcome a permission system can produce. `resolve_approval` forced scope to `"once"` for any `edit_batch` and `scope_options` omitted `"task"`, so in-project edits could never escalate. NOTE: `context["project_permissions"]` is DENY-ONLY by design ("can only narrow, never widen"), so this is a SEPARATE grant beside `_TASK_APPROVALS`, not a reuse of that dict. Low-risk set is reads, listings, safe commands and writes resolving INSIDE the project root — nothing else. **Adversarially verified by execution:** `../outside/secret.txt`, an absolute path outside the root, a symlink inside the project pointing out of it, and a write naming no target are all refused; `FINANCIAL_ACTION`, deletes, moves, sends, git push and arbitrary commands are absent from the set; and the grant is evaluated AFTER the mode DENY branch so it can only turn a prompt into an allow, never resurrect a Plan-mode write. **Open:** `revoke_project_approval()` exists and is tested but has no UI — nothing calls it. |
| An approved turn resumes instead of re-running | BROKEN | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane 2026-08-01** | One approval cost a full turn re-run: `resumeApprovedTurn()` popped the assistant bubble and re-ran everything. The fingerprint covered the EXACT arguments, so a re-planned turn with slightly different arguments missed the token the client had already consumed, and a NEW approval was raised; worse, the resumed identical call tripped the duplicate guard and broke to grounded synthesis INSTEAD OF EXECUTING. The fingerprint now hashes the resolved action (intent + resolved target paths + side-effect classes) while still binding session + task + mode revision; the client holds its token until the server acts; and a payload resuming an approval is exempt from the duplicate guard once. Approvals now reach `routing_decisions.jsonl` — 893 decisions were logged in one day with exactly ONE mentioning approval, so the loop the operator hit was invisible in the ledger. |
| Reasoning is never delivered as the answer | BROKEN | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane 2026-08-01** | One turn's entire visible reply was internal monologue — "Let me look at…", "But wait…" — cut off mid-sentence at 7,786 tokens, with no answer and two explicit instructions unaddressed. The cure already existed in one lane: the builder sends `think: True` so Ollama quarantines reasoning into `message.thinking` and reads `content` only, while `adapters/openai_compatible_adapter.py` defaulted thinking OFF — so on the general path the monologue arrived AS the content. Now quarantined on both. The lead-pattern guard only fired when the monologue was the very first token (no `re.MULTILINE`) with `let me` limited to `check\|inspect\|look\|think`, so "Okay, so the user wants…", "Hmm, let me…" and "But wait…" all escaped; widened. A reasoning-only reply is now a FAILED LANE that escalates to the next ranked model, reusing the soft-failure path the empty-completion fix established. A real answer containing "but wait, actually" mid-text survives untouched. |
| A STREAMED reply gets the same output guard as a buffered one | UNVERIFIED | **PARTIAL — FIXED, driven through the real transport in-process 2026-08-01** | The monologue fix above guarded only the BUFFERED reply. The shipped UI posts `stream:true`, where `_stream_response` (`core/memory_first_router.py`) emits every raw chunk as a `model_output_chunk` event and the transport (`core/web/api/runtime.py::stream_agent_with_events`) held back only line-anchored tool-call braces — `looks_like_internal_payload` and `suppress_internal_reasoning_leak` ran on no streamed byte. So on a streamed turn the raw monologue shipped verbatim, and because chunks had been yielded, the guarded buffered reply was then DISCARDED for a bare usage footer. Fixed with `StreamReleaseGate` (`core/model_output_guard.py`) at the transport choke point. Chunks are unretractable, so the gate's only power is WHEN to release: every monologue detector keys on how the reply OPENS, so the gate holds only the head — released the moment it provably diverges from every risky opening (ordinary prose clears within a word or two), held to turn end when it matches one, where the FULL text is judged by the same `internal_payload_or_monologue` the buffered path uses. The gate can therefore never withhold a reply the buffered path would have delivered — and a suppressed stream has shipped ZERO bytes, so the transport falls back to delivering the guarded buffered reply instead of the footer. Closed in the same pass: the structured `summary`/`bullets` synthesis branch and the `respond.direct` lane printed model text unguarded (both now refuse internal payload and fall back to their grounded lanes); a leading `<think>` block on the stream path now neither streams its reasoning nor survives in the joined buffered text (`strip_reasoning_block` on join — the adapter cannot strip per NDJSON frame, the join can); and a fully-held clean reply is now delivered exactly once (the old flush yielded the tail AND the buffered response). Driven through the REAL `stream_agent_with_events` generator with real runtime events, chunked as a provider delivers, using the verbatim live-captured qwen3:8b monologue. 31 tests (`tests/test_streamed_monologue_never_reaches_the_screen.py`, `tests/test_internal_payload_is_never_an_answer.py` — the guard branches at `turn_reasoning.py` and `orchestrator.py` are now driven behaviourally, not grepped for); 7 sabotages, each naming the test(s) that bit. BOUNDARY: in-process transport drive, not a live streamed daemon turn — the running daemon predates this fix. Shared runtime — KAS rebases. |
| Context budget scales to the answering model | BROKEN | **PARTIAL — the arithmetic landed but never ran on a live turn; window now pre-resolved, driven in-process 2026-08-01** | Three defects, found in two passes. First pass: `must_keep` was a SORT KEY ONLY (`context_budgeter.py`) — the `max_items` and token gates treated it like anything else, so 8 mandatory items totalling 930 tokens were cut to fit a 180-token layer and `bootstrap-task` was dropped, leaving the model with **no task description**. Mandatory items are now exempt from `max_items` and TRIMMED rather than dropped, with a half-layer cap so one oversized item cannot starve the rest. But the deeper bug was the budget being a FIXED CONSTANT: the same 180 tokens whether the turn ran on a 4k local model or a 200k paid one, so an operator sending a long considered request to an expensive model got the identical shredded context as the smallest model on the box. `scale_budget_to_window` grows every layer to a 25% share of the answering model's REAL prompt window, in the proportions the strategy already declares, and **never shrinks** — an unknown window leaves the declared values untouched, so no existing turn can get worse. **Measured against 930 tokens of mandatory context:** at 24576 (qwen3:8b) bootstrap is 2594 and at 200k it is 21111 — NOTHING is trimmed on any real model. The 380 floor remains only for the unknown case, where at 180 the safety rule was cut mid-sentence, keeping "Sensitive conversation is allowed…" and losing "do not confuse it with permission to take action". **Second pass (this branch): the scaler above NEVER RAN on a live turn.** It reads the window from `source_context` (`available_prompt_tokens` / `model_context_window` / `context_window` / `num_ctx`) and nothing on any live path wrote any of those keys — the server-built context in `core/web/api/service.py` does not carry them and no client volunteers them — so the loader saw 0 on every real turn and the declared floors held everywhere. The 2594/21111 figures above reproduce as function arithmetic only; this row’s earlier VERIFIED overstated it. The hole is ordering: the answering model is picked by `memory_router.resolve` only AFTER `context_loader.load` builds the context, so at scale time the runtime could not know the window. FIXED by pre-resolution: `MemoryFirstRouter.planned_context_window_tokens` runs the same candidate ranking `resolve` runs later — an explicit owner pick is trusted outright (it is a strict execution contract), the auto lane takes the MINIMUM positive window across the ranked swarm (failover can land on any of them, and with never-shrink a low guess only means less growth), paid manifests are excluded (an auto fallback never holds a reservation, and planning must not build one), and any unknown window plans 0 — do not scale. `stamp_planned_context_window` writes the plan into the loader’s copy of the context at BOTH live call sites (`core/agent_runtime/chat_surface.py`, `core/agent_runtime/turn_reasoning.py`); a window the turn already carries wins, and a planner failure returns the context unstamped — the plan is an optimization, never a gate. 23 tests in `tests/test_context_budget_scales_to_model_window.py`: the arithmetic + never-shrink property, the shared window-key contract, the loader end-to-end (`model_context_window: 200000` → report budget 50000), the planner (min, vision/embed exclusion, unknown-window refusal), and both call-site wirings through the REAL turn code. **Sabotage, four rounds, each naming its own tests:** scaler reverted to a no-op → 4 fail incl. the loader end-to-end; the stamp removed → 3 fail incl. both wirings; both call sites reverted to the raw context → exactly the two wiring tests fail; planner min→max → exactly the smallest-candidate test fails. **Boundary: PARTIAL, not VERIFIED.** Everything is executed in-process (the real loader against the real DB; the real turn code with the loader mocked at the seam); no live daemon turn has been driven on this code. Goes VERIFIED after a daemon restart onto the fix and a live turn whose prompt assembly report shows the scaled budgets. |
| `workspace.read_file` / `machine.read_file` | VERIFIED | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | The same defect as the listing bug, on the CONTENT axis. `read_text().splitlines()` pulls the whole file in before `content[start:start+max_lines]` discards the rest, so the 160-default/400-ceiling saved no I/O — it only hid the size. The result carried no `total_lines` and no `truncated`, so a 160-line file and a 4,620-line file produced **byte-identical envelopes** and a model that had seen 3.5% of a module could not tell; that is how a reader concludes "this file has no error handling" about code it was never shown. 43% of this repo's Python files exceed 160 lines and 15% exceed the 400 ceiling a caller was even allowed to request. Fixed on both surfaces: default 2000 / ceiling 50000, `total_lines` and `truncated` emitted in `details` AND in the observation, `status: truncated` when short, and the body carries "…lines N-M not shown (M lines in total). This is a fragment; it cannot support any conclusion about what the file does NOT contain. Re-read from line N to continue." — including on `verbatim` reads, which is what the audit uses. Both `output_schema`s now DECLARE `total_lines`/`truncated`, so a reader has a field to look for. **Driven:** the 201-line Apache codec at `max_lines=160` returns `status truncated, total_lines 201`; at the new default it returns `executed, truncated False`. **Sabotage:** forcing `truncated = False` fails `test_a_partial_file_read_says_so_and_names_the_total` and `test_a_verbatim_read_still_discloses_truncation`, and nothing else. Suite 8676 passed. |
| Truncation disclosure reaches the model (observation layer) | UNVERIFIED | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | The disclosure written into `_list_files`' body was being deleted one layer up: `_tool_observation` omitted `total`/`dropped`, and `response_policy_tool_history` rendered `Listed 200 item(s)` from the PAGE count with a bare "Results were truncated." So the sentence written specifically to prevent the false-absence conclusion never arrived, and the number the model saw was 200 — never 682. Both now pass `total`/`dropped` through, and the preview renders `Listed 200 of 682 item(s) … TRUNCATED — 482 item(s) not shown. This is an alphabetical prefix, not the whole set; it cannot support any conclusion that something is ABSENT.` The tool-history sample stays bounded at 60 (it rides on every later turn, so it is a recurring cost) — the defect was never the sample size, it was the missing total. **Driven** against the real 682-file workspace through the actual observation payload. |

| Factual Q&A | VERIFIED | VERIFIED | "capital of Australia" → **Canberra** (clean prose, `qwen3:14b`) |
| No reasoning-leak on the shipped model | VERIFIED (qwen2.5) | VERIFIED (qwen3) | the Canberra answer from a **thinking** model was clean prose — no `Okay, the user` / `<think>` / `Hmm,` markers. Windows could NOT test this (no qwen3 there); Apple can, and it is clean. |
| Casual turn routes to a text model, not a vision model | VERIFIED | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane (`7518423`)** | The old multi-model build routed casual turns to `moondream` (a vision model) → garbage/`????`. `is_text_generation_ollama_model` now excludes `moondream`/`llava`/`bakllava`/`minicpm-v`/`-vl`/`-vision` and the embedders, so on HEAD the live daemon's `/v1/models` lists **only** the qwen3 text lanes and "can you make me a coffee?" routes to **`qwen3:8b`** with a clean answer in **24.9 s**. **Shared Python — KAS re-verifies on Windows.** |
| Local thinking OFF by default, switchable on | UNVERIFIED (no qwen3 there) | VERIFIED (`72bcc6e`) | qwen3 is a thinking model that over-thinks trivial turns and times them out; the adapter now reads the deep-reasoning switch at call time and sends `think:false` by default (the coffee turn answered in 24.9 s with no `<think>`/`Okay, the user` leak), `think:true` only when the user enables deep reasoning. **Shared Python — KAS re-verifies on Windows.** |
| Word-phrased arithmetic | BROKEN (3/3) | UNVERIFIED | one probe returned an empty body (curl timeout); not a clean repro. The Windows cause (`response.py:318` sanitizer catch-all) is shared code, so it likely affects Apple — re-drive with a longer timeout. |

Embeds a relocatable **CPython 3.11.15** (python-build-standalone via `uv`) + the lean runtime deps (`pydantic cryptography requests pynacl keyring psutil pyyaml starlette uvicorn solders pywebview`; **no torch/transformers**) + VOOL source + bundled **ollama 74 MB**; `Info.plist` carries `CFBundleDisplayName VOOL`, `CFBundleExecutable VOOL` (frozen), and the `vool://` scheme. **Boot-verified:** ran the bundled daemon from the embedded python (`PYTHONPATH=<bundle>/app`, isolated `VOOL_HOME`, real Keychain reachable) on `:11466` → `/chat` 200 with `<title>VOOL` + the new Activity-panel JS, `/api/runtime/version` served `0.4.4-closed-test`; `apps.vool_api_server` imports clean (no missing excluded-dep); the live `:11435` daemon left untouched. Built on the key machine under the user's explicit "safe installs" permission (`uv` installs prebuilt wheels of already-vetted deps — no npm-style postinstall). `ALLOW_TCC_ROOT=1` (repo on `~/Desktop`; harmless — the app embeds its own source, but move the `.app` to `/Applications` before a Finder double-click). |
| Signed / notarized `.dmg` for distribution | UNVERIFIED | Both `.dmg`s (thin-launcher and the new self-contained one) are **unsigned**. Distribution to other Macs needs a **Developer ID Application** certificate + `notarytool`; the available identities are *Apple Development* + *Apple Distribution* (`security find-identity`), neither of which is Developer ID. Unsigned, the self-contained `.dmg` runs on this Mac and others via right-click → Open. |
| Wrapper launcher no longer bakes the build path/username | PARTIAL | Shipped-payload audit (`docs/AUDIT_2026-07-23_installer_secret_scan.md`) found the wrapper launcher baked `PROJECT_ROOT="/Users/<builduser>/…"` — build-username leak + non-relocatable. Fixed in `build_macos_app.sh`: the emitted launcher resolves `PROJECT_ROOT` at run time (self-relative when the `.app` sits in the repo's `dist/`, else a `${HOME}`-relative template re-expanded on the user's machine — no absolute build path baked), and a wrapper-mode `--dmg` now prints a not-distributable warning. Verified: `bash -n` on script + generated launcher; runtime simulated both ways (self-resolve → repo root; moved → `${HOME}/…` fallback); no `builduser` string in the output. **Boundary:** source only — the current `dist/VOOL.dmg` still carries the old baked path; reaches users only after a self-contained `.dmg` rebuild (sandbox, needs `uv`). |
| Launcher auto-starts the local LLM (Ollama) | VERIFIED | The daemon's default local backend is Ollama on `:11434`, but `Start_VOOL.sh` only ever ensured the optional llama.cpp lane — so if Ollama was down, every local turn failed with "couldn't get a live model response". Added `ensure_ollama_server` to the emitted launcher (generator `installer/install_vool.sh` + deployed copy), called in both the supervisor loop and the direct exec path. Live harness: already-running guard returns 0 with **no** second spawn (procs before=1/after=1); down-path spawned `ollama serve` on an unused alt port and it came **READY listing 8 models**; honors `OLLAMA_HOST`; non-fatal when Ollama is absent (cloud/BYOK still boots). Contract tests green (27 passed). **Apple-lane `.sh`; KAS mirrors in the `.bat`/`.ps1` launcher.** |

## Runtime reliability — audit Tier 1 remediation (2026-07-24, `aa95148..89dce8f`)

A runtime-orchestration audit found the runtime delegating deterministic responsibilities (identity, tool dispatch, synthesis validation, retry, memory admission) to whichever free model was selected. Five parallel readers verified each finding against live code (the audit was directionally right; two findings smaller than stated, two worse), then the fixes landed as **bounded deterministic guards — the first bricks of the proposed `TurnExecutor`, not a rewrite.** Shared runtime — KAS rebases. Driven **live on the daemon (`127.0.0.1:11435` @ `89dce8f`)**, not test-suite inference.

| Capability | Apple | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Foreign tool-call syntax never reaches the user (P0) | VERIFIED | `aa95148`: new `core/model_output_guard.py` wired into the universal output choke point `model_output_contracts.validate_contract` (plain_text). A leaked `<\|tool_call>call:google_search(...)` is scrubbed + trust-penalised; a pure-foreign answer fails the contract; prose about a tool and ordinary code (`print("hi")`, a bare `<function>`) survive. 12 tests. **Live:** normal turn → clean `Paris.` (qwen3:14b), no markers. |
| Terminal synthesis rejects non-answers (P0) | VERIFIED | `f7472b4`: `research_tool_loop_facade` calls the shared guard (`claims_pending_tool`/`is_ungrounded`/`foreign_markers`) on the final synthesis; an echo / leaked-syntax / still-asks-for-a-tool / observation-ignoring / empty answer → the already-wired grounded fallback (`_tool_loop_final_message(None, …)`), event `stale_result_rejected:<reason>`. **Integration test** drives a real `VoolAgent` whose synthesis returns `<\|tool_call>call:google_search(...)`: the leak never reaches the user, the answer is grounded, the rejection is recorded. |
| `user_address` is the authoritative identity fact (P0/P1) | VERIFIED | `0c138d4`: the deterministic recall (`runtime._private_memory_values`) prefers `user_address` for the name (harvested block is only a fallback); the fact-admission gate (`fact_extractor._fact_grounded`) rejects any `user_profile` Name contradicting a non-empty `user_address`. 7 tests. **Live:** `what is my name?` → `Your name is LOOP.` (authoritative, no model guess); broad recall carries no residual `Rick`. Boundary: capture-disabled group surfaces still answer via the model with the `user_address` bootstrap anchor. |
| "try again" retries the preceding failed task (P1) | VERIFIED | `1bac457`: `runtime_continuity.latest_failed_checkpoint` + `checkpoints.prepare_runtime_checkpoint` re-dispatch the last FAILED task's immutable `request_text` envelope (new `retried` state) instead of the static Discord/Telegram string; no failed task → asks what to run. Optional callback (default `None` → old behaviour). 4 tests incl. real-DB. **Live:** `try again` (fresh) → `There's nothing in this chat to retry yet…` — no Discord/Telegram. |
| Project binding fails closed (P1) | VERIFIED | `89dce8f`: `vool_chat_page` `newChatInProject` + `assignSession` gate on the bind POST's `r.ok` — on failure they reflect the true (unbound) state and warn `it is NOT isolated`, so a swallowed bind (incl. a deleted project's 404) no longer leaves a false "bound" chip that silently leaks the project's global memory. Page-JS only. `node --check` clean; 3 tests. **Live:** `GET /chat` 200 carries the fail-closed JS. |

**Not done here (Tier 2 — routing core, KAS-coordinated, see `STATUS_BOARD.md` N11):** the free-cloud lane is unreachable end-to-end today (`set_free_cloud_enabled` orphaned + `CloudTaskRequirements` test-only), the two cloud paths disagree on `:free` freeness, and free-model selection scores only family+context. These gate real spend on both lanes — reconcile by design with KAS, do not land unilaterally.

Post-audit fixes (Codex-amended plan). Shared runtime — KAS rebases. Each per-fix regression green; the live end-to-end drives land in the step-8 smoke.

| Capability | Apple | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Project create → auto-create + focus a bound chat | VERIFIED (tests) | `131efca`: `createProjectFlow` reuses the fail-closed `newChatInProject`. node --check + presence test. Live drive pending. |
| Scope authority: "this/current/local folder" = bound root | VERIFIED (tests) | `5f0bab3`: demonstratives stand the machine named-folder lanes down → the project-aware folder-overview reads the bound `workspace_root`; unbound fails honestly. Gate #1 (bound `web0` never → VOOL) + `Local`-folder / `../` / symlink / cross-project confinement (`8327c21`). |
| Workspace-audit EXECUTOR (deterministic read-only) | VERIFIED (tests) | `b76d8c9`: `list_tree → manifests → git → lint`, real outputs → grounded findings + a runtime event per step; new `workspace_audit` class. Acceptance: several real tool steps, not listing+prose. |
| Continuation honesty (no fabricated "in progress") | VERIFIED (tests) | `cf01639`: `enforce_final_action_honesty` replaces a short fabricated in-progress claim when no tool ran this turn. |
| Stop = TRUE turn cancel (turn_id-keyed) | VERIFIED (tests) | `6f5c7cc`: per-turn Event `(session_id,turn_id)` → router `_cancel_check` → `model_call_cancelled`; idempotent `POST /api/chat/cancel`; A≠B; signal-reaches-a-running-consumer. Live real-Ollama drive → step-8 smoke. |
| OpenRouter categories 2 (not 4) | VERIFIED (tests) | `a23ae99`: default `personal-agent,programming-app` + hard cap. 64 attribution tests green. |
| Activity panel = ledger-backed, turn-scoped | VERIFIED — data layer LIVE on the daemon (`82bc0e9`); render by node-check + tests | **Live (daemon `82bc0e9`):** drove a real turn (`turn_id=verify-activity-…`) → `/api/runtime/events` returned 3 events (`task_received`, `model_lane_proof`, `task_completed`), **all** tagged `client_turn_id == turn_id`, zero untagged, zero cross-turn leak — the exact key the panel scopes on. The served `/chat` carries `pollLedger`/`loadRecoveredLedger`/`renderLedgerRows`/"No tool ran". Server: `emit_runtime_event` tags every event with `client_turn_id` from `source_context['cancel_turn_id']` (additive, never overrides a caller detail). Client (`vool_chat_page.py`, page-served, NO routing-core change): the Activity tab polls `GET /api/runtime/events`, keeps only rows tagged with `run.turnId` (a queued/overlapping turn never bleeds in), maps the real event vocabulary (`scope_resolved`/`task_classified`/`model_lane_*`/`tool_*`/`workflow_planner_*`/`task_*`; `model_output_chunk` skipped) to Scope/Classified/Model/Tool/Verification rows, prints "No tool ran — answered directly" for a tool-less turn, and rebuilds the last turn after a page refresh (never blank while the card says working). Tool rows also carry a **redacted arg summary** (`path=README.md`, `query="…"`) via `core/tool_arg_redaction.py` — display-only, secret-safe (masks `api_key`/`password`/`token`/`private_key`/`mnemonic`, summarizes bulky values, final `redact_secrets` pass), wired into `research_tool_loop_facade` + `workspace_audit`. `tests/test_chat_page_activity_ledger.py` + `tests/test_tool_arg_redaction.py` (8) + a `client_turn_id` server-tag case; `node --check` clean; 164-test regression green. **LIVE-VERIFIED @ `74d5614`:** a bound-project audit's `workspace.read_file` event carried `tool_args=path=README.md, max_lines=60` (redacted); no-arg tools show nothing. |

| Path-escape failures surface clearly (containment intact) | VERIFIED (tests) | `resolve_workspace_path` now names the bound project and points at the fix instead of a cryptic "Path escapes the active workspace." dead-end; the escape is still REFUSED (prompt-injection gauntlet green, load-bearing substring preserved). Deliberately NO silent auto-re-root — that would change a security boundary; honest-fail + model retry is the confinement design. `test_scope_authority` surfacing test; gauntlet + 52-test regression green. |

| Prompt capture (`VOOL_DEBUG_PROMPT=1`) — see what the model was actually sent | **VERIFIED (live)** | `core/prompt_debug.py` + both payload builders in `adapters/openai_compatible_adapter.py`. Off by default (one env read); on, it appends one JSON record per model call to `$VOOL_HOME/logs/prompt_debug.jsonl` — roles, per-message sizes, and the full outbound payload run through `redact_secrets` first, clipped at 24k/message, file `0600`, dir `0700`, and it swallows every exception so a capture failure can never break a turn. `find_in_last_prompt(needle)` answers "which message told it that?" **Drove it live** on the daemon: one `whats your name?` turn produced a record showing `qwen3:8b | 3 messages` with VOOL×4 in the system message and VOOL×5 in the recall message — which is how the three injectors below were found after four rounds of reading the assembly code had failed to. |
| The assistant says its own name (VOOL, not the legacy one) | **VERIFIED (live)** | The name was reported fixed three times and kept coming back, because the persona row, display name, self-knowledge doc and system prompt were all already correct — the wrong name arrived through **recall**, and only prompt capture showed it. Three injectors, all fixed at the READ point in `core/memory/entries.py` so every consumer is covered and the operator's stored data is never rewritten: `search_relevant_memory` (was emitting `Relevant durable memory: **My name**: VOOL`), `search_session_summaries` (was quoting the assistant's own prior reply back as `Last assistant outcome: I'm VOOL` — a self-reinforcing loop), and `summarize_memory`. `core/memory/learning.py` applies the same mapping when it writes a new summary and on the `Continuity:` line. `core/bootstrap_context.py` reads the display name through `get_agent_display_name()` instead of the raw identity row — still gated on the row carrying a name, because emitting a `must_keep` identity item for an operator who never set one evicts grounding facts from the evidence budget (that regression was caught by `test_prompt_assembly_profiles`, not by me). The mapping is **path-safe and code-span-safe**: `~/Desktop/vool-local-product`, `vool_runtime/…`, `vool.api`, `apps.vool_api_server` and backticked CLI literals like `` `vool resolve <name>.null` `` are left exactly as stored — rewriting a path or a pasteable command is a worse failure than the wrong name. A name the operator deliberately sets is never overwritten. **Live on the daemon, 6/6:** `whats your name?` → "I'm VOOL."; `who are you?` / `introduce yourself` → "I'm VOOL, your local-first assistant…"; `hey whats up` → smalltalk, no tool; `what date is today?` → "Today is Tuesday, 2026-07-28." (this one previously answered "I don't have a real-time clock"); `what can you do?` → capability list. Post-fix capture shows **0** legacy-name tokens in the recall message. `tests/test_memory_legacy_name_recall.py` (13 cases) asserts on returned text rather than on a file having changed — the earlier version of this fix passed a test that only proved the prompt source was edited while the running product still said the old name — and the mapping was sabotaged to confirm 7 of them fail without it. Full suite **6007 passed**. |

| A failed shell command is not reported as a successful tool call | **VERIFIED (live + tests)** | `sandbox.run_command` returned `ok=True, status="executed"` for every exit code. `returncode` and `success` were correct in `details` the whole time — only the two flags anything downstream reads were wrong — so `cat missing.txt` came back as `mode="tool_executed"` with a signed receipt saying the call succeeded, and nothing between the sandbox and the ledger could tell a working command from a broken one. Now `ok=False, status="command_failed"` on a nonzero exit, with the command artifact carrying the same status; `response_text` still gives the model the exit code and both streams so it can react. Drove the real sandbox: `cat missing.txt` / `grep <no-match>` / `ls <missing dir>` → `ok=False, command_failed, rc=1`; `cat present.txt` → `ok=True, executed, rc=0`; end to end through `execute_tool_intent`, the failing call now reaches the turn as `mode="tool_failed"` (→ `tool.failed`, repairing stage). `tests/test_sandbox_command_failure_is_not_success.py` (7 cases, real sandbox not a mock); sabotaged the check to confirm 5 fail without it. |
| The user is never shown the prompt's own scaffolding as an answer | **VERIFIED (live + tests)** | Asked "run the command: ls -la", the product replied with the literal words **final grounded reply** — the example message value from the tool-catalogue instruction in `prompt_normalizer.py`, copied verbatim by a small local model. The example is now an angle-bracket slot that cannot pass for prose, and `tool_intent_direct_message` refuses to hand a placeholder back (returning `None` routes into the path an empty message already took, so the caller keeps working the turn). Matched on the **whole** normalised message, so a real answer containing "the final answer is 42" survives. `tests/test_direct_message_placeholder_guard.py` (17 cases). Re-drove both leaking requests live: template gone. |
| Tool SELECTION runs on a model that can actually select a tool | **VERIFIED (live + tests)** | `output_mode="tool_intent"` was scored as cheap utility work and landed on `qwen3:0.6b`. Measured, not assumed — replayed one captured tool_intent prompt ("run the command: ls -la", ~17.5k chars, 57 tools) at two sizes: `qwen3:8b` → `{"intent":"sandbox.run_command","arguments":{"command":"ls -la"}}`; `qwen3:0.6b` → `respond.direct` carrying invented prose about a staging database. Sub-4B models are now removed from the tool-selection candidate set, and it **never empties** — on a machine whose only model is small, a small model attempting the call beats no brain. Cheap `classification`/`format`/`extract`/`tag` keep the tiny lane untouched. **A score penalty was not enough:** `provider_routing._rank_provider_candidates_internal` re-scores by ordinal position (`total - index`) and then adds `_role_bonus`, which discards the magnitude of the lane-fit penalty and put the drone-tagged 0.6b straight back on top — confirmed live on the daemon before the candidate-set fix landed. Live after: the same request is served by `qwen3:8b`. |
| Command output reaches the step that has to report it | **VERIFIED (live + tests)** | Terminal output was stripped from the observation wholesale for `sandbox.run_command`/`run_tests`/`run_lint`/`run_formatter` — a sound guard against a 50k-line log, applied absolutely. Live: `ls -la` ran, exited 0, produced 36 lines, and the model was handed only `stdout 36 line(s); full output saved in artifact(s): command_output`, so it answered "I'm ready to help. Let me check the current directory for you." — the listing existed and it never saw it. Now a bounded head/tail excerpt (40 lines / 2800 chars stdout, 16 / 700 stderr) is inlined with an explicit omission marker naming what was left out; the artifact still holds the full text, and a 5000-line log compresses to ~360 chars with both ends intact. Char budget is derived from the line budget (`40 * 70`) after a 1600-char cap clipped a 36-line listing the line cap had allowed. Live after: the captured prompt for that turn carries the real `drwxr-xr-x …` rows, and replaying it returns a written-out directory summary. |

**Still open on this path (reproduced live, unseen phrasings):** `machine.list_directory` rejects the model's `directory` argument — `unsupported argument(s): directory. Allowed arguments: directories_only, limit, path` — and that raw contract error is delivered to the user as the final answer instead of the loop retrying under the right name; and "what does `du -sh .` report here?" is answered by describing the command rather than running it. Also observed while testing: a repeated identical request returns a byte-identical reply from a cached decision, so **verify with unseen phrasing** or a fix looks like it did nothing.

**Deferred (documented, next batch):** the structured pending-plan envelope so "yes proceed" resumes the exact plan; the `human_input_adapter` subject-priority fix; extending the cancel Event into every tool/verification sub-step. **KAS release-blocking:** the routing/tool path (`STATUS_BOARD.md` N12). *(Activity-panel rebuild + redacted tool-args + path-escape surfacing — now done, see the rows above.)*

## Tooling lane — 2026-07-28

Driven on this box against live Ollama (`qwen3:8b`, `qwen3:0.6b`) and live OpenRouter. Every row
below was measured by running the product or the adapter, never inferred from a green suite.

| Capability | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Local model emits native tool calls | **VERIFIED** | `qwen3:8b` 5/5 on unseen phrasings, all via `message.tool_calls`. `qwen3:0.6b` 2/5 — its 3 failures are one hallucinated answer, one wrongful refusal, one invented path. |
| Cloud dialect chosen from advertised capability | **VERIFIED** | 5/5 dispatch each on `openai/gpt-5-nano`, `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5`, `kimi-k2.5` (Kimi), `nvidia/nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b:free`, through `_invoke_openai_compatible`. 13 of 14 free OpenRouter models advertise `tools` in `supported_parameters`. Spend $0.009. |
| Cloud tool turns get room to reason | **VERIFIED** | `tool_intent` allots 700 output tokens and the cloud lane had no thinking reserve; a reasoning model hit `finish_reason: length` mid-thought. Same model + prompts at 512 → no call, at 3000 → `tool_calls`. nemotron went 3/5 → 5/5. |
| One request answered however it is phrased | **VERIFIED** | 8/8 phrasings grounded on a fresh folder, including three that carry NO typed path ("whats in my harbor folder on the desktop", "show me the files in…", "peek inside… and tell me whats there"). The no-path class was the planner matching the word 'desktop' inside the phrase and listing the whole Desktop; it now resolves the named child on a unique match, and degrades to today's root listing on zero or ambiguous matches. Was 1/8 at session start. |
| `format` and `tools` never sent together to Ollama | **VERIFIED** | Both in one body → zero `tool_calls`, model degenerates 222s to `done_reason: length`, turn dies on the 60s provider budget. Identical body minus `format` → clean call in 35s. |
| `machine.find_folder` given a path | **VERIFIED** | Was a depth-6 walk of every drive answering "not found" for a folder that existed (`~` unexpanded). Now resolves in 0.00s vs 3.19s; a bare name still searches. |
| Answer-to-tool binding | **PARTIAL** | Computes and logs on every returned answer; catches the measured fabrication, invented filenames and untouched targets, passes correct answers. Enforcement is OFF — the false-positive rate has not been measured on real traffic. |
| Plugin tools (the extension surface) | **PARTIAL** | A manifest's declared tools load, register, reach the model catalog as native schemas, and execute sandboxed with an env allowlist; `qwen3:8b` chose a plugin tool on 2/2 unseen phrasings. OFF by default: no install-consent flow exists, so enabling it would register tools from any manifest on disk without approval. |
| Plugin skills | **VERIFIED** | The 2 installed plugins' 3 skills parse with real YAML and inject 3,665 chars of author-written instructions that were previously parsed and discarded. |
| 13 orphan tool intents governed | **BROKEN** | Still advertised to every model with no contract, no side-effect class, no approval requirement. Includes `operator.cleanup_temp_files`, `operator.move_path`, `operator.schedule_calendar_event`, which delete/move/write on this machine. Adopting them broke the capability ledger and the operator dispatch gate; reverted rather than forced. |
| A refused turn is not fossilised | **VERIFIED** | A decision is recorded at model-call time, when validation only asks whether it is well-formed — so a well-formed WRONG tool choice was stored `valid` at trust 1.0 and replayed. A turn that refuses now invalidates the decision behind it; the failure stays in the audit trail. |
| A metadata question gets a metadata answer | **VERIFIED** | `workspace.identity` reports the active workspace path, project name and git status in one line. Four phrasings including "do you see what folder our workspace is set on?" answer correctly; search requests ("find where X is wired in this workspace") and listing requests still route to their own tools. |
| A completion claim is receipt-backed | **PARTIAL** | 'I audited X' is refused unless a record exists whose resolved target IS X, and the refusal names what actually ran. Proven by unit tests and a replay of the owner's verbatim session against their real non-git workspace; NOT yet driven through a live chat turn on the built app. |
| An audit audits the file that was named | **PARTIAL** | Target parsed from the request, `.html`/`.css` added to the source set, and the file's verbatim source carried into the model's evidence. Same verification boundary as above. |
| macOS `.app` / `.dmg` | **VERIFIED** | First bundle ever built 2026-07-28: `VOOL.app`, `ai.vool.desktop`, 2.1M, build id `0.4.4-closed-test+macos+<sha>`. Installed at `~/Applications/VOOL.app`, launches, daemon healthy on :11435. Unsigned (needs a Developer ID) and not self-contained — it runs against the `~/Applications/vool` checkout, which is deliberate for a closed team. |
| One under-described context item no longer fails a turn on every provider | **VERIFIED** | The audit turn on the owner's real project died on `ollama-local:qwen3:8b`, `ollama-local:qwen3:14b` and the OpenRouter free lane in ~0.1s each, before any network call, and the user saw only "I couldn't get a live model response in this run". Persisted ledger, all three lanes: `reason: "selected context source 6 is missing content_hash"`. `_validate_selected_sources` required six provenance fields per selected context item and raised on the first gap; selected sources are a property of the **request**, so one gap failed every ranked lane identically. **Not** a token budget — the same rows record `available_prompt_tokens: 22136` with nothing dropped, and my earlier `6367 + 2288 > 8192` claim was wrong on both the arithmetic and the cause (`qwen3:8b` declares 24576). 8 of 11 `ContextItem` producers never stamped `content_hash`; the 3 reachable on an ordinary chat turn (persistent memory, user heuristics, prior-session summaries) made it look intermittent — a greeting retrieves nothing and survives. Producers now hash the content they include; the seal **marks** an under-described item `unverified-<field>-<seed>` and records it rather than raising. Dropping the item would be worse: it is already in the prompt, so a manifest omitting it would misdescribe what the provider received. 9 tests; 6 sabotages (each producer's hash removed, hash computed from constant text, seal raising again, seal marking a complete item) each caught. Suite 6671. |
| Lane-matrix harness — outcomes, not one phrasing | **PARTIAL** | `tests/live/lane_matrix.py` scores intent × phrasing × workspace × lane on real outcomes (was the named file opened, did the expected tool run, is the answer non-empty), with ≥8 phrasings per intent including the owner's verbatim typos, imperatives and no-apostrophe forms. Routing cells run Auto on purpose: pinning a model sets `requested_model`, trips `explicit_model_owns_semantic_turn`, and disables every front-door fast path — an early drive pinned `qwen3:8b` and read the resulting silence as a product failure when it was the harness. **First measured baseline: workspace-identity 0/9, greeting 4/4.** Live-lane cells and the cross-workspace dimension are built but not yet driven, so PARTIAL. |
| A launcher serves the code it was built from | **VERIFIED** | Corrects the row above, which claimed "`git pull` updates the app with no rebuild". The pull updated the *files*; the running daemon did not change. `is_healthy()` asked only whether **something** answered on 11435, so a launch attached to whatever was alive. Measured 2026-07-28: the `.app` reported `…+51afc4c2850c` while `/healthz` reported `commit a9744211d166` — a daemon up since 01:19 serving the previous day's code, so every landed fix was absent from the user's seat. Both launchers now compare the running stamp against their own commit (wrapper: `git rev-parse` on the checkout, live, so a pull alone still suffices; self-contained: the commit in its `Info.plist`) and stop a mismatch via `Stop_VOOL.sh` / `installer.vool_stop` — never `pkill`. Unknown on either side leaves the daemon alone. **Live:** `serving=a9744211d166` vs `installed=51afc4c2850c` → restart; after a real restart both read `51afc4c2850c`. 29 tests execute the shipped `serving_commit()` and the guard block itself (a marker file stands in for the daemon, a real stop script frees it); 5 sabotages — anchorless regex, dropped `-n` operands, guard behind `if false`, no `head -1`, no `nogit` escape — each caught. |
| A sentence about a dangerous thing is not a dangerous command | **VERIFIED** | `core/task_router.py` matched 11 risk markers with a bare `marker in text` containment test and 6 fired on ordinary English. Measured against the real classifier: `website displays most of data in PDF format so agent needs to read it`, `can you export the report in CSV format for me`, `i am advising a startup on this`, `look up the registry office address`, `we exfiltrate nothing, that is the point` and `sudo is not installed on this box` were all `risky_system_action`. Not cosmetic — `destructive_command` is in the hard_block set (`runtime_gate_policy.py`), so the turn is refused outright with `allowed_actions=[]`, decomposition skipped, confidence clamped to 0.30, and the class drops out of the plain-text chat set. Markers now split by shape: a COMMAND token (`rm`, `format`, `sudo`, `crontab`, `diskutil`, `mkfs`, `launchctl`, `systemctl`, `schtasks`, `reg add/delete`) counts only at a command position — start of input or line, or after a shell separator — and an ALWAYS token (`system32`, `powershell -enc`, `exfiltrat*`) counts anywhere but on word boundaries, so `cron` stops matching inside `cronbach`. Both then require the token be INVOKED, not discussed: a following copula, negation or modal excludes it. The single-case `_looks_like_readonly_format_check_request` exemption is deleted — it existed only because matching was substring-based. Testing found two bugs reading did not: anchoring `exfiltrate` at both ends missed `exfiltrated`/`exfiltrating` (a false NEGATIVE introduced while fixing false positives), and `del `/`rm ` were declared with a trailing space that `.strip()` removed while the boundary was decided from the *unstripped* token — so both matched bare and `delta between the two branches` was a destructive command. 37 tests (13 prose sentences, 7 command-tokens-as-word-prefixes, 12 real commands that must still be caught); 5 sabotages — command position removed, talked-about guard removed, boundaries dropped on always-tokens and on command-tokens, boundary from the unstripped token — each caught. Suite 6968 passed, 0 failed. |
| `skill.create` hands back a handle the next step accepts | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | QA drive 2026-07-30, tools driven directly through `execute_runtime_tool`. Run in the order `create` itself prescribes, the three-step flow did not work: `skill.create {"name": "qa-echo"}` returned `ok`, slug `qa-echo`, and the sign-off "It is not active yet - validate it, then install it."; `skill.validate {"path": "qa-echo"}` returned **not_found, "no SKILL.md at qa-echo"**; and `skill.install {"path": "qa-echo"}` then returned **refused, "the skill does not validate, so installing it would add a capability that cannot work"** — a false statement about a skill that validates perfectly when given its full path. Every step behaved as written and the sequence still failed: `validate` resolved the name against the process working directory like any relative path, so the one identifier `create` returns was the one identifier the next step could not take. The steps are deliberately separate calls, which is exactly why the handle passed between them has to survive the gap. New `resolve_skill_markdown` accepts a file, a directory, or a NAME, searching staging before the installed tree (a redraft is the copy being validated). `install` also distinguishes not_found from refused, because a skill that is not there has not failed validation and saying so sends the reader to fix contents that were never written. **Verified end to end:** the create → validate → install sequence now runs on the slug alone and `load_skills` loads the result; `"QA Echo"` and `"qa echo"` resolve to the same skill. 13 tests; **5 sabotages, two of which initially escaped and exposed dead code** — the old probe at `plugins/<slug>/SKILL.md` never matched the real layout, so reordering it changed nothing; the search order is now a single named list and both order tests bite. **Boundary:** driven through the tool executor, not through chat — the chat phrasings that reach these tools were not re-driven (the daemon was restarted by another lane mid-run). |

Now: markers run over `prose_only()`, and each marker is bounded on the edges where a boundary means something. `.null` deliberately keeps NO leading boundary (a name is written `alice.null`; a first attempt at this fix demanded a non-word character before the dot and lost every real question), and the buy/register stems stay open at the end so "registering", "buying" and "resolved" still count — a boundary at both ends dropped "registering" and a gauntlet test caught it. 32 tests, and the restraint and the real questions are pinned in the same file. **5 sabotages, and two of them initially escaped and are the reason two tests exist:** removing `prose_only` left every case green because the trailing boundary was covering for it (now pinned by `/Users/me/.null/config.py`, where the marker is spelled exactly with non-word characters on both sides), and removing the trailing boundary left every case green because `prose_only` was covering for it (now pinned by `what is .vool_runtime for, and can i get rid of it?`, which has no path at all). |

Reproduced offline: a file list of `["<ws>/f1/palindrome.py", "<ws>/f1/test_palindrome.py", "<ws>/f1/README.md"]` confined to `[None, None, None]`. An absolute path INSIDE the workspace is now rebased through the same `workspace_relative_path` helper the routing fix uses; outside it is still refused, because confinement is the point. Verified in every direction: `<ws>/f1/x.py`, `f1/x.py` and `x.py` all land on `f1/x.py`, while `/etc/passwd`, `/tmp/elsewhere/x.py`, `/Users/other/x.py`, `C:/win/x.py` and `../escape.py` all stay `None` — and so does an absolute path when NO workspace is known, since it has nothing to be relative to. 2 sabotages: discarding absolutes again (3 tests bit) and rebasing everything with a bare `lstrip("/")` (3 confinement tests bit). |
| A skill request is not an app build | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | The live verification of the row below, which is why live verification is not optional. With the new skill lane in place, `new skill: qa-wordcount. counts words in a block of text. go ahead and create it.` drafted a real `SKILL.md` in **0 s** — and `create a skill called qa-echo2 ...` and `make a skill called qa-upper ...` BOTH fell through to a cloud model that produced nothing, reproducibly. The detector matched all three offline, so this was never the detector. `skill` is a **PROJECT noun** in `build_request_intent`, so the verb forms satisfy `looks_like_agentic_build_request`, and that gate returns `{"result": None}` — which means "no fast path claimed this turn" — handing a skill-authoring request to the app builder. Only the heading form, which carries no imperative verb, slipped past it and reached the lane. The lane now sits ABOVE that gate: authoring a skill is not building an app, however much the sentence looks like one. Sabotage: moving the lane back below the gate fails both verb-form cases and leaves the heading form green — which is exactly the shape the live drive showed. |
| Asking for a skill creates one | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | The three skill tools already WORKED — driven directly through `execute_runtime_tool` they draft, validate and install a `SKILL.md` the real loader loads. Nothing routed chat to them. Measured 2026-07-30: `plan_tool_workflow` returns `handled=False` for every create and validate phrasing tried, and no fast path existed anywhere, so the only route in was a small local model choosing to emit `skill.create` as tool JSON — the exact failure the fast paths exist to remove. A blind drive of eight skill phrasings created **zero** skills and hung three times at 300 s. One phrasing was not merely uncovered but WRONG: `new skill: qa-wordcount. counts words in a block of text. go ahead and create it.` routed **deterministically to `hive.create_topic`**, posting a research topic titled with the request. New `core/agent_runtime/fast_paths_skill.py`, wired into the front door beside the PDF lane and ahead of the hive lane. It reaches `create`/`validate`/`install`/list, and it will not INSTALL on its own — `create` drafts into staging and names the next step, because authoring is not authorisation to change behaviour (the line `core/skill_tools.py` already draws). It also never invents a NAME: a nameless request is asked about rather than guessed, since a drafted file the operator cannot refer to is worse than a question. Listing is decided BEFORE the shared deliberation gate — that gate reads "what skills do i have" as discussion, which is right for a gate whose job is to stop writes and wrong for a read. 33 tests over 24 phrasings (8 create, 5 other verbs, 11 that must author nothing); **4 sabotages each named a failing test**, including one on the front-door wiring — and that test is a real `_handle_turn_frontdoor` drive asserting the staged file exists on disk, after a first version that compared source-character offsets stayed green when the call was disabled. |
| "Ran 0 tests ... OK" is not a pass | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | Found in the FRESH-PHRASING retest, which is the only reason it was found: `please write <ws>/g2/titlecase.py that title-cases a sentence` built the module and a README in the right folder and answered **"Tests passed. (Ran 0 tests in 0.000s | OK)"**. Nothing was verified, and the sentence saying otherwise is the one thing this lane must never produce. Two causes, both fixed. (1) `_tests_passed_from_output` read the `OK` that `python -m unittest discover` prints for an EMPTY suite as a pass; a run is now a pass only if `Ran N tests` has N > 0, and a runner that prints no count line at all (`npm test`) is still judged on its verdict rather than failed closed. (2) The deeper one: `_pick_test_command` was given the PLANNED file list, so a build that listed `test_titlecase.py` and then failed to generate its contents ran `discover` over a folder with no tests. The command now comes from the files actually WRITTEN — a plan is an intention, only what exists can be run. The reply for an empty suite says neither "passed" nor "still failing" but what happened: the files were written and their behaviour is unchecked. 4 sabotages, and **one escaped twice**: asserting on `_pick_test_command` alone left the CALL SITE untested, so it is now a real `build_app_from_spec` drive with a model that lists a test module and returns nothing for it. Building that drive exposed a second flaw in my own fixture — every per-file prompt embeds the whole project list, so keying on the filename matched every prompt and the fake built nothing at all. |
| A build asked for without an imperative verb | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | The eight-fresh-phrasing retest earning its keep. Two of the eight answered **"I couldn't map that cleanly to a real action."**: `i need <ws>/g5/wordcount.py that counts words in a block of text` and `knock up <ws>/g7/initials.py that turns a full name into initials, ta`. Neither carries a build verb in imperative position — "i need X" is a statement, and "knock up" is in nobody's verb list — and the first fixture set had happened to use imperative verbs throughout, which is exactly what a fresh set is for. `write_root_honesty` had already made the same call for the same reason (its comment records that `I need a file at /tmp/...` with no verb was read as a request to READ the file), so this is the established reading. Kept LOCAL to this lane rather than widened into the shared gate, because here it is bounded by a named source file. Restraint is decided ONCE for both routes: deliberation and opt-out are checked before either, and the second route additionally refuses anything that opens with an interrogative or ends in a question mark, and refuses a mental verb after need/want (`i need to KNOW what greet.py does` is a question; `i need retry.py` is a request). 8 build phrasings and 25 restraint phrasings, including seven aimed squarely at the new route. 4 sabotages, and **one escaped**: removing the shared deliberation check changed nothing because every restraint fixture was already stopped by the question guard — the four cases that isolate it (`lets discuss whether i need a config.yaml`, `i need retry.py but do not write any files`, and two more) are now their own test. |

No test module was generated at all. `_FLAT_IMPORT_RULE` already tells the model the project is FLAT and tells the test module to import by bare filename, so a nested source file cannot be imported by the tests written for it; a sub-directory is now dropped rather than honoured. The ordering is the load-bearing part: flattening BEFORE the escape check turned `../secret` into `<target>/secret`, because taking the last segment discards the very `..` that makes it an escape — validation runs first, flattening second. Caught immediately by the pre-existing `test_path_confinement_rejects_escapes`. 2 sabotages, each named a failing test (flattening removed; flattening moved ahead of the check). |
| A build that names its FILES reaches the builder | **PARTIAL** | Blind-QA drive 2026-07-30 against the live daemon (`e3f59af`). Four clear build instructions, four outcomes; two ended on the capability-gap report **"I do not have a real bounded builder path for that request on this runtime."** with nothing on disk — verbatim: `make a file at <ws>/qa1/fizz.py with a fizzbuzz function, and a test_fizz.py next to it. go.` and `Could you please create <ws>/qa2/greet.py containing a function greet(name)...`. That answer was false: a third phrasing (`build me a small python script in <ws>/qa3 called reverse.py...`) went down the model-build lane and produced 3 files that **parse** (`py_compile`) with 6 generated tests passing, in 573 s. New `core/agent_runtime/builder/named_file_build.py` supplies the missing noun — a real filename, the most concrete artifact noun there is — and borrows the shared instruction test via the new `build_request_intent.imperative_build_sentences`, so it is strictly NARROWER than the artifact gate and the discuss-vs-build restraint is inherited whole (14 discussion phrasings still build nothing). The same change fixes the destination: the third phrasing built in `<ws>/reverse.py/` because `called reverse.py` was read as a FOLDER name and the rooted `<ws>/qa3` was dropped for being rooted — a rooted path INSIDE the workspace is reachable, so it is now rebased rather than discarded, which is the difference between honouring a destination and relocating silently. Outside the workspace it is still dropped; `write_root_honesty` owns refusing those by name. 33 tests; **5 sabotages each named a failing test** (routing branch removed → the two wiring tests + the folder test; rebase removed; filename-as-folder guard removed; instruction gate removed → 11 restraint cases; workspace confinement removed). **Boundary — why PARTIAL:** the routing and target layers are verified, and the model-build lane is verified end-to-end for the one phrasing that reached it before the fix; the re-drive of the refused phrasings through that lane on the daemon is recorded per phrasing in the QA report. That lane is slow (573 s measured) and one of the four turns failed on the cloud provider (`I couldn't get a usable model response in this run`), not on this code. |
| Discussing a build does not perform one | **VERIFIED** | Five separate implementations of "is this a build request?" existed — `looks_like_builder_request`, `looks_like_agentic_build_request`, the inline gate in `_should_run_builder_controller`, `_looks_like_write_intent_request`, `looks_like_explicit_workspace_file_request` — each with its own vocabulary, each matching by bare substring, disagreeing constantly, so which one a turn reached decided whether files were written. Measured by calling the real functions on 17 discussion sentences and 12 real build requests: **31 wrong of 116**. `lets discuss whether we should build an api` reached the file-writing builder, as did `how would you build a bot like that`, `explain how to implement a webhook` and `why did you create that file earlier`. Substring matching also collided with English: `app` inside "happens", `site` inside "opposite", `api` inside "rapid", `cli` inside "client". The decision now lives once in `core/agent_runtime/build_request_intent.py` and draws the line at instruction versus deliberation — not framed as a question, explanation request, comparison or report of work already done, AND the build verb in imperative position (clause start, after at most a discourse or politeness marker). Clauses are judged separately, so `build me a bot, then tell me how it works` still builds. Two scopes: PROJECT (app/bot/service/cli) selects the model-driven build lane; ARTIFACT adds file and folder work owned by the workspace tools. **After: 0 wrong of 116.** 87 tests; 7 sabotages each caught — two survived the first run and the fixtures written for them exposed a real gap (`our api creates a new file per request` still claimed a build). Suite 7055 passed, 0 failed. |
| A pinned model does not hand over the runtime | **VERIFIED** | `explicit_model_owns_semantic_turn` guarded the front door with an early `return {"result": None}` — a value that means "no fast path claimed this turn", so `apps/vool_agent.py` fell through to `_prepare_turn_task_bundle` and into `_maybe_run_builder_controller`. `builder/controller.py` posts directly to Ollama's `/api/chat` with a local model tag, so the gate written to protect a pinned turn was routing it to `qwen3:8b`.
