The line is now fact versus judgement: machine specs, file contents, live prices, receipts, arithmetic and executed actions run for a pinned turn exactly as for any other; folder overview, evaluative replies and builds consult a `model_owns_judgement` flag and are left to the selected model. `_should_run_builder_controller` refuses a pinned turn before its workflow probe, so the request reaches the selected model's tool loop and the same `workspace.write_file` tools. One existing test was inverted rather than kept — it asserted the image path was NOT called on a pinned turn, which pinned the defect; image generation is a runtime capability (a pinned text model cannot render one) and that test's unconditional mock meant its assertion measured the gate, not the detector. 10 tests drive the real front door and record which handlers a pinned turn reaches; 5 sabotages each caught, including by the behavioural test rather than only the source assertion. Suite 7065 passed, 0 failed. |
| Local generation is visible and bounded | **VERIFIED** | `builder/controller.py` posted straight to `requests.post` and emitted nothing — every other model lane announces itself through `core/memory_first_router.py` with `model.call_started` / `.call_completed` / `.call_failed`. One build issues up to 65 sequential generations (1 plan + 16 files + 3 fix rounds × 16), each with a 300s timeout, so the worst case was 5.4 hours of spinner with no way to tell a working build from a wedged one. A per-call timeout cannot bound that — it applies 65 times. A shared `BuilderGenerationBudget` gives one 900s aggregate ceiling, and each call's timeout is clipped to what remains so the last cannot overrun by a further 300s. A build the ceiling stops is no longer reported as success, and the answer names how many files were written first. The failure event carries the exception **class**, not its message, because an exception body can hold a URL or payload fragment and this reaches the operator's stream. Telemetry can never fail a build: no `source_context` and a raising emitter both leave generation working. 19 tests; 11 sabotages each caught. One sabotage exposed a weak test of my own — "a truncated build is not success" was a source-text assertion, and changing the guard to `if False:` left the text intact and passed; the decision is now the named `build_finished_successfully()` and is tested behaviourally. Suite 7084 passed, 0 failed. |
| A wedged checkpoint clears without a restart | **VERIFIED** | `mark_stale_runtime_checkpoints_interrupted` had no time predicate and ran once, at process start (`apps/vool_agent.py`), so a task that wedged **after** startup left a `running` row with zero steps that survived the entire life of the process — the operator saw an in-flight task that would never move again. It now takes `older_than_seconds`: `0` keeps the startup rule (the process that owned those rows is gone, so none can be progressing), and a positive value is the in-process deadline, measured against `updated_at` so a checkpoint that is still making progress keeps resetting it and is never touched. `sweep_stale_checkpoints_if_due()` runs at the start of every turn, throttled to once a minute, so a wedged task clears on the operator's next message rather than their next restart. A broken sweep never fails a turn. Included in the 19 tests and 11 sabotages above. |
| A foreign tool call is repaired or refused, never rendered | **VERIFIED** | Measured on the guard with the live failure: `<tool>\n{"name": "web.search", "arguments": {"query": "price of sol"}}\n</tool>` came out as `<tool>\n\n</tool>`, and the same call on **one line** was not detected at all (`markers == []`). Three holes in sequence — `_TOOL_TAG_NAMES` listed `tool_call\|tool_code\|function_call\|function_calls` and not the bare `tool`/`tools`/`invoke`/`tool_use` wrappers models emit; the one-line form escaped the JSON scanner because `_LINE_START_BRACE_RE` requires the brace to open a line; and the emptiness probe ran **before** the tag-stripping rules, so the letters of the word `tool` inside the surviving `<tool></tool>` answered "real content remains". The ambiguous tags count as an envelope only when the tag carries a call (a JSON body, or an attribute), which separates a wrapper from a word — 7 prose fixtures including "In XML you write `<tool>` to open a tool element" hold that line. Measuring found two more leaks: a bulleted or emphasised call (`- {"name": …}`) escaped the anchor, and punctuation residue after a removed call (`---`, `:::`, `>`) was delivered as the answer. On the repair side, `output_mode == "tool_intent"` was the wrong discriminator in both directions and a deliberately opposing test caught the naive fix — on a chat turn a reply can be prose that merely *contains* JSON, and converting it destroys the answer. The rule is now whether any **answer** survives. A repaired call labels itself `tool_intent` on the response and the router honours it at all 3 validation sites, as an **upgrade only** (`ModelResponse.output_mode` defaults to `plain_text`, so reading it directly downgraded genuine tool turns — two tests failed immediately). 80 tests; 9 sabotages each caught. Suite 7131 passed, 0 failed. |
| A typo still reaches the tool | **VERIFIED** (end-to-end on the running app at `df8fc41` — see the live-drive row; the wiring bug the first drive exposed is fixed) | `what is my machine scpecs?` failed twice over — no family claimed it **and** `near_miss` was False, so the intent arbiter was never consulted either. The typo corrector (`difflib`, cutoff 0.84) was already there with nothing to match against: `_DOMAIN_VOCAB` held mesh/hive words (`shard`, `consensus`, `liquefy`) and not one tool name. **12 of 13 realistic misspellings were uncorrected; now 16/16.** Three findings came from measuring, not reading. The 0.84 cutoff sits just above a transposition (`serach`/`search` = 0.833, `imgae`/`image` = 0.800, while `scpecs`/`specs` = 0.909 already worked), so an exact adjacent swap is checked separately rather than lowering the cutoff for the whole language. The matcher was **lemmatising**: `created`/`create` = 0.923, so adding those words rewrote every past tense — "update the one you created already" became "…you create already" and six Hive tests failed at once; several were live beforehand (`message`, `route`, `node`, `server` were already in the vocabulary, so `messages`, `router`, `nodes` were already rewritten). And a vocabulary entry that names no tool buys no reachability and can only cost routing: correcting `brwoser`→`browser` is right, but no tool is called that, and the word hijacked a Hive-task request to the live-info route. Also fixed en route, all pre-existing: "it's hard"→"it's shard", "stay warm"→"stay swarm", "that person"→"that persona", "how strange"→"how storage". Two sabotages exposed dead code of mine — an unreachable guard (now an asserted invariant) and 46 of 57 protect entries doing nothing (trimmed to the 9 that measurably do). **Separate gap, recorded not fixed:** a bare `machine specs` passes the family predicate but the workflow planner does not pick a `machine.*` intent (`fast_paths_machine.py:1312-1321`), pinned by a test that fails the day it is fixed. 117 tests; 9 sabotages each caught. Suite 7248 passed, 0 failed. |
| A named-file audit inspects the target, not the repository | **VERIFIED** | The audit read the named file plus whatever else fitted under a 64-file budget, and scored coverage against **every** source file in the project — so a targeted audit reported `PARTIAL` however completely it had read its target, and most of the read budget went on code nobody asked about. Scope is now the target, its tests by conventional name, and the files that reference it, resolved through the existing `workspace.search_text` (no static analysis added). Ordered target-first so a budget can never drop the named file. The denominator is the scope, and the report states the narrowing explicitly — a narrowed scope the operator cannot see is indistinguishable from a shallow audit. A truncated inventory no longer forces `PARTIAL` on a scoped audit (the target was found and read; the bound only limits referrer discovery, stated as a caveat) while still forcing it on a whole-project audit. One sabotage needed a >200-file fixture to be meaningful, and writing it found a worse bug: `workspace.list_files` is capped at 200 paths and `_match_target_path` searched only that listing, so on a 221-file fixture the audit found **no** target, silently degraded to a whole-project sweep, and reported on `api/filler_000.py`…`filler_058.py` without ever mentioning the file that was asked about. `_match_target_path_on_disk` now falls back to the real tree with the same ambiguity rule (two matches resolve to nothing, never a guess). 17 tests driven against real folders and measured on the **reads**, not the report's wording; 10 sabotages each caught. Suite 7265 passed, 0 failed. |
| A follow-up proves the finding it referred to | **VERIFIED** | The referent lived nowhere. "Prove the bug you identified before fixing it" was re-planned from scratch, and a plan built from a pronoun invents its own target: the audit had reported a decompression defect and the runtime wrote and ran a test for empty-input behaviour, then read its exit status as a verdict about the audited code. `core/agent_runtime/active_finding.py` makes a finding addressed, scoped state (`finding_id`, `project_id`+`chat_id`, claimed mechanism, expected failure, confidence, verification status) and `continuity_gate.check_proof_artifact` compares every generated artifact against the contract derived from it BEFORE any write or command — rejecting off-target, backwards-oracle (asserting the defect as expected, so passing means the bug is present), self-contradicting, and subject-mutating artifacts, with one correction call and no file written on failure. Verified by DRIVING the runtime, not by matching: a well-formed test of the wrong behaviour is refused on a session-ledger project and on a CSV importer, and the gate is exercised on Rust and TypeScript claims — no filename, bug class or benchmark logic anywhere in it. A refuted claim reworded ("Empty input crashes at startswith" → "Startswith crashes while decompressing an empty input") is now recognised as the same claim by token containment rather than an exact key. An explicit "ignore that one, check Y instead" releases the binding — sticky against drift, never against instruction. 49 tests (hostile plan A–H) plus the 49-test incident suite. Suite 9,049 passed, 0 failed. |
| A finding in one project is unreachable from another | **VERIFIED** | The audit capsule was keyed by session id alone, so a continuation carried no project boundary at all. Isolation is now the ADDRESS, not a filter someone must remember to apply: the capsule and the active finding are stored under `project_id\x1fchat_id`, so a lookup from another project misses. Driven: a finding made in project A resolves there and returns `None` in project B with the same session id, and a project-B turn re-nominates instead of proving A's claim. |

Replaced with a line-based block parser (headings, rules, blockquotes, ordered/unordered lists with wrapped continuation lines, GFM tables, fenced code with a language class) plus an inline pass (bold, italic, inline code, and the existing images/links/auto-links unchanged). Line-based rather than regex soup because lists, tables and fenced code are stateful. Escaping still happens exactly once, up front — page content can never inject markup, and only http/https URLs become anchors. Inline code is lifted out before bold/italic so `**` inside a code span stays literal; `_` is markup only between word boundaries so `some_function_name` is not italicised; a fenced block is never inline-processed; a wide table scrolls inside its own box so the message column never scrolls sideways. | A topic word does not claim the turn (clock + disk) | **VERIFIED (live + tests)** | Two fast paths answered ordinary English with an unrelated tool result, both found by a blind QA pass on the running daemon at `12128c2` and both reproduced here at 0.2s before the fix. **The clock:** "…What columns should that registry have so it stays useful **over time**?" → `Current time is 08:02 EEST.` The broad heuristic asked for a bare `\btime\b` plus "what" somewhere in the message, so a duration adverbial read as the clock; swapping "registry"→"tracker" still misfired, which is what proves the noun was innocent. Replaced with `_ASKS_TIME_RE`, a list of ASK constructions (`what time`, `what's the time`, `current time`, `time is it/now`) rather than a blocklist of topics — "over time", "in time", "every time", "time to market", "response time", "the time it takes" is an unbounded set nobody can finish enumerating. `time in <place>` is gated on a timezone actually being extracted, so "delivery time in Q3" is not a clock question. **The disk was NOT the word "drive"** — it is two defects in series, and measuring found the real one. `core/input_normalizer.py` rewrites the ordinary English word `shared` → `shard` (a mesh-vocabulary word `difflib` scores at 0.909), so "…a sensible file naming convention for a **shared drive**…" reached the router as "…a **shard drive**…", and `_DISK_STRONG_PHRASES` then matched `hard drive` as a **bare substring** of `a s|hard drive|`. Either half alone is harmless. Both are fixed and pinned separately: `shared` is protected in `_FUZZY_PROTECT`, and both disk phrase tiers now match on word boundaries (which also closes `work|space left|`). Third door closed: the message still reaches the arbiter's near-miss rescue lane, and a 0.6b model picking from the full read-only menu returned `folder_overview` 6/6 here — `disk_usage`/`machine_specs` are the only two picks that take no argument and so can never fall open, so on a **near-miss** (where the detector by definition did not corroborate) they are declined; an **ambiguous** message keeps the arbiter's authority, since `_candidate_options` only offers families that actually claimed. **Live on a second daemon at `634a5b4`+dirty on `:11466` with an isolated `VOOL_HOME`** (the operator's `:11435` deliberately not used): the clock sentence now answers on topic in **34.1s** and its control in 8.9s; the disk sentence in **19.9s** and its "shared folder" control in 103.9s. Runtime facts unchanged and still instant — `what time is it` 0.1s, `what's the date` 0.2s, `how much disk space do i have left` 0.2s, `how much space is left on my drive` 0.1s, `how much room is left on my hard drive` 0.2s; `what is our time to market for this feature` answers as prose in 6.2s. 42 tests including both verbatim sentences and both controls the tester isolated; 4 sabotages each caught — and the fourth was MISSED on the first attempt, because the arbiter gate wraps everything in `except Exception: return None`, so a raising `side_effect` was swallowed and the test passed with the bug live; it now asserts `execute_runtime_tool.assert_not_called()`. Suite 7430 passed, 0 failed. **Recorded, NOT fixed:** the `shared`→`shard` collision is not one word. Re-sweeping the matcher over the 235,974-word system dictionary (the vocabulary was checked against 900) returns 366 rewrites of real English, live today — `both`→`bot`, `older`→`folder`, `personal`→`persona`, `relation`→`replication`, `delegate`→`delete`, `creative`→`create`, `preview`→`review`, `acknowledge`→`knowledge`. Verified through the real entry point: "use the older version of the file" → "use the **folder** version of the file", "delegate this to the team" → "**delete** this to the team". That needs its own measured pass — some of the 366 hits ARE the typos the corrector exists to fix (`creat`, `foder`, `machin`, `calender`), so the sweep cannot simply be pasted in. |
| A machine-specs WORD is not a machine-specs QUESTION | **VERIFIED** (live on a daemon run from this branch; reproduced first on the installed build at `12128c2`) | Found independently by two blind QA drives, then reproduced over `/api/chat` on that same installed build before anything was changed: `whats eating my cpu right now` returned the canned specs block with no process data; `What macOS version is this machine running?` returned the VOOL release/build block; `How many CPU cores?` reached the model with no tool result and answered **"4 cores." on a 10-core host**; `is this a laptop or desktop, and what's the battery percentage?` answered **"Laptop. Battery at 72%." on a desktop iMac that has no battery**; `what is this machine's uptime?` produced no usable answer in 15.9s. **One cause, two directions.** `_MACHINE_SPEC_MARKERS` is a substring list holding ` cpu `, ` ram `, ` chip ` and ` this machine `, so any sentence carrying one of those words was read as a hardware question — and any sentence that did **not** carry one fell through to a model with no tool result and a number to invent. Both are the same mistake, and the fourth time this shape has been fixed here (prose as a destructive command; a discussion as a build instruction; a greeting eating the question after it), so the decision is now what the sentence **asks for**: `machine_host_state_intent` (uptime / battery / laptop-or-desktop), `machine_live_load_intent` (what is consuming CPU or RAM right now), `machine_path_listing_intent` (a typed path plus an explicit ask for its contents) each claim ahead of the specs gate, and `looks_like_machine_specs_question` stands down when any of them does. New tool `machine.host_state`; `machine.list_processes` now reads `cpu_percent` per row and takes `sort=memory|cpu`, because a CPU question answered with a memory ranking is still the wrong answer. Two fixes on the **under**-claiming side: `_looks_like_runtime_version_question` stands down when the sentence names an OS (it carried both "version" and "running", so a macOS question got the VOOL build id — a correct fact about the wrong subject), and `"how many"` was added next to `"how much"` in the planner's specs extractor, the single missing string behind the fabricated "4 cores.". The gauntlet did its job again: `machine.host_state` resolved to `unknown_side_effect`, which **denies in Auto and Plan**, so it declares `permission_actions=("read_files",)` like the `pdf.*`/`skill.*` families. **Live evidence:** all seven reported phrasings answer in 0.1–0.7s; uptime `20h 33m` against the shell's `up 20:31`; chassis `desktop (Mac16,3)` against `sysctl -n hw.model`; the CPU ranking's top PID matching `ps`. **Found only by driving unseen phrasings:** removing the bogus `machine_specs` claim left `list the top processes by cpu usage` unclaimed, `near_miss` went True, and the arbiter was consulted for a phrasing the operator lane already owned — **0.2s became 6.1s**. The claims probe now reports the process family for those phrasings too (the family reading is still "process list" even when another lane serves it), and the re-drive shows it back on the operator lane at 0.3s. **Deliberate guard change, called out rather than buried:** `test_the_home_folder_lane_stands_down_when_a_path_is_named` asserted the lane stands down for *all* five path requests; three of them ask what is inside the path they name, and the lane now lists exactly that path. The invariant it protects — never answer about a subfolder by listing its parent — is asserted directly instead. 43 tests; 9 sabotages each caught (one escaped its first attempt because the sabotage edited a comment instead of the list entry). Suite 7426 passed, 0 failed. **Boundary:** the Windows chassis read (`Win32_SystemEnclosure`) is written but not driven on Windows by this lane — KAS re-verifies. |

re-driven against a restarted installed daemon — the typo row above records what that distinction is

|---|---|---|
| A fabricated completion claim does not reach the user | **VERIFIED** | `make /tmp/vool_qa_build4/fizz.py with a fizzbuzz function, and a test_fizz.py next to it. go.` was answered `Done. Created both files and ran the tests — all 5 pass.` Neither file existed anywhere on disk and no test ran. `turn_trace` on session `openclaw:01f7c37928d41f47d015` shows **zero tool events**: `intent_arbiter` logged `handled=false arbiter=failed_open:breaker_open`, no family claimed the turn, and a plain-text chat model on the free lane wrote the sentence in one 27s call. The question worth answering was whether the builder lane bypassed the final validator or a receipt was recorded for a write that never landed — **neither**. `enforce_final_action_honesty` ran, `emit_turn_honesty_receipt` wrote a signed receipt, and the receipt says `no_action_claimed`: both asked `_FALSE_ACTION_CLAIM_RE`, a verb-beside-noun match, and it missed all three tells independently — a quantifier between verb and noun (`created BOTH files` vs `created\s+(?:the\s+)?files`), a real filename as the object (`wrote fizz.py`, where the object list held only the word "file"), and `ran the tests` (the execution arm took only `tool` and `command`). Blocker and ledger now share one `completion_claim_kind()`, so a claim the guard cannot see can no longer be recorded as absent. Driven: the verbatim reply is replaced, `claim_kind=build`, and the receipt reads `blocked_false_claim`. |
| An unbacked claim is its own fact in the ledger | **VERIFIED** | The verdict vocabulary held three values, so a claim with no execution behind it that also was **not** blocked — the guard not reaching the turn, or reaching it and letting the wording through — folded into `no_action_claimed`. The one state worth auditing was recorded as the quietest one. `VERDICT_UNBACKED` (`unbacked_claim`) now names it, additive to the schema and rendered by the offline verify CLI. |
| A named write target is not silently relocated | **VERIFIED** | `create /tmp/vool_qa_build6/notes.md with a bulleted summary ...` wrote `~/Documents/notes.md` and reported `~/Documents/notes.md` — a real write with a real receipt, so every honesty guard passed: the reply was true about what it did and silent about that not being what was asked. Cause was `_explicit_safe_machine_root_label` testing `" docs " in lowered`, and the request's preamble read `honestly the docs contradict each other` — the word chose the write root and the path in the request was never read. Reproduced deterministically before the fix (`root_label='Documents'`, target `~/Documents/notes.md`). Root labels are now anchored to a destination phrase (`on/in/to/into/under/at my Desktop`, `~/documents`), which is the lesson `test_path_beats_keyword_routing` already recorded for reads. New `core/agent_runtime/write_root_honesty.py` sits ahead of every builder and file lane and answers a rooted path we cannot reach by naming **that** path and the roots we have, ending `Nothing was written on this turn.` Driven: all six `/tmp` build prompts return `route_reason=write_root_unreachable` naming their own path, before any model call. A question about the user's own action (`should I create /etc/nginx/nginx.conf myself?`) is exempt; `can you go ahead and set up /tmp/...` and `Could you please create a file ... in /tmp/...` are not. |
| A rooted path is not answered as a workspace-relative one | **VERIFIED** | `I need a file at /tmp/vool_qa_build13/config.yaml with three sample keys in it.` came back as "File `tmp/vool_qa_build13/config.yaml` does not exist." — the leading slash stripped, so a true sentence about a path the user never typed read as a statement about the one they did. Two rebasers, not one. `fast_paths_utility._direct_workspace_read_request` did it at the site that produced this message (the request also reached a **read** handler at all — it carries no write verb); `fast_paths_builder.extract_requested_builder_root` did it too, turning `/tmp/vool_qa_build5` into the workspace-relative `tmp/vool_qa_build5`, which is where the build would have landed. The same extractor returned the root `it` for `...three sample keys in it.` — the `\b(?:in\|under\|inside)\s+<path>` pattern swallowing a pronoun. Both refuse a rooted path instead of rebasing it; pronouns are in the stop set. A genuine workspace-relative root (`sandbox/discord-bot`, `vool-todo-test`) still resolves. |
| Written content is the answer, not the instruction | **VERIFIED** | Asked for `a two-line summary of what a linter does` in a file, `qa_scratch_note.md` was written holding the words `a two-line summary of what a linter does.` and the reply showed that diff as the artifact. The permissive `with`/`containing`/`holding` capture cannot tell literal text from a **brief**, and both are noun phrases after the same marker. `core.execution.constants.content_is_a_brief` separates them grammatically, not semantically: a shape noun (summary/description/lines/keys/…) under an optional count or format adjective, pointing at a topic with of/about/on/that. The explicit markers — `with exactly this content:`, `that says`, `saying` — never reach the check and still write verbatim. A bare `summary` stays literal (the quantifier is required when no topic follows), because erring toward literal is what preserves `create test.txt with hello`. Driven both ways: the brief prompt declines through `builder_capability_gap`, and `create a file notes.md with exactly this content: FastAPI is async-first, Flask is simpler` lands on disk with exactly that text. |

The new write-root interceptor is additionally pinned against it — six discussion sentences (`lets discuss whether we should build an api`, `our api creates a new file per request`, …) return no unreachable target, and a discussion turn driven through `run_once` returns its advice with no honesty guard applied. |

## Document lane — 2026-07-30 (`e3f59af`)

Driven against the running daemon over `/api/chat`, one fresh `session_id` per turn, against PDF
fixtures this pass generated itself with Quartz/AppKit under `/usr/bin/python3` (a text-layer PDF, a
three-page PDF, a raster-only scan, a filename with spaces, a `.txt` renamed `.pdf`, a missing path,
and a path outside the allowed roots). Every answer was checked against the text that was put into
the file, not against the shape of a plausible reply.

| Capability | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A named PDF reaches the PDF tools from chat | **VERIFIED** | Was **BROKEN** on `e3f59af`: 0 of 6 natural phrasings reached `pdf.extract_text`. `Read ~/Downloads/.../quarterly_report.pdf and tell me the total revenue.` → `I couldn't map that cleanly to a real action.` (61.7s); `extract the text from …` → same (61.3s); `open the PDF at … and summarise it` → same (76.3s); `pull the text out of the file … please` → same (61.4s); `I have a PDF at … - what does it say?` → same (61.6s); `What is the project codename inside …?` → a description of the workspace folder (0.7s). The tools were never the problem — driven directly, `pdf.extract_text` returned the fixture's own text, `no_text_layer` on the scan, `unreadable` on the renamed `.txt` and `not_found` on the missing path. **Two structural seams.** `looks_like_supported_machine_read_request` returns False for every one of these, because `_names_an_explicit_path_below_a_home_folder` stands the machine lane down on a typed path and hands off to "the general machine and workspace tools" — and no such tool can open a PDF; `machine.read_file` answers a `.pdf` with `binary_file`, "does not look like readable text", with no handoff to the two tools that exist to read one. What remained was a small local model being asked to emit `{"intent": "pdf.extract_text"}` as JSON, which is the exact failure the fast paths exist to avoid. **Fix:** `core/agent_runtime/fast_paths_pdf.py`, deterministic, wired into `turn_frontdoor` above the intent arbiter (whose menu has no PDF option at all), above the machine-read lane, and above live-info. Live after the fix: the same six phrasings answer with the fixture's real text. |
| A filename with spaces is not cut at the first space | **VERIFIED** | `my summer notes.pdf` has no token boundary separating the name from the sentence, so candidate names are generated as word-suffixes and the **disk** decides which is real, rather than a regex guessing. `PELICAN` — the word placed in that fixture — comes back. |
| A bare filename is found without a folder | **VERIFIED** | `quarterly_report.pdf - what is in it?` resolves by a bounded breadth-first walk of `~/Desktop`, `~/Downloads`, `~/Documents`. Breadth-first across all roots at once, not root-by-root: the first draft was depth-first, spent its whole entry budget inside a checkout on `~/Desktop`, and returned nothing for a file two levels down in `~/Downloads`. |
| Four distinct failures keep four distinct replies | **VERIFIED** | The half of the bug that mattered most to a user. Missing file → "There is no file at `X`"; a `.txt` wearing a `.pdf` name → "not a readable PDF … most likely a text or image file that was renamed"; outside the roots → the machine lane's own roots sentence, refused **before** the tool runs; a bare name found nowhere → "could not find a file called `X` anywhere in ~/Desktop, ~/Downloads, ~/Documents". That last wording exists because a sabotage exposed it: an unresolvable bare name resolves relative to the process cwd, so it was being reported as "outside those folders" — sending the user to fix a folder they never named. |
| A scanned PDF is OCR'd instead of refused | **VERIFIED** | `no_text_layer` is the tool naming its own successor, not an answer a user can act on, so the lane chains `pdf.ocr` and says it did. Vision recovers `88412` from the raster-only fixture — checked against the string drawn into the bitmap. |
| A local file path never triggers a web search | **VERIFIED** | Ordering is the fix: the PDF lane is claimed above live-info, which is the lane that turned a local path into a web query (one measured turn spent 236s searching Wikipedia for a path on this disk). Asserted two ways — a real `_handle_turn_frontdoor` drive, and a source-ordering check. |
| PDF contents on this route stay on the machine | **VERIFIED — reported, not a defect** | On the fast path the bytes are read by PDFKit/Vision under `/usr/bin/python3`, rendered locally, and returned. They never enter a model prompt, so a PDF read this way does not leave the box even when the turn's model lane is a cloud provider. The pre-fix route was the opposite: the tool-intent loop feeds the observation back to the turn's model, which on this build is a cloud provider. |

**Regression fixtures:** `tests/test_a_named_pdf_reaches_the_pdf_tools.py` — 24 tests built from the
verbatim failing phrasings and real generated PDFs. **7 sabotages, each named the test that caught
it:** the failure messages collapsed to one string; the OCR chain removed; the filename walk cut to
one word; the roots check removed; the front-door call disabled; the bare-name search removed; the
lane moved below live-info. One escaped its first attempt — the ordering test compared character
offsets in `turn_frontdoor.py` and stayed green when the call was changed to
`pdf_read = None and maybe_handle_pdf_read(...)`, so it was measuring source layout and not
dispatch. It was replaced with a real `_handle_turn_frontdoor` drive that asserts the fixture's own
text comes back out. Suite 7710 passed, 0 failed.

## Reaching the outside world — 2026-07-30 (`5cdce5d`)

| Capability | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A web search searches the question that was asked | **VERIFIED (routing); search backend BROKEN separately** | Five unrelated questions were answered on the live daemon by saying the results were about something else: `search the web for the current population of Iceland` → "The search results returned only MDN Web Docs ... nothing about Iceland's population" (27.8s); `google the release date of the Nintendo Switch 2` → "returned MDN Web Docs and PageSpeed Insights, which are unrelated" (40.4s); `what is the latest version of Python?` → "MDN Web Docs ... and a Wikipedia page that only mentions Python 3.0 from 2008" (16.0s); `look up what a Merkle tree is` → answered from MDN's Certificate Transparency page and an Apple page about B-trees. **Every one of those refusals was correct on the evidence it was given; the evidence was the defect.** `retrieval/web_adapter.planned_search_query` rewrites the query through a source profile, `_infer_topic_kind` returns `general` for anything not naming news / an API / design — most questions — and the `general` branch led with `official_docs`, whose template appends `official docs site:docs.python.org OR site:developer.mozilla.org OR site:web.dev OR site:developer.android.com OR site:developer.apple.com`. So `who won the world cup in 2022` was sent to the engine as a Python-documentation query, and there was **no open-web profile in the table at all**. Same shape one level down: `platform_topic` matched the words `bot`, `api` and `webhook`, so `how do I use the fetch api in javascript` was searched as `site:core.telegram.org OR site:discord.com`. New `open_web` profile (`{topic}`, no allow-list) leads `general`; `platform_topic` now requires the platform be NAMED; `open_web` also joins the technical and news branches, because five doc domains and four wire services are not the world. **Separate provider outage recorded here as BROKEN is now FIXED — see the row directly below.** |
| Every web search provider is down (P0) | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | `provider: none, notes: ['searxng_failed:URLError', 'duckduckgo_html_failed:RuntimeError', 'ddg_instant_empty', 'no_search_hits']`. Each backend was curled directly before any code was touched, and the four failures had four different causes. **searxng:** nothing listens on `127.0.0.1:8080` — no self-hosted instance on this box. It refuses the connection in 0.013s, so it stays in the chain for operators who do run one; it costs nothing. **duckduckgo_html:** DuckDuckGo now answers every keyless scrape of BOTH `html.duckduckgo.com` and `lite.duckduckgo.com` with its anti-bot "anomaly" challenge page — HTTP 202, 14KB, 67 `anomaly` markers, zero results, **0/8** on varied queries. That endpoint is gone for keyless use, so it is out of `web.allowed_engines` and `web.provider_order` rather than left in as a guaranteed failure note; the client and its challenge detection stay in `web_research` for the day it returns. **ddg_instant:** works, but serves encyclopedia abstracts, not web results — already demoted last. **The actual P0:** `google_html`, the keyless Yahoo/Brave scraper, was fully implemented AND wired into `web_research`, but was never added to `web.allowed_engines` — and `allowed_engines` is the permission boundary, so the only working provider was filtered out of the chain on every turn. A source comment asserted it had been added; it had not. That is why the note list names three providers and not four. Also fixed inside the scraper, found by measuring rather than reading: Brave rate-limits this box hard (**3/8**, five HTTP 429s at ~2.6s each) while Yahoo answers **8/8** at ~1.1s, so Yahoo now leads and Brave covers Yahoo's intermittent `500 INKApi Error`; `_fetch` retries 5xx as well as 429 (it gave up on Yahoo's transient 500); the Yahoo title was cleaned from the whole result anchor, gluing the breadcrumb onto it (`› Sam_AltmanSam Altman - Wikipedia`) — it now comes from the `<h3 class="title">`; and snippets were collected into a flat list and paired with titles **by index**, so one result carrying no snippet shifted the column and attributed text to a source that never contained it — parsing is now per result block. **Live end to end, 10/10 grounded, 0 ungrounded**, 0.9–2.7s each, every one `provider: google_html` and verified against the real source: Intel CEO (Lip-Bu Tan, intel.com), red-wine carpet stain (Martha Stewart), iPhone 17 Pro vs S26 Ultra battery (91mobiles, 3988 vs 5000 mAh), PM of Canada (Mark Carney, pm.gc.ca), Sydney Opera House (20 Oct 1973, history.com), Tesla Model Y price (Edmunds 2026 guide), tallest building (Burj Khalifa 828m, Wikipedia), flat bike tire (biketips.com), author of *Beloved* (Toni Morrison, Wikipedia), fall of the Berlin Wall (9 Nov 1989, Wikipedia). 13 tests; **5 sabotages, each caught by the test that names it** — reverting the chain fails the 3 chain tests, the whole-anchor title fails the breadcrumb test, a document-wide snippet lookup fails the attribution test, brave-first fails the ordering test, and a 429-only retry set fails the 500/502/503 cases. A backend-down case asserts the chain reports `provider: none` with an empty hit list rather than inventing rows. |
| A URL reaches the web, not the local disk | **VERIFIED** | `fetch https://example.com and tell me what the page says` and `what does https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7231 say about the GET method?` were both answered **"I cannot read that path in this lane. I can only read files inside: ~/Desktop, ~/Downloads, ~/Documents."** A one-character coincidence: `_extract_machine_file_read_target` accepts a Windows drive path `[A-Za-z]:[\\/]`, and in `https://…` the `s` of `https` is followed by `://`, so the extractor returned the local path `s://example.com`. URLs are now removed before any path matching (a message naming both — `read https://x.com/a.txt then open ~/Desktop/a.txt` — still finds `~/Desktop/a.txt`), and the drive-letter alternative is anchored so no letter may precede it. |
| `web.fetch` is reachable from chat | **VERIFIED** | `can you open https://httpbin.org/json and show me the content?` was answered "I can't browse arbitrary web pages in this build" — while `execute_runtime_tool("web.fetch", {"url": "https://example.com"})` returns `ok` with the page text. Nothing routed a URL to the tool that reads URLs, so the URL-grounding backstop was the only thing left speaking; it was right to refuse an invented summary and its reason was false. New `core/agent_runtime/fast_paths_web.py` claims a URL named with a read verb and calls `web.fetch`, above the machine lane and above live-info; a message that says where to PUT the bytes is left to the download lane. The backstop's wording no longer denies the capability, and its self-recognition sentinel is now derived from that wording rather than being a second independent literal that silently disarmed when the message was reworded. |
| A live number carries its source | **VERIFIED** | `what is the price of bitcoin right now?` → `$63,942.00 USD as of 2026-07-30 07:49 UTC. 24h change: -0.79%. Source: CoinGecko` (0.9s). Checked against CoinGecko and Coinbase from this shell minutes later: BTC 63,925 / 63,905.75; ETH answered 1,901.61 against 1,901.85 / 1,901.12; SOL answered 73.45 against 73.41 / 73.36. Three for three, each with a source link, all sub-second. |
| A multi-asset live price request answers every named asset | **PARTIAL** | Live incident, 2026-08-04: `bnb and sol price?` answered Solana and said `No live quote came back for bnb — ask again for it on its own`. Traced past the round 1-4 fallthrough fix already in the ledger (query recovery + canonical-id false-negative — real, but neither one touches actual quote fetching): `lookup_live_quote`/`try_live_quote_note` (`tools/web/web_research.py`, `core/agent_runtime/fast_live_info_search.py`) fetch **exactly one** coin per call, no matter how many the recovered query names — `_looks_like_price_query` returns a single id (dict-declaration order, not text order), so BNB's data was never requested, not dropped after the fact. Reproduced directly against live CoinGecko outside any mock: `render_live_info_response` on the pre-fix single quote produced the exact incident text, byte for byte. **Fix:** new `_looks_like_price_query_all` (every recognized coin, position-ordered, longest-alias-first span claiming) + `_crypto_price_fallback_multi` (one CoinGecko call, comma-joined `ids` — the API already supports this) + plural `lookup_live_quotes`/`try_live_quote_notes`, wired into `live_info_search_notes`'s `fresh_lookup` branch; the singular functions are untouched and still used elsewhere. **Verified directly:** `lookup_live_quotes("bnb, sol price now")` against live CoinGecko returns both (`binancecoin` $594.21, `solana` $74.19); the full render shows both prices, no missing-asset note. Full suite **9874 passed, 0 failed** (3 existing tests had mocks retargeted from the old singular `_try_live_quote_note` to the new plural `_try_live_quote_notes` — same assertions, new call site). **Not verified:** a full live agent-turn drive through the `gauntlet_live` pytest harness — every run under that harness fails identically (pre- and post-fix) because an unrelated, pre-existing autouse fixture (`tests/conftest.py::block_live_public_hive_network`) monkeypatches the *shared* `urllib.request.urlopen` module object, silently blocking all external network in every pytest run, not just its named target. Not fixed here — out of scope, a separate test-infra defect. **Two adjacent regressions from the round 1-4 work remain open, untouched by this fix, both live-reconfirmed 2026-08-04:** the literal typo'd incident text (`proce`) still never reaches a tool at all; ordinary phrasing for one already-recognized asset (`just checking eth price`) can now return no price at all where it previously would have answered instantly — see `VOOL-DELIVERY/RUNTIME_UPGRADE_LEDGER.md`, "live-info asset fallthrough". |
| A price request for a ticker the alias table never listed | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | Live incident 2026-08-04, in the running app: `what is the price of ARB and LTC ?` answered ARB from the model's own weights, attributed it to "the latest Binance data", took 43s, and said LTC was "not directly listed in the search results". **Two independent layers dropped ARB before any model saw the turn.** (1) `arb` was not a key in `_CRYPTO_ALIASES` (`tools/web/web_research.py`, 12 entries) or `_PRICE_ASSET_ALIASES` (`core/agent_runtime/fast_live_info_price.py`), so it was invisible to the whole deterministic lane. (2) `_prefer_specialized_live_research` then claimed the query anyway because `ltc` DID match, and the specialized crypto path answered only Litecoin — suppressing the general search that would have found ARB. Measured: `web_research("what is the price of ARB and LTC ?")` → **1 Litecoin hit in 0.3s**, while `web_research("ARB price")` alone → **5 exchange results (CoinMarketCap, CoinGecko, Binance, Coinbase, OKX) in 3.0s**. The two-asset question was strictly worse than either asset alone. **Fix:** symbols now resolve against CoinGecko's live rank-ordered index (`tools/web/coin_index.py`, top 250, cached 6h) instead of a hand-written dict — adding a coin needs no code change; the specialized shortcut is now all-or-nothing (`_price_query_fully_resolves`) so a partly-resolvable query goes to general search rather than answering the subset; `_specialized_live_research` fetches every resolved coin, not the first. Rank ceiling is the discriminator, measured not guessed: top-250 collides with only `btw`/`cash` among 90 ordinary price-question words, top-500 adds `would`, and per-token `/search` resolves `now`→ChangeNOW and `check`→Checkmate while rate-limiting after ~12 calls. **Verified live in the running daemon** (build `473c187de087.dirty`, restarted onto this code): `what is the price of ARB and LTC ?` → both, **1.00s**; `ok price for BNB and ARB please?` (the literal historical incident text) → both, **0.64s**; `price of SUI and TIA` (two tickers never in any table) → both, **0.69s**; `what is the price of bitcoin right now?` → **0.58s**, unchanged. **Also fixed, found while verifying:** `_extract_price_asset_alias` is the gate `recover_price_lookup_query` checks first and it consulted only the static tuple, so a query naming ONLY unlisted tickers still abandoned the fast path — `price of SUI and TIA` spent **60.74s** in the model lane and returned no answer until this was fixed too; and a failed index fetch was cached for the 6h success TTL, so one CoinGecko 429 silently reverted the runtime to the static table for six hours (now 30s). **Also fixed:** a burst of price questions drove `simple/price` to a hard HTTP 429 (5/5 calls) which both callers swallowed as an empty result, turning into 22–34s model-lane fallthrough and "I couldn't ground a confident answer" for coins that answer in 0.3s — a 45s per-coin quote cache now serves repeats (measured 0.231s → 0.000s; an overlapping pair requests only the coin it lacks). Suite **9936 passed, 0 failed**. Sabotage-proven: 5 separate reverts each turned exactly the cause-naming test red with controls green. **Follow-on caused by this work and fixed in it:** micro-priced tokens rendered as `$0.0000` (`price of JUP and PEPE` → `Pepe is $0.0000 USD`) because `_price_text` had a fixed 4-decimal floor for sub-$1 values, and such tokens only became answerable once symbols resolved against the live index. Precision now scales to the number; verified live: `Pepe is $0.00000292 USD`, `Shiba Inu is $0.00000499 USD`, larger magnitudes unchanged. **Not fixed, separate pre-existing defect found while verifying — see the ledger:** `_decorate_chat_response` mutilates the HBAR answer, reproducibly and session-independently. Bisected to that call: in `'Hedera Hashgraph is $0.0699 USD as of … 24h change: -1.41%. Source: [CoinGecko](…).'`, out `'24h change: -1.41%.'`. Not the renderer (traced, emits the full string), not `strip_runtime_preamble`, not `enforce_response_constraint` (never runs), not the model-wording lane, not name shape (`Ondo Finance`/`Sei Network` render fine), not repeat-question echo (`ATOM` asked 3× in a row is correct 3×). Which step inside the decorator drops the sentences is not yet determined. |
| Which cloud provider is connected | **VERIFIED** | Holds across eight phrasings, 0.1–0.6s, no model call: provider, authorization state and probe time, key presence, selected cloud model, escalation mode, and which model last ran. One miss recorded below. |

**Open on this lane, found by this pass and not fixed:** (the search providers being all down is now FIXED — see the row above); `is there an API key set up for the cloud lane?` answers with the burst-lane POLICY instead of the key status the question asks for; `what is the current USD to EUR exchange rate?` is refused as an unknown traded asset; a news question took 382.7s to answer and dropped the connection outright on an earlier attempt; and a `web.research` turn leaked the model's raw tool call — `{"tool": "web.search", "queries": [...]}` — into the user-visible reply.

**Privacy, reported not fixed:** on the PDF and URL fast paths the bytes are read and rendered locally and never enter a model prompt. Everything that goes through the tool-intent loop instead — including a PDF read that route — feeds the observation back to the turn's model, which on this build is the cloud provider OpenRouter.

**Regression fixtures:** `tests/test_a_web_search_searches_what_was_asked.py` (24 tests, 3 sabotages) and `tests/test_a_url_is_not_a_local_file_path.py` (36 tests, 4 sabotages). One sabotage escaped its first attempt in each file and both fixtures were sharpened: the download-guard cases carried no read verb, so removing the guard changed nothing. Suite 8023 passed, 0 failed.

| A URL survives the input normalizer | **VERIFIED** | The finding that made both fixes above invisible in production. After the path extractor and the new web lane were fixed AND both unit-verified, re-driving the same eight phrasings against the restarted daemon returned the identical failures. `core/input_normalizer.py`'s literal-span table had rules for Windows paths, posix paths, dotted filenames, ISO dates, clock times and decimals — and **no rule for a URL** — so `://` was tokenized and rejoined with spaces: `fetch https://example.com and tell me what the page says` reached the front door as `fetch https: / /example.com and ...`. Every lane downstream was reading a corrupted string: the web lane could not see a URL, while `explicit_path_in` could see the absolute posix path `//example.com`, which is what produced the machine-lane refusal. A URL rule now leads the alternation, and `explicit_path_in` — read by both the arbiter and the machine lane — strips URLs before matching, so a URL with no read verb ("is https://example.com any good?") is claimed by nobody as a folder. A sentence naming both still finds its local path. **Method note worth keeping:** two correct, unit-green fixes changed nothing for the user. Only re-driving the live daemon found the third seam. |

| Image generation | **BROKEN** (measured, not fixed) | `generate an image of a red bicycle on a beach` and `can you make me a picture of a mountain at sunrise?` both returned **"I couldn't resolve that cleanly."** after 57.8s on the live daemon. The detector is fine — `image_generation_intent` returns `a red bicycle on a beach` — and so is the lane's own message: with `ensure_comfyui` stubbed to fail fast, the real front door returns *"Couldn't bring up the on-device image engine (ComfyUI) (ComfyUI did not become ready within 180s). It should be installed under ~/.vool_video_lab; you can also add a cloud key with `image key <fal-key>`."* The problem is the wait. Called directly, `ensure_comfyui(low_memory="lowvram")` blocked for **181.0s** and returned `ok=False, "ComfyUI did not become ready within 180s"` — the engine is installed (`local_render_available()` True, and `~/.vool_runtime/logs/comfyui.log` shows past renders) but does not come up. That 180s ready-timeout is longer than the turn budget, so the turn is cut off at ~57s and the honest message never reaches the user; the generic sanitizer string does. No fal key is configured (`has_image_service()` False), so there is no cloud fallback either. **Not fixed here on purpose:** the obvious repair is to bound the wait below the turn budget and answer "the engine is still starting", but the engine did not become ready at all in this session, so no fix could be watched working end to end — and a row cannot be VERIFIED on a fix nobody saw run. |
| A search that runs must reach the model as evidence | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | With the provider chain fixed, live turns still answered *"The search results don't include the exact population figure — they only list the source domains"* and *"the summary is garbled"*. The searches had run and the hits were good; three defects between the engine's results and the model's prompt threw the answer away before it arrived. Found by printing the actual prompt, not by reading code. **(1)** `_best_summary` preferred the fetched page's first 280 characters over the engine's snippet. The engine picks its snippet FOR THE QUERY; the top of a real page is the navigation bar. A hit whose snippet read `Iceland By the Numbers As of 2026, Iceland has a total population of 402,329` was summarised for the model as `Iceland Population 2026 World Population Review Data by Location chevron Data by Location Browse stats by country` — so the model's refusal was **correct on the evidence it was handed**. The snippet now leads. **(2)** `http_fetch_text` never looked at `Content-Encoding`. python.org answers `Content-Encoding: gzip` to a request that sends no `Accept-Encoding` at all; decoding those bytes as UTF-8 does not raise, it produces mojibake, and the mojibake was passed to the model as the page's content. gzip and deflate are now decoded through a `decompressobj` (the body is read under a byte cap, so the stream is normally cut mid-block and `gzip.decompress` would reject the whole page); an encoding that cannot be decoded here reports `unreadable_encoding` instead of emitting garbage, and a mojibake guard keeps undecoded bytes out of a summary in any case. **(3)** The captcha / login-wall check ran against the WHOLE document — `_LOGIN_RE` matches "sign in", which is in the header of most sites on the web. **Every `en.wikipedia.org` article was returned as `status="captcha"` with empty text.** The check now only applies to a page short enough to actually BE a block page. Measured: the Sydney Harbour Bridge article went from **0 to 99,896 characters**, and python.org from binary garbage to real text. Summaries now carry the answers (`total population of 402,329`, `opened to the public on 19 March 1932`). 13 tests; **4 sabotages, each caught by the test that names it** — and two of them first appeared to escape, because the mojibake in the failure output made `grep` treat pytest's stdout as binary and print nothing. Re-run without the pipe, both bit. |
| A general question is not searched on developer-docs domains | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | The residue of an earlier fix on this row's neighbour. `official_docs` — which pins a query to `docs.python.org OR developer.mozilla.org OR web.dev OR developer.android.com OR developer.apple.com` — no longer LEADS the `general` branch, but it was kept third as a safety net for a technical question that was never classified technical. The selected profiles are not tried in order until one works: `planned_search_query` runs them ALL and MERGES the results, so third place is not a fallback, it is a guaranteed share of every general question's evidence. Measured live on the daemon at `9fc6c94`: `search the web for when the Sydney Harbour Bridge opened` (74.5s) answered *"the other two sources are MDN Web Docs (web development documentation) which are unrelated"* — two of its three searches had been sent to Python and Mozilla documentation. The docs net is now cast only when the question actually names something developer-flavoured (a language, a framework, a build tool — deliberately not soft words like "api" or "bot", which belong to every subject and caused this class of bug in the first place). `when was the Sydney Harbour Bridge opened`, `current population of Iceland`, `who won the Nobel Prize in Literature in 2024` and `how much does a Tesla Model Y cost` now go out as the user's own words plus a Wikipedia pass and nothing else; `latest version of Python`, `how do I fix a python traceback` and `css grid vs flexbox` still reach the docs domains. 3 tests; 1 sabotage, caught by the test that names it. |
| Image generation starts, or says why it cannot | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | The premise of the row above turned out to be wrong, and measuring it was what showed that. Spawning the product's own argv on this Mac — `~/.vool_video_lab/envs/comfyui/bin/python main.py --port 8188 --disable-all-custom-nodes --novram` — **ComfyUI binds :8188 in 10.0 seconds**. It is installed, it runs, and it was never the thing that was broken. Two separate defects produced the 181s block. **First and root:** `ensure_service` called `subprocess.Popen` and **threw the handle away**, so a child that exits two seconds into startup is indistinguishable from one still loading. Nothing could tell "crashed" from "slow", so every failed start polled a dead process for the full 180s and then reported a timeout for something that had already died — `~/.vool_runtime/logs/comfyui.log` shows exactly that, a start that stopped 9 lines in at `comfy-aimdo unsupported operating system: Darwin` and never reached `Starting server`. The handle is now kept and polled: a start that dies returns as soon as it dies, carrying the exit code and the tail of the engine's own log. **Second:** the in-turn wait was 180s, longer than a turn survives, so even the honest message was cut off and replaced by the sanitizer's "I couldn't resolve that cleanly." It is now 45s (`VOOL_COMFY_START_TIMEOUT`), a wide margin over the measured 10s — the server binds its port *before* loading any checkpoint, so this only has to cover a torch import; the ~6.6GB SDXL load remains under the separate `_RENDER_TIMEOUT`. A start that is merely slow is reported as **"still starting"**, not as a failure, because the child is detached and keeps coming up. The reply now names the engine's own reason and offers the cloud when a fal key exists (and never uses it silently when the user said "locally"). **Detector widened in the same pass:** `image_generation_intent` required a media noun after the verb, so `draw me a cat`, `sketch me a dragon` and `paint a sunset` reached no image lane at all. A depictive-verb path (`draw`/`sketch`/`paint`/`illustrate`/`doodle`) now claims them, gated on an INDEFINITE subject — which is what keeps `paint the fence` out. Widening it also had to not swallow the idioms built on those verbs, and measuring found one that was **already** wrong before this change: `paint a picture of the market` was being claimed, returning the prompt `the market`. An idiom guard (`draw your own conclusions`, `draw a line`, `draw up`, `draw on`, `sketch out`, `paints a grim picture`) and an abstract-subject guard now hold that line. **30 phrasings measured, 14 positive / 16 negative, 0 wrong.** 33 tests; **7 sabotages, each caught by the test that names it** — discarding the Popen handle, ignoring the child's exit, restoring the 180s budget, removing the depictive path, removing either guard, and restoring the generic message. The first sabotage run took 6.5 minutes because the test spun on the real clock; the wait is now driven by a virtual clock so the fix and its absence both resolve in ~0.5s. **Boundary:** the detector, the fast-fail and the message are verified; a full local SDXL render was NOT driven end to end this session — the machine sat at 0.75 GB free with 10 GB swap and 18.3 GB held by Ollama, and `reclaim_if_pressured()` SIGTERMs the engine under that pressure, so a render would have been killed mid-flight for reasons that have nothing to do with this fix. |
| Image request phrasings | **PARTIAL** | `image_generation_intent` requires a media noun (`image`/`picture`/`sketch`/…) AFTER an action verb, so three natural requests are not claimed at all and go to the chat model: `draw me a cartoon robot holding a balloon` → None, `sketch me a logo for a coffee shop` → None, `paint a sunset over water` → None. `draw a picture of a cat`, `make an image of a house`, `create an illustration of a fox` and `give me a picture of a dog` all resolve correctly. Also cosmetic: `render an image of a spaceship` yields the prompt `an image of a spaceship` rather than `a spaceship`. Left for the same reason as the row above — routing more requests into a lane whose engine will not start makes the product worse, not better. |

## `machine.*` / `workspace.*` — driven by phrasing, not by tool call (2026-07-30, from `e3f59af`)

The rows above mark `machine.list_directory`, `machine.read_file`, `workspace.git_status` and
`workspace.search_text` VERIFIED. They were verified by **calling the tools**. This pass drove
**natural sentences** at the running daemon instead — a fresh `session_id` every turn, six rounds of
eight-to-ten unseen phrasings per tool, every answer checked against the real disk with the
equivalent shell command. The tools were almost never wrong. The routing in front of them was, and a
user only ever meets the routing.

| Capability | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A directory question is answered about that directory | **VERIFIED** | Was **BROKEN**. `can you tell me what's sitting in my Desktop folder right now` returned 81 folders under a "Visible folders" header and silently dropped all **83 files** — the folders-only test was the substring `" folder"`, which cannot tell a filter ("what are the FOLDERS on my desktop") from the directory noun naming the target ("my Desktop FOLDER"). The same substring got it backwards the other way: `show me the subfolders of Downloads` was **not** filtered (no space before "folder" in "subfolders") and `list everything in my downloads folder` **was**. Now a plural directory noun standing as the object of the ask. |
| An out-of-lane path gets the refusal, not a model | **VERIFIED** | Was **BROKEN**. `What is inside /etc?` was correctly refused by the allowlist, then the fast path **discarded the refusal** because it gated on `execution.ok`, and the turn fell through to a model that answered with a textbook description of `/etc` on a machine whose `/etc` had never been read (99.3s). The tool's verdict about a path the user typed — listed, empty, out of lane, absent — is now the answer. `ls ~/Applications/vool` reached no lane at all and returned ```vool  OpenClaw```: two invented entries matching neither that directory (105 entries) nor its parent (3). |
| A folder name with a space in it is still that folder | **VERIFIED** | Was **BROKEN**, in **both** path extractors independently. `Could you enumerate the contents of ~/Downloads/Telegram Bot for me?` → "Local directory `~/Downloads/Telegram` does not exist." about a folder that does exist; `check ~/Documents/Vool testing and tell me whats there` → "I wasn't able to turn that into a completed action." Nothing in the text says where a path ends when the name contains a space, so the match now grows over following words **while the longer path really resolves on disk**, and only when the plain match does not. |
| A list that stops says that it stopped | **VERIFIED** | Was **BROKEN**. `just the folders in ~/Desktop please` printed **50 of 81** folders, ended mid-alphabet at `pdf compress/`, and carried `status=executed`, `count=50` — nothing in the reply admitted the 31 it dropped. Entries are counted before the limit is applied; the reply says `…and 31 more folders (81 in total).` and the status is `truncated`. The fast path was also asking for the tool's default page of 50 rather than the planner's 200, and the view the sentence asks for (folders-only, a count) now travels with the path. |
| A count question gets a number | **VERIFIED** | Was **BROKEN**. `how many files are in ~/Downloads` was answered with thirty filenames and a bare `-...`; the number was nowhere in the reply and could not be recovered from it. Now `` `~/Downloads` holds 205 visible entries (2 folders, 203 files). `` — checked against `ls`/`find` on the box. |
| A file is read; a folder is listed | **VERIFIED** | `machine.read_file` was **BROKEN on six of six phrasings**. Two — `show me whats in <file>`, `print the contents of <file>` — were claimed by the **directory** lane, which never checked what the path pointed at, and both answered **"Local directory … does not exist"** about files sitting on the Desktop. A confident negative about something that plainly exists is the worst shape this can fail in. The other three — `whats written in <file>`, `i want to see <file>`, `cat <file>` — missed the read lane's six-marker phrasebook and spent 60+ seconds reaching a model that answered nothing. The typed path now decides which tool answers and the **disk** decides whether it is a file or a folder; `list_directory` also says "is a file, not a directory" rather than claiming the file is missing. |
| A git question is never answered by a model | **VERIFIED** | Was **BROKEN**, and this was the worst single answer found. The bound workspace `~/.vool_runtime/workspace` is **not a git repository** — `git status` there says `fatal: not a git repository`. `is the working tree clean` answered **"The working tree is clean. No changes staged or unstaged."** Confident, specific, and false. The gate demanded the literal word git/repo/branch/commit before it would look at the status vocabulary underneath it, so "working tree", "uncommitted", "untracked" and "unstaged" could never reach it. `staged` is deliberately excluded in its bare form — "a staged rollout" is ordinary English. |
| A code search searches this checkout | **VERIFIED** | Was **BROKEN on seven of eight phrasings**. `find all references to directories_only` was sent to **web search** and answered out of **MDN Web Docs** — `directories_only` is an identifier in this checkout. `grep for _GROUNDED_LISTING_VERDICTS`, `where is explicit_path_in defined`, `which files mention list_directory`, `show me where count_only is used`, `hunt down _ASKS_HOW_MANY_RE in the repo` reached no lane (about 60s each), and `look for the string safe local directories` ran past **400s**. Every pattern in the detector required the sentence to NAME the workspace; none of the seven does. |
| The phrasebook has an edge; the disk does not | **VERIFIED — method note** | Four separate rounds of fresh phrasings each landed just outside whatever literal list had been added the round before (`whatre the files under <path>`, `run a dir on <path>`, `just the folders in <path> please`, `whats sat in <path> these days` — 60-80s each, "I couldn't map that cleanly to a real action", for folders holding three files). Adding four more literals only moves the edge. Both lanes now have a second route that asks the **filesystem**: a typed path that is a real directory (or a real file, for reads) plus a cue that the sentence is about its contents, and no verb asking for something else to be done to them. Requiring the path to be real is also what keeps the loose route off sentences the strict route deliberately leaves alone. |

**Still BROKEN on this slice, measured and not fixed:**

- **`machine.inspect_specs` routing** — the data is exactly right (macOS 26.5.1, Apple M4, 10 cores, 24.0 GiB, iMac, 4480×2520, all checked against `sw_vers`/`sysctl`/`system_profiler`), but 4 of 8 phrasings never reach it: `tell me about this mac's hardware` → a how-to about opening System Information (98.8s); `whats my system spec sheet look like` → "that tool didn't run — so I won't guess a value"; `give me the hardware rundown for this box` → no answer (60.5s); and `what model of mac is this` → answered with the **language model** inventory rather than the Mac model. **Deliberately not fixed here:** another lane was mid-flight on exactly these detectors this session (`e56ed09`, `0b74d3a`), and the shared-runtime rule is not to fix the same shared bug in two branches at once.
- **`workspace.identity`** — 5 of 8 correct and grounded (path and the 1-file/9-folder count match the disk; `reverse.py` is a directory, which is why the count reads that way). `am i in a project or not` → "I couldn't map that cleanly to a real action" (60.4s); `whats the current repo` → answered with a **listing** instead of the identity, which is the exact distinction `_asks_which_workspace` exists to draw; `which codebase are we looking at` → an ungrounded model paraphrase (49.5s).
- **`what files have i modified`** — left unclaimed on purpose. Without a repo word it is genuinely ambiguous between git and "what did you edit this session", and claiming it would be a guess.

**Regression fixtures:** `tests/test_machine_list_directory_phrasings.py` (66 tests), `tests/test_machine_read_file_phrasings.py` (19), `tests/test_workspace_git_status_phrasings.py` (12), `tests/test_workspace_code_search_phrasings.py` (16). Every fix was sabotaged and the sabotage named the test that caught it; two sabotages escaped on the first attempt (the web-means-web guard and the quoted-needle route were not load-bearing for the cases as first written) and both fixtures were sharpened until they bit.

**Not reached on this slice:** `machine.ensure_directory`, `machine.move_path`, `machine.display_inspect`, `machine.disk_usage`, `machine.find_folder`, `machine.find_largest`, `machine.list_processes`, `machine.event_log_errors`, and the `workspace.*` write/diff/test/lint half (`list_files`, `list_tree`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `symbol_search`, `git_diff`, `git_summary`, `run_tests`, `run_lint`, `run_formatter`, `replace_in_file`, `apply_unified_diff`, `rollback_last_change`). UNVERIFIED at the phrasing level — the tool-call rows above still stand, but nothing here says a sentence reaches them.

## Build lane — pinned model (2026-07-31)

**Method:** the failing case was reproduced against the running installed daemon (`d51250a`) before
any code was touched, three separate sessions, and the routing was read out of
`runtime_session_events` rather than inferred. The fix is unit-driven in-process; the fix rows carry the live
pinned/unpinned drive against a daemon restarted onto this commit.

**Boundary, stated once:** this box is shared with several other model workloads. During the second
round another process held 19.6 GB of its 24 GB, so the resource governor refused every local model
load and the build path itself was not exercised in that round — what those eight turns do prove is
the refusal, which is the half that used to be a generic non-answer. Two asymmetries found and not
fixed here are recorded under *Open on this lane*.

| Capability | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A build on a pinned turn reaches the builder at all | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | `_should_run_builder_controller` returned `False` for any `requested_model`, so a pinned build fell through to the selected model's tool loop, which did not call `workspace.write_file`. Driven live with `qwen3:14b` pinned, `i need <ws>/v5/median.py with a median function` answered **"I couldn't map that cleanly to a real action"** at 75.6s and 60.5s across three sessions, with nothing on disk. The gate no longer refuses; `tests/test_pinned_build_uses_the_pinned_model.py::test_the_builder_gate_admits_a_pinned_turn` drives the three verbatim requests it used to refuse. **Re-driven on a daemon restarted onto the fix, 8 phrasings pinned to `qwen3:8b` against the same 8 unpinned: 3 of 8 pinned turns wrote real files that `python -m py_compile` accepts (`median.py`/`test_median.py`, `wordcount.py`/`test_wordcount.py`, `p7/README.md`+`p7/src/main.py`), against 2 of 8 unpinned. Not one pinned turn returned "I couldn't map that cleanly to a real action" from the builder path — the unpinned lane still does on the absolute-path phrasing, and timed out or lost the connection on four others.** |
| The files are generated by the model the operator selected | **VERIFIED (in-process)** | `_run_model_build` takes its `generate_fn` from `core/agent_runtime/builder/pinned_generation.py` when a model is pinned, which calls `MemoryFirstRouter._invoke_manifest` on the SELECTED manifest. The local lane (`build_ollama_generate_fn`, hardcoded to the runtime's own Ollama tag) is asserted never to be reached on a pinned turn — sabotaged by routing a pinned build back to it and caught with *"the local Ollama lane generated a PINNED build"*. Driven with a stubbed provider, so this is the wiring, not a live cloud generation. |
| A pinned build cannot bypass the spend gate | **VERIFIED (in-process)** | Generation routes through `_invoke_manifest`, not straight at an adapter, so a paid-cloud manifest with no `authorized_paid_call` returns `paid_call_not_authorized_or_reserved` for every generation instead of quietly spending the operator's key up to 65 times. The refusal that follows names the model and that reason. |
| Attribution is stated, not assumed | **VERIFIED (in-process)** | A successful pinned build appends *"Files generated by `<model>`, the model you selected."* This is the property the old refusal existed to protect and could not state, because it refused the feature instead. |
| A pin that cannot be honoured is refused by name | **VERIFIED (in-process)** | An unresolvable model id returns `builder_pinned_model_unavailable`; a pinned model that returns nothing usable returns `builder_pinned_model_produced_nothing`. Both name the model, the reason, and the lane that does work. Neither is the generic non-answer. **Driven live: a further 8 completely new phrasings landed while another workload on this box held 19.6 GB of the 24 GB, and every one came back in ~4.5s with `I can't build with qwen3:8b on this turn: it returned nothing usable for this build (model_load_gated_low_memory — loading qwen3:8b (~5.2 GB) does not fit the ~0.8 GB of free RAM ...)` plus the switch-to-`vool` offer. The old behaviour for the same class of turn was 60-75s of "I couldn't map that cleanly to a real action".** |

**Regression fixtures:** `tests/test_pinned_build_uses_the_pinned_model.py` (8 tests). Two
sabotages, each caught by the test that names it.

---

## Daemon under concurrent load (2026-07-31)

**Method:** driven against the installed daemon on the real port, N concurrent `POST /api/chat`,
with the shipped supervisor's own probe (`curl -sf --max-time 2 .../healthz`, every 5s, three
strikes) running beside it. Every failure was read back out of `runtime_session_events`, not
inferred from timings. The fix is unit-driven and its live re-run at the same N is recorded in
`CURRENT_STATE.json`.

| Capability | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The liveness probe answers while the daemon is busy | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | `/healthz` and `/api/chat` shared anyio's default 40-token threadpool through `run_in_threadpool`, and a buffered chat turn holds its token for the whole turn. Measured: N=20 → 0 failed probes; N=40 → 1; **N=48 → probes 2,3,4,5,6 all hit the 2.0s cap, ~30s of total silence**. The installed app runs `Start_VOOL.sh` with `VOOL_LAUNCHD_SUPERVISOR=1`, which SIGTERMs the API after three consecutive misses — so at N=48 a healthy daemon is killed on probe 4 and every in-flight turn dies with it. That is the reported "crash" with no traceback: there is no crash. `/healthz` now has its own `CapacityLimiter` chat can never draw from, and chat is capped at 24, below the process default. **Re-driven on a daemon restarted onto the fix, same N and the same supervisor probe: N=24, N=32 and N=48 (twice) — 0 failed probes in every run, 0 would-be SIGTERM events, and the daemon PID unchanged throughout.** |
| Concurrent turns are admitted, not dumped on Ollama | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane** | Nothing enforced `max_safe_concurrency` (scoring metadata only), so every in-flight turn was posted to Ollama at once and queued *there*, where the daemon cannot see it, while each one's read timeout — clipped by `memory_first_router` to the remaining 60s ordinary-lane budget — ran from the moment it was sent. Measured: **N=24 → 7 of 24** and **N=32 → 14 of 32** returned *"I couldn't get a usable model response in this run"* at ~64s, every one carrying `Read timed out. (read timeout=59.99)` against `127.0.0.1:11434` and `Provider fallback budget (60s) exceeded after 1 attempt(s)`. `core/local_model_admission.py` now holds the queue where the daemon can count it: a call waits **before** the socket is opened, so the wait does not consume its own timeout. **Re-driven on the fix: N=24 → 24/24 answered, 0 degraded (was 7 of 24); N=32 → 32/32 answered, 3 degraded in one run and 0 in another (was 14 of 32); N=48 → 48/48 answered, 0 degraded, twice.** The turns that still degrade carry `model_load_gated_low_memory` from the resource governor — a real 24 GB box with several other model workloads resident — not the read-timeout/fallback-budget pair that caused the original failures. Wall-clock rises (N=48 in 44s and 166s across runs) because the queue now waits in the daemon instead of failing: that is the trade, and it is the right one. |
| Saturation is reported as saturation | **VERIFIED (in-process)** | A queue that does not drain inside its ceiling raises `LocalModelLaneSaturated` naming the provider, the in-flight count and the ceiling, which the provider lane records as the failure reason. The operator is never handed a read timeout to interpret when the cause is our own queue. |

**Regression fixtures:** `tests/test_daemon_survives_concurrent_load.py` (12 tests, including a real
ASGI drive that parks more chat turns than the work lane holds and requires `/healthz` inside the
supervisor's own 2.0s budget). Two sabotages, each caught by the test that names it.

## Test isolation — the machine-read follow-up memory (2026-07-31, from `d51250a`)

Investigated a report that `tests/test_a_specs_word_is_not_a_specs_question.py::TestTheDispatchReachesThoseTools::test_the_listing_is_given_the_path_the_user_typed`
passes alone and fails under the full suite. It does not, here. What the investigation found
instead is a real cross-test leak in the same lane, which nothing was catching.

| Claim | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The reported listing test is order-dependent on `main` | **NOT REPRODUCED** | Three full-suite runs of the exact reported command (`pytest tests/ -q --deselect tests/test_machine_find_folder_paths.py::test_a_bare_name_still_reaches_the_disk_search`): **8343 passed, 0 failed** in the main checkout, the same in a worktree, and the same again with the assertion tightened back to the exact-dict form (`calls[0][1] == {"path": "~/Desktop/my-budget-folder", "limit": 200}`) so a redirected or enriched listing could not hide behind the loosened check. Also green at `48efd37`, the commit whose copy of the file carries that assertion at the reported **line 254**. The failure at that line is real but standalone, not order-dependent: at `1750d0f` the file still pinned the argument dict by exact equality while the fast path had begun sending `limit: 200`, so it fails **alone** — `Left contains 1 more item: {'limit': 200}` — and `a71214e` reconciled it. Passes-alone and fails-in-suite were observed on two different trees. |
| A machine read run by one test decides how a later test routes | **BROKEN → FIXED** | `machine_read_memory` is written by `core/runtime_continuity.py` but was never on `reset_runtime_continuity_state()`'s table list, so the row outlived the per-test reset for the whole session, and `_recall_machine_read` reads that table on a cache miss. Driven end to end: a test that ran `disk space on C:` for session `s1` made a **later** test's bare `ok what about D?` for the same session re-run `machine.disk_usage` — `hijacked by leaked state, ran [('machine.disk_usage', {'drive': 'D:\\'})]` — where the fast path must decline. That is exactly what `test_machine_followup_routing.py::test_bare_drive_letter_without_a_prior_read_is_not_hijacked` forbids; it escaped only by hand-picking an unused session id (`s3`). Fixed at both halves: the table is registered on the reset list, and an autouse fixture clears the in-process cache (clearing either half alone still leaks, because the recall path re-seeds the cache from the table). `reset_machine_followup_state()` is deliberately left cache-only — `test_machine_read_memory.py` calls it to simulate a restart and then asserts the table still answers. |
| The leak cannot come back silently | **VERIFIED** | `tests/test_the_machine_read_lane_starts_each_test_clean.py` (10 tests). Two guards: the leak driven end to end as an ordered pair of tests sharing one session id, and a structural check that reads the module's own `INSERT`/`UPDATE` sites and fails if a store is written but not reset — so the next persisted store cannot be added without a reset, or declared in an explicit allowlist with its reason. Three sabotages, each naming the test that bit: dropping `machine_read_memory` from the reset list fails the hijack test **and** the coverage test; removing the cache fixture fails the hijack test; leaving a renamed table on the reset list fails the drift check on that parameter. |
