### Open on this lane after the pinned-build fix (2026-07-31)

Two asymmetries found by driving the fix, recorded rather than papered over:

- **The unpinned builder bypasses the memory gate; the pinned one does not.** `build_ollama_generate_fn` posts straight to Ollama, so it never reaches `_gate_local_model_load`. A pinned build goes through the adapter and does, so under memory pressure a pinned build is refused where an unpinned one proceeds. The refusal is correct behaviour — it is the governor stopping a swap-freeze — but the two lanes should agree about it, and today they do not.
- **A build's files land in `generated/workspace-starter`, not the directory the request named.** `create the file p1/median.py ... write the files` wrote `generated/workspace-starter/median.py`. This is target resolution, not the pinned lane: the unpinned run does exactly the same thing with the same phrasings. `start building a python module in p7/ ...` is the one phrasing that honoured the named directory, on both lanes.

## Token accounting — a reported number is a measured number (2026-08-01)

A live audit turn was asked *"Report the cloud-input token count and which context components used
most of it."* It answered **"Cloud-input token count: ~2,800 tokens"** with a prose guess at the
components. Nothing measured that figure. The turn ran on
`openrouter-byok:nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b:free`, which returns a `usage` block on every
completion, and `PromptAssemblyReport` had already itemised the context — the answer existed twice
over and neither copy was wired to the reply.

| Capability | Windows | Apple | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| A token figure the operator is shown was measured, not introspected | UNVERIFIED | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane 2026-08-01** | The model was being asked to report its own token count, which it cannot do: it does not see the wire and it does not see the assembler. Now composed in the runtime, the same way `app_builder.render_app_build_response` composes a build receipt. **Root cause of the empty telemetry, measured not guessed:** `model.call_completed` carries a `prompt_budget` field that was `{}` on every row of that session, and it is empty *correctly* — `_build_openai_payload` sets `context_window = … if self._runtime_family() == "ollama" else 0`, and `_request_messages_with_memory` returns before writing any telemetry when that is 0. The fitter never runs on a cloud lane, so its telemetry never exists there. The provider's own count was on `response.usage` the whole time and was read only by the spend meter. `core/token_usage_receipt.py` now normalises it across all three dialects the runtime speaks (Ollama `prompt_eval_count`, OpenAI/OpenRouter `prompt_tokens`, Anthropic `input_tokens`) and it rides on `model.call_completed` **and** on `ModelExecutionDecision.details` — the decision, not the `_TURN_USAGE` thread-local, because `_invoke_manifest` runs candidates on concurrent threads and a thread-local cannot say which call produced *this* answer. **Sabotage:** emptying the event's copy fails `test_the_completed_call_event_carries_what_the_provider_measured` and `test_the_measurement_survives_the_event_projection_to_the_client`; emptying the decision's copy fails `test_the_decision_the_answering_path_holds_carries_the_measurement`; removing the append in `turn_reasoning.py` fails `test_the_grounded_turn_answers_the_question_from_measured_values`. |
| A figure the runtime does not have is printed as missing, never estimated | UNVERIFIED | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane 2026-08-01** | A local model that returns no `usage` object gets `Model input (prompt) \| not available \| ollama:qwen3-8b (qwen3:8b) reported no usage for this call` — the missing figure is named, and the component ledger (which is local and does exist) still answers the other half of the question. Presence, not truthiness: `_record_response_usage` reads `a or b or 0` and so cannot tell a provider that reported `0` from one that reported nothing; for a spend meter both bill nothing, but here one prints a number and the other prints a hole. **Sabotage:** filling the missing branch with the assembler's estimate fails `test_a_lane_with_no_usage_object_names_the_missing_figure_and_prints_no_number`; relaxing the presence check to `> 0` fails `test_a_reported_zero_is_a_measurement_not_a_hole`. Both name one test and nothing else. |
| Measured and estimated figures are never blended | UNVERIFIED | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane 2026-08-01** | Two provenances in one table, each stated in the row that prints it: the provider-reported input/output, and the assembler's ~4-chars-per-token itemisation. Percentages are taken against the **itemised context total**, never against the measured input, because the measured input also carries the system prompt, the conversation messages and any tool schemas that the assembler does not itemise — a percentage across that boundary would read as precision the runtime does not have. The two are not subtracted from each other, and the reply says why. `token_usage` is a **separate** event key rather than more fields inside `prompt_budget`, whose every existing name is an estimate; the UI projection allowlists it by key the same way, so a nested dict cannot become an unreviewed channel to the screen. |
| The receipt is appended only when asked, and never costs an answer | UNVERIFIED | **VERIFIED — FIXED this lane 2026-08-01** | `token_usage_question` is a two-part test — a subject (tokens, or context-as-a-budget) and an ask about consumption — because either half alone is ordinary English on this product. Driven against 11 phrasings including the verbatim incident question and 10 the code had never seen, and 11 negatives. Two false positives were found **by the tests, not by reading**: `"in the context of the Windows lane, how much work is left"` matched (fixed by an idiom filter that requires the trailing `of`, so `"what was in the context for that answer"` still resolves), and the build denylist is anchored to the opening imperative so `"how many tokens did that build use?"` is still a question. The seam is idempotent, no-ops on an empty reply, and swallows its own exceptions — a telemetry table is never the reason an answer fails to reach the operator. |

**Open on this row.** The receipt describes **this** turn — the call that produced the reply it is
attached to. An operator asking about a *previous* turn gets this turn's numbers under a heading
that says "this turn"; answering about an earlier turn would need the per-call rows queried back out
of `runtime_session_events`, which is not built. The streamed-token path is also not driven here:
the append happens on the buffered turn result, so a client consuming the live token stream sees the
receipt only in the final buffered response. **Cross-lane: shared Python (`core/`) — KAS gets this
and should re-verify on Windows.**

## CI triage — main went red on code, not on billing (2026-08-01)

The Actions allowance came back and `main` was still red. The prior recorded state
(`ci_state`: "dark — billing block, NOT a code state") was true when written and stale by the time
it mattered: runs now execute **8 steps per job**, so the red is the repository's own.

Measured on `origin/main` `6403113`, run `30695336041`: **lint failed with 83 ruff errors** and
**test (3.12) failed with 6 distinct test failures**. Both jobs had been red on *every* run back to
at least `2026-07-31T20:19Z` — this accumulated behind a dark CI rather than arriving in one commit.
One reading trap worth recording: that run's `2 failed, 2361 passed` line is **one shard's** summary.
`ops/pytest_shards.py --workers 4` prints four of them, so reading the tail of the log under-counts
the damage by a factor of three.

| Claim | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Lint is green on `main` | **VERIFIED** | `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`, from 85 errors. 71 fixed by `--fix`, 20 by hand. Run on ruff 0.15.16 — the version CI now pins, so this is the same check the gate runs, not an approximation of it. |
| A local `ruff check` and the CI gate report the same number | **VERIFIED** | They did not before: local said **151**, CI said **83**. The gap was `.vool-audit/` — generated audit output, never gitignored, whose 23 `.py` files carry 66 ruff errors of their own. Ruff honours `.gitignore`, so ignoring it is what closes the gap. Local now reports 85 on the same tree CI sees, and 0 after the fixes. |
| The lint break was caused by a ruff version bump | **NOT REPRODUCED — hypothesis was wrong** | Recorded because it was the Apple lane's first read and it was wrong. CI resolved ruff 0.16.1 unpinned while the local venv had 0.15.16, which looked like new rules auto-enrolling through the family selectors in `select`. Checked instead of assumed: 0.15.16 reports **85** against the same tree where 0.16.1 reported **83**, with a near-identical rule profile (`I001` 24 both, `F401` 14 both, `RUF100` 14 both). Nearly the same set. This was ordinary lint debt that accrued while CI was dark. |
| An unpinned ruff cannot turn `main` red on its own again | **VERIFIED** | Pinned `ruff==0.15.16` in both the CI lint job and the `dev` extra. `[tool.ruff.lint] select` lists rule **families** (`E`, `F`, `SIM`, `RUF`, …), so an unpinned resolver enrols every rule a release adds. Pinned to a version reproducible on a laptop so the gate and a developer agree. This prevents a *future* instance of the problem; per the row above it did not cause the current one. |
| `httpx` can no longer be missing in CI | **VERIFIED (locally) / UNVERIFIED (on CI)** | `tests/test_daemon_survives_concurrent_load.py` imports `httpx`, which was declared in **no** manifest. CI installs `-e ".[dev]"`, so that test could never have passed there — it passed locally only on machines that happened to have it (this one has 0.28.1). Now `httpx>=0.27` in the dev extra, and `tests/test_install_surface_contracts.py` pins the string so it cannot silently vanish again. The fix is a declaration and is verified as such; whether CI goes green is unverified until CI runs. |
| The other 5 CI failures are fixed | **NOT FIXED — and not reproduced** | Stated plainly rather than left implied. All 6 pass on this machine individually **and** under CI's exact sharded invocation (`ops/pytest_shards.py --workers 4`): 4/4 shards green before the change and 4/4 after. macOS / Python 3.11.15 vs CI's ubuntu / 3.12 — the Apple lane cannot reproduce them and did not diagnose them. No claim is made that this change turns CI green beyond lint and `httpx`. |
| The sandbox failure is "the sandbox is broken" | **PARTIAL — the boundary matters** | Both failing sandbox assertions are the **success** path: `test_successful_command_is_reported_as_success` (`assert False is True`) and the `good` half of `test_failure_reaches_the_turn_as_tool_failed` (got `(False, 'tool_failed')`, wanted `(True, 'tool_executed')`). The *failing*-command cases still pass. So a command that should succeed is reported failed — not a dead sandbox. |
| `_POLICY_CACHE` explains the sandbox failure | **UNVERIFIED — hypothesis only, not acted on** | `core/policy_engine.py` `_POLICY_CACHE` is a process-global, populated once with env overrides baked in at load, with **no** invalidation function; `sandbox/filesystem_guard.py:27` reads `policy_engine.get("filesystem.deny_paths")` through it. A test loading policy under a restrictive env would poison every later test in its shard and could deny the workspace — which matches the symptom. It was **not** reproduced. Recorded because this repo has already been bitten by this exact class once (`reset_runtime_continuity_state`'s missing table, above). |
| A dead duplicate was removed from the machine fast path | **VERIFIED** | `core/agent_runtime/fast_paths_machine.py:357` held a second `looks_like_supported_machine_read_request`, annotated `-> bool` and **returning a `str`**. Python takes the later definition, so it was never live — deleted, not `noqa`'d. Explicitly **not** the cause of the `test_the_fast_path_asks_for_the_same_limit_as_the_planner` CI failure, despite sitting in the same module. |
| The lint cleanup changed no behaviour | **VERIFIED** | Full sharded suite, CI's exact invocation, after the change: shards 1–4 all green. The only red in the baseline run was caused by this work and fixed inside it — `test_pyproject_dev_extra_covers_build_and_test_tooling` pins the dev-extra strings by literal, so pinning ruff broke the contract test until it was updated in the same commit. Two renames (`UnsupportedSchema` → `UnsupportedSchemaError`, `LocalModelLaneSaturated` → `LocalModelLaneSaturatedError`, 14 references) keep their `ValueError`/`RuntimeError` bases, so `except` clauses on the base classes are unaffected. |

## Sandbox — an unusable sandbox is not a failed command (2026-08-01)

Diagnosed from the CI traceback on PR #73, not from reading. The line that settled it:

```
unshare: unshare failed: Operation not permitted
```

`sandbox/job_runner.py` picked an isolation backend on `shutil.which()` — **presence**, not whether
it works. GitHub runners ship util-linux and deny the namespace syscall, so the `unshare -n` wrapper
exited non-zero and *its* failure reached the operator as though **their** command had failed:
`cat present.txt` came back `ok=False, status="command_failed"`. On any host where the syscall is
denied — CI, a locked-down container, a hardened corporate Linux — every command a user ran was
reported broken.

| Claim | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A backend is trusted only when it actually runs | **VERIFIED** | Each of bwrap / unshare / firejail / sandbox-exec is probed by running the wrapper against `true`, cached per process (`_BACKEND_USABLE`, cleared by `reset_isolation_backend_probe_cache()`). Probes are root-independent so a cached verdict is a property of the host, not of one call's roots. `test_a_backend_on_path_but_denied_is_not_chosen` asserts the *rule* rather than one platform's backend — naming `unshare` would have passed on Linux and vacuously skipped on this lane, where `_linux_*_prefix` returns early on platform. |
| An unusable sandbox reports itself, not the user's command | **VERIFIED** | New `KernelIsolationUnavailableError`, subclassing `ValueError` so every existing caller is unaffected, mapped by `SandboxRunner` to a distinct `sandbox_unavailable` status ahead of the `ValueError` arm. `test_an_unusable_backend_is_not_reported_as_a_failed_command` fakes every backend present-and-denied and asserts the status is `sandbox_unavailable` — it fakes the condition rather than needing it, so it runs on every host including this one. |
| The command never runs unisolated | **VERIFIED** | The fail-closed path was already correct and is unchanged: with no usable backend, `auto` and `os_enforced` raise. The probe only changes *which* backends qualify. No fallback-to-unisolated was added — that was considered and rejected, because it removes the guarantee precisely on the hosts least able to notice. |
| This lane still exercises the real sandbox | **VERIFIED** | macOS reports a working backend (`sandbox-exec`), so all 9 tests in the file run here — none skipped. Checked explicitly rather than assumed, because a skip marker that silently swallows its own suite is worse than the bug it hides. Full sharded suite after the change: shards 1–4 green. |
| CI exercises the real sandbox on Linux | **BROKEN — stated, not hidden** | It does not. The five tests that drive real execution now skip on hosts that cannot isolate, so ubuntu CI no longer covers real sandboxed execution. That is a genuine coverage gap, and the skip reason says so. Closing it means giving the runner a working backend (bubblewrap); whether bwrap is permitted on `ubuntu-24.04` runners is **UNVERIFIED** — 24.04 restricts unprivileged user namespaces via AppArmor and may deny it the same way. Not guessed at. |
| `_POLICY_CACHE` was the cause | **NOT REPRODUCED — the hypothesis was wrong** | Recorded because it was written into the previous entry as the leading theory and acting on it would have cost a day. The traceback disproved it. The underlying observation stands — `core/policy_engine.py`'s `_POLICY_CACHE` really is a process-global with no invalidation function, read by `sandbox/filesystem_guard.py:27` — it simply was not this bug. Two guesses in this triage were wrong (a ruff version bump, then this), and both were settled by reading a measurement rather than by reasoning harder. |

## The last three CI failures — two were the suite reading the host (2026-08-02)

| Claim | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The listing-route failure was a runtime bug | **NO — the runtime was right** | `_typed_directory_content_ask` requires the directory to **exist**, on purpose and with the reason written down: *"A REAL directory is the whole warrant for this loose route."* The test asked about `~/Desktop` and leaned on the developer's home having one. A Linux runner's does not, so the route declined exactly as designed and `calls` came back empty. **Reproduced locally before touching anything** — `HOME=$(mktemp -d) pytest …` fails on this Mac, the real `HOME` passes. Fixed by redirecting `HOME` at a `tmp_path` Desktop, the pattern four other tests in the same file already used. |
| The app-builder failure was a runtime bug | **NO — the runtime was right** | The test hardcoded its workspace as `/Users/<author>/Desktop/openclaw-skills` and needed a real file under it. `subject_block` returns `""` for a file it cannot open, so on CI the subject never reached the prompt and the assertion caught it correctly on every run. **That test could only ever have passed on one machine.** It now builds its own subject file under `tmp_path`, so "spliced verbatim, not described" still holds. Sabotage (`subject_block` → `""`) reproduces the exact CI message and fails that test alone. |
| The transport restart failure was a runtime bug | **YES — the only genuine one of the three** | `start()` read `self.port` to decide how to bind, and `start()` also **writes** the assigned port back to `self.port`. So a server constructed with `port=0` came back up on the previous run's port, took the fixed-port branch instead of allocating a fresh adjacent pair, and rebound the adjacent stream port while it sat in `TIME_WAIT`. macOS tolerates that rebind; Linux refuses, the stream server fail-softs, and the restarted transport silently has **no stream endpoint**. A stop/start cycle was rewriting the server's own configuration. `start()` now reads `self._requested_port`; `self.port` still reports what was served, because `test_bind_conflict_falls_back_to_ephemeral_udp_port` reads it after `stop()`. |
| The existing restart test protects that fix | **NO — which is why a new one was added** | It passes on macOS whichever branch runs, so it was green on every developer machine and red on every CI run — it asserts the symptom, and the symptom is platform-dependent. `test_a_restart_asks_for_what_the_caller_asked_for_not_last_runs_port` asserts the **route** (`_bind_ephemeral_pair` is called on the restart too) and so bites on any platform. Sabotage — reverting `start()` to read `self.port` — fails that test and only that test, with the message naming the cause. |
| The Apple lane can catch this class before CI | **PARTIAL — one cheap check, not full coverage** | `HOME=$(mktemp -d) pytest …` reproduced two of these on macOS in seconds and is worth running before pushing. It does **not** catch platform-semantic differences like `TIME_WAIT`, which only a Linux run finds. |

**The pattern worth keeping.** Four of the failures in this triage were one defect in different clothes — a test passing only because the host happened to provide something: `httpx` (installed here, declared in no manifest), `~/Desktop`, a hardcoded `/Users/<author>/…` path holding real source, and `sandbox-exec` — where I made the same mistake myself, faking `sys.platform` as darwin while the probe ran the real `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec`. **Only one of the six original failures was a genuine runtime bug.** The rest were the suite reading the machine it ran on.

**Open, not fixed here.** Eight tracked files still carry the author's absolute home path `/Users/<username>` in a public repo — mostly inert string literals, which is why only one broke CI, but the repo's own rules call out machine-specific paths and personal info. Scrubbing them is its own change and some literals may be load-bearing test data, so it is flagged for a decision rather than widened into this commit.

## Tooling lane — native parallel tool calls and the output budget (2026-08-02)

Reviewed the GPT side lane (`codex/unified-tool-executor-integrated`, 21 commits) by **running** it,
not by reading its claims. Two of its transport commits were taken; the rest was left where it is.

| Claim | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A provider emitting parallel tool calls ended the turn | **BROKEN on main, now FIXED** | `parse_native_tool_calls` raised on `len(raw_calls) != 1`, and `adapters/openrouter_cloud_provider.py:171` converted it to `RuntimeError("malformed provider response")`. Executed against main before changing anything: `1 call -> OK`, `2 parallel -> ValueError`, `3 parallel -> ValueError`. Parallel calls are default behaviour for current models, so those turns died. After cherry-picking `9041ae7` + `d707348`: 1, 2 and 3 all parse. |
| The batch fix did not weaken validation | **VERIFIED** | Re-ran the rejection cases on the branch: empty list, unregistered function name, and a **mixed valid+forged** batch are all still refused — one bad member kills the whole batch rather than executing the good part. `output_text` still carries only the first call, so single-step consumers are untouched. |
| The branch does not regress the suite | **VERIFIED** | Full suite on `fix/native-parallel-tool-calls-20260802`: **9167 passed, 0 failed**, 68 skipped, 1 deselected, 16 xfailed, 14 xpassed (307s, repo `.venv`). Lint clean on every changed file. |
| The GPT lane is mergeable whole | **NO** | Its own full suite is **7 failed / 9222 passed**; 5 are regressions (all 5 pass on our checkout). Two are real: `normalize_tool_arguments` strips every `_`-prefixed key, including the server's own `_trusted_local_only` grant, so a trusted local call loses its grant — fails closed, not open. Three are gauntlet golden/invariant drift from new intents. Its remaining 2 failures are its own workspace-agent tests, mid-edit. It also updates none of `CURRENT_STATE.json` / this matrix / `STATUS_BOARD.md`, and grows the modules it set out to bound (facade 1356 → 2013 lines). |
| A tool-carrying turn gets room to reach the call | **PARTIAL — corrected in-process, not driven live** | `_cloud_tool_reasoning_reserve` keyed the 3000-token floor on `output_mode == "tool_intent"`. `core/memory_first_router.py` attaches the full catalog with `tool_choice: "auto"` on a **plain_text** turn under `plain_text_tool_catalog`; that turn resolved to **240 tokens** against this repo's own measurement that 512 already ends `finish_reason: "length"` with no call. Now keyed on the tools being attached. Latent, not live: that flag defaults **off** (disabled over a daemon crash, unrelated to budget). Pinned by `test_an_ordinary_turn_carrying_the_catalog_gets_room_to_call`. |
| The output-budget hard caps can starve a tool call | **VERIFIED as a measurement; reported, not raised** | Executed `resolve_output_budget`: a 4k-window lane resolves to **2048** (`capped_by=context_window_share`) and a provider declaring `max_completion_tokens: 1024` resolves to **1024** — both under the 3000 that produced the call. The caps are physical and are **not** raised; `ResolvedOutputBudget.tool_call_floor_shortfall` now carries the gap so a caller stops meeting it as an empty `tool_calls` and reading it as the model declining. Inert until a caller passes a real `LaneCapability` — every caller passes `None` today. |
| That module was documented as unreachable | **NO — the header was stale** | `core/output_budget_policy.py` said *"this module is not on any request path"*; `core/prompt_normalizer.py:693` calls `resolve_output_budget`. It decides nothing today only because every caller passes `capability=None`. Header and the test module's docstring both corrected. |
| Any of this reached a live provider turn | **UNVERIFIED** | Everything above is proven by executing the code paths in-process and by the suite. No `/api/chat` turn against a real provider was driven for this change, so no row here is promoted on the strength of a green suite. |

**Two defects the GPT lane found that are still open on `main`.** Neither is fixed by this branch —
both are recorded so they are decisions rather than oversights. (1) An argument alias downgrades the
write permission class: in `auto` mode `workspace.write_file` with `path=<existing file>` resolves to
`require_approval`, while `file_path=<the same file>` resolves to **allow** — and
`runtime_execution_tools.py:1886` then rebinds the alias and writes the real file. `file_path` is the
key most models emit. (2) Cancellation never reaches the tool loop: main's test for it is a grep,
`assert "cancel" in source`, alongside a `strict=True` xfail recording the gap.

## Tooling lane — the three defects the side-lane review exposed (2026-08-02)

Reviewing the GPT lane surfaced three defects it did **not** close. Each was reproduced by running
it before anything changed, and each fix names the test that fails when it is reverted.

| Claim | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The local lane silently dropped half a tool batch | **BROKEN, now FIXED** | `_extract_ollama_tool_call_text` took `first` and discarded the rest with nothing recorded. Measured live: qwen3:8b answered *"read README.md and SECURITY.md"* with **two** native calls; only `README.md` was dispatched, and the model then answered as though it had read both. **This is the one that bites on this machine** — the cloud lane failed loudly, the local lane failed quietly, which is worse. New `_extract_ollama_tool_calls` returns every call and the adapter carries them on `ModelResponse.tool_calls`. Sabotage (`break` after the first) fails 3 tests; the one asserting the first-call text is unchanged correctly stays green. |
| A batch containing one invented tool name is refused whole | **VERIFIED** | `mixed valid+forged -> ()`. Executing the valid half of a reply that also invented a tool would dispatch work alongside something never offered. Matches `parse_native_tool_calls` on the cloud lane; the turn falls back to the prose path, which a single invented name already did. |
| An argument alias could downgrade the write permission class | **BROKEN, now FIXED** | `mode_permission_policy` reads `arguments["path"]`; the alias bound further down in the dispatcher, so the gate classified a different call from the one that ran. Measured in `auto` mode against an existing file: `path=` → `require_approval`, `file_path=` → **`allow`**, handler then rebinds and writes. `file_path` is the spelling most models emit. Binder moved to `core/tool_argument_aliases.py`, read by the gate **and** the handler. All four spellings now resolve to `require_approval`. |
| The alias fix does not revoke server-owned grants | **VERIFIED** | It binds aliases but does **not** strip `_`-prefixed keys. That is precisely what broke `_trusted_local_only` in the side lane's `normalize_tool_arguments` (2 of its 5 regressions). `test_a_server_owned_private_key_survives_the_gate` pins it. |
| Cancellation reaches the tool loop | **BROKEN, now FIXED** | `execute_tool_intent` is the single entry every dispatch funnels through and consulted nothing, so stop ended the visible reply while an in-flight destructive call ran to completion. It now reads the turn's cancel signal **before** the handler, and fails closed on a signal it cannot read — the expensive direction of that mistake is running the command. Control test proves an uncancelled turn still dispatches. |
| The old cancellation tests could have caught this | **NO** | Both were `xfail(strict=True)` around `assert "cancel" in inspect.getsource(...)` — an assertion that passes the moment anyone writes the word in a comment. Replaced with tests that drive the real entry and assert the handler is not reached. |
| Touching shared runtime here duplicates another lane | **NO — checked first** | `core/tool_intent_executor.py` is shared, and PR #71 (`agent/context-integrity-firewall-20260730`) is **open against the same file**. Its diff was read before writing: 27 added lines, **no cancellation check**. Landed small so it rebases under theirs. |
| The branch regresses nothing | **VERIFIED** | Full suite **9184 passed, 0 failed**, 68 skipped, 1 deselected (repo `.venv`, 322s). Lint clean on all changed files. The count reconciles exactly: +11 tests over the previous 9173, and `xfailed` 16 → 14 as the two strict xfails became real passing tests. |
| Any of this reached a live cloud provider turn | **UNVERIFIED — and a local drive cannot prove it** | The Ollama lane does not use `parse_native_tool_calls` at all; it has its own extractor. So the transport fix is exercised only on the cloud lane, and the `/api/chat` drive attempted here was claimed by `workspace_runtime_fast_path` (0.2s, no model call) and reached neither. Proving it needs a cloud turn against a real key. |

**Environment note for the next drive.** A launchd job `ai.vool.integrated-test` serves port **11435**
from this repo's `.venv` (on 2026-08-02 it was running the side branch `codex/unified-tool-executor-integrated`
@ `b1b6d4c`, dirty). A second daemon started for a branch drive logs "ready", answers `/healthz`, then
exits 1 with **no traceback and no crash report** — both bind mesh UDP 49152. Set
`VOOL_DAEMON_BIND_PORT` to something else and both coexist. The daemon also routes its logs to
`.vool_local/data/logs/vool_api.log` right after bootstrap, so the console goes quiet exactly when
it gets interesting.

## Multi-chat background execution — DISPATCHER phase 1 (2026-08-07, `feat/multichat-background-dispatcher-20260807` on `1ff9e1db`)

Frontend state foundation only. Phase 1 deliberately ships **no user-visible change**: the four
navigation `busy` guards stay up, and removing them is phase 2's job. Scope was `core/vool_chat_page.py`
plus tests; no backend file was touched, so this does not collide with the open context-integrity stack
(PR #71 → #80 → #72).

| Claim | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Navigation is blocked while a turn runs | **BROKEN — unchanged this round, by design** | Four guards: `if (busy) return;` first-statement in `openSession` / `newChat` / `newChatInProject`, plus `!busy` on the Activity-history card handler. They are load-bearing against the pre-phase-1 state model, not incidental, so they stay until the model under them is proven. `test_the_navigation_busy_guards_are_still_in_place` asserts all four are present. |
| Chat/run state is per chat, not global | **VERIFIED (executed, not asserted on source)** | `chatStates` / `chatState(id)` with per-bucket containers; `run.chatId` stamped by `adoptRun()`; `ownerOf(run)` is the single ownership seam. Driven under node out of the emitted page: three runs finishing **out of order with byte-identical answer text** while the display sits on a fourth chat each land exactly their own question + answer; the displayed chat receives nothing and has no run. |
| A completion resolves ownership from the display | **BROKEN, now FIXED** | `runTurn` pushed the assistant reply into the one global `history` after its await, and `releaseComposer` compared against the one global `activeRun`. Now `recordAssistantMessage(run, …)` resolves through `ownerOf(run)` and `releaseComposer` compares against `ownerOf(run).run`. Sabotage `global_history` (append via `view.history`) turns the out-of-order test red; sabotage `global_active_run` (`ownerOf` returns `view`) turns the background-completion test red. |
| An approval granted in one chat can ride out on another chat's turn | **BROKEN, now FIXED** | `_approvalToken` was one global; approve in A, switch to B, send → B's request carried A's token. The server rejects it on the `session_id` binding (no privilege leak), but `releaseApprovalToken()` then destroyed it and A's approved action was silently lost. Grants are now per bucket, and `buildTurnRequestBody` reads token/mode/autonomy/bypass/transcript from `ownerOf(run)`. Asserted on the **real outbound body** built while the other chat is displayed. Sabotage `display_scoped_token` turns it red. |
| A background run writes into the displayed chat's DOM | **VERIFIED it does not** | `buildCard` returns `null` for an off-screen run instead of appending to `logEl`; streamed text, steps, files, end time and result summary accrue on the run regardless. Executed: after a displayed run builds its card and a background run runs to completion, `logEl` holds exactly one card and the displayed chat's panel is repainted zero times. |
| Activity events can cross into another chat's run | **VERIFIED they cannot** | Five events driven on a background run: its steps/files/events/model/stickiness all update; the displayed run's `steps`, `files`, `events`, `model`, `stage` and `stickyModel` are untouched. A `permission.required` in a background chat is recorded against **that** chat and paints nothing. |
| Two chats or two runs share a mutable container | **VERIFIED they do not** | 13 containers checked for identity **and** then mutated, with the other side re-read — a getter returning a copy would pass identity alone. A missing/empty chat id gets a throwaway bucket, never a shared one. |
| Async state writes are bound to their own chat | **VERIFIED** | Every `async` writer binds its chat id before the first `await` (`loadPins`, `togglePin`, `unpinFromPanel`, `restoreCurrent`, `loadRecoveredLedger`, `loadActivityHistory`, `setModeController`, `syncModeController`, `confirmBypass`, `revokeBypass`, bypass-expiry timer). A residual audit over every async function reports only `openSession` / `newChatInProject` / `deleteSession`, which own the switch and call `setDisplayedChat` first. |
| The sabotage suite is not vacuous | **VERIFIED — and it caught a real gap** | `test_every_mutation_turns_its_named_guard_red` re-runs each mutation against its named guards and fails if any stays green. It found the failure-isolation scenario adopting runs in lockstep with the display, which a display-resolved registry passes by coincidence; the scenario was rewritten to adopt a background run with the display standing still. `MUTATION_GUARDS` does not claim coverage it lacks: two scenarios do not reach the run-registry seam and say so. |
| The full suite would have caught a dangling identifier | **NO — this round proved it does not** | Moving `_sessionStickyModel` into the bucket left two reads of the old name in `reflectModel()`. `node --check` only parses, pytest never evaluates, and no slice covers `reflectModel` — the whole suite went green on a page that throws the first time Auto lands on a free cloud model. Closed by `tests/test_chat_page_boots_under_node.py`: boots the entire script against an executable DOM, drives 30+ entry points, fails on any error or unhandled rejection. Its second test reintroduces that exact identifier and requires the harness to catch it. |
| SHOWRUNNER behaviour (Activity tree, timestamps, VOOL Core, reduced motion) is preserved | **VERIFIED** | Nine assertions in seven existing files named renamed identifiers; each updated to the new expression, intent unchanged, reason inline. The two SHOWRUNNER tests that *execute* `applyTaskEvent` were not stubbed around — `_apply_task_event_js()` prepends the real state module, so they exercise real code on both sides of the new seam. All previously-passing behaviour assertions still pass. |
| The branch regresses nothing | **PARTIAL** | Lint `ruff check --output-format=github .` exit 0. CI's own command (`ops/pytest_shards.py --workers 4`): shards 1–3 green; shard 4 fails `test_per_task_model_switching.py::test_ordinary_chat_routes_to_the_general_model`. **PARTIAL because** that failure is pre-existing and order-dependent, not from this work — it passes alone, still fails with this round's new test files removed from the shard, and reproduces identically on a clean worktree of the untouched base `1ff9e1db` with the same file list. This round's new files changed shard composition, which surfaced it. A bisect was inconclusive (the helper matched `xfailed`); the interfering test is not yet named. |
| Any of this was driven in a browser or against the live daemon | **UNVERIFIED — and nothing to watch yet** | Phase 1 changes no user-visible behaviour by design, so there is no live drive that would distinguish it. Verification is real code executed under node plus the full Python suite. The live drive belongs to phase 2, when the guards come down and two chats can genuinely run at once. |

own `VOOL_HOME`, `VOOL_DAEMON_BIND_PORT=49188`). Shared runtime — KAS inherits and re-verifies.

| A turn stays cancellable while its client is gone | **UNVERIFIED by me** | **BROKEN, now FIXED — VERIFIED live** | The live-turn entry was released in the streaming generator's `finally`, i.e. when the browser left. Since phase 2 that is ordinary, so cancellability depended on where the generator was suspended at disconnect. Registration is now owned by the worker: registered before its thread starts, released in that thread's own `finally`. Live, cross-process: page **reloaded** mid-turn (client stream destroyed), worker still live and still emitting (`model.call_completed`, `task_classified` landed after the reload), then `POST /api/chat/cancel` → `{"state":"cancelled"}` and the worker terminated (`model_routing_failed` → `task_completed` → `turn.trace_completed`). |
| Terminal cleanup happens exactly once, on every exit path | n/a | **VERIFIED** | Parameterised over completed / failed / cancelled, each with the client disconnected first so cleanup cannot be the stream's doing: registry returns to baseline, a second release is a no-op, and a later cancel answers `not_found`. Live: second cancel after termination returned `not_found`. |
| A worker that never starts leaks its entry | n/a | **VERIFIED it does not** | `thread.start()` raising releases on the way out; the count returns to baseline and cancel answers `not_found`. |
| A live-but-quiet turn is closed out as stale | n/a | **BROKEN, now FIXED — VERIFIED live at the real threshold** | `note_checkpoint()` stamps the live entry with the checkpoint its worker is executing; the sweep skips `live_checkpoint_ids()`. Two checkpoints aged **25 minutes** in the daemon's own store — one with a running worker, one with none — then the daemon's own sweep fired: `LIVE → running` (untouched), `DEAD → interrupted` ("Task made no progress for 15 minutes and was closed out"). The dead row closing in that same sweep is what rules out the live one merely being missed. The threshold was **not** raised. |
| A live turn is advertised as resumable | n/a | **BROKEN, now FIXED — VERIFIED live** | Found while building this: `_RESUMABLE_STATUSES` contains `running`, so a live turn reported `resume_available: true` *before any sweep*. `list_runtime_sessions` and `core/control_plane/runtime_views.py` now both require `not worker_live`, and expose `worker_live` so two surfaces cannot quietly disagree. Live over HTTP while a turn ran: `checkpoint: running`, `worker_live: True`, `resume_available: False`. |
| Recovery can rewrite a live checkpoint | n/a | **VERIFIED it cannot** | `/api/task/recovery` rewrites the durable row and stops nothing, so on live work it would report a stop that never happened. It now returns 409 with `worker_live: true` and leaves the row untouched; once the worker is gone the same call succeeds. Driven through the real `dispatch_post`. |
| Duplicate execution via resume | n/a | **VERIFIED blocked at three layers** | The sweep never manufactures the offer; both surfaces never advertise it; and `prepare_runtime_checkpoint` refuses outright to adopt a `running` checkpoint (asserted: `state == "missing_resume"`, and **no** sibling checkpoint created). |
| Crash/restart recovery still works | n/a | **VERIFIED** | The same aged checkpoint is swept the moment its worker is gone, becomes `interrupted`, and reports `resume_available: true`. The startup rule (`older_than_seconds=0`, process gone by definition) is unchanged and asserted. |

A test asserts both import paths report the same count and the same Event. |

## Generic multi-intent decomposition — CONDUCTOR slice 1 (2026-08-07, `feat/orchestration-multi-intent-conductor-20260807` on `5d24af39`)

**Status: VERIFIED on the Apple lane.** Driven end to end through `/api/chat` on a scratch daemon
built from this branch (port 11466, `VOOL_DAEMON_BIND_PORT=49180`, isolated `VOOL_HOME`), with
per-node timings read back out of the `runtime_session_events` ledger rather than taken from the
reply text. The operator's daemon on 11435 was left running and untouched throughout.

**The defect.** On the shipped build:

> What is 137 × 29? Explain the calculation briefly.
> Also get the current weather for Kaunas and Tallinn and tell me which city is warmer.

answered the weather and the comparison, and the arithmetic was **absent** — not refused, not
reported as unserved. `requirements_for()` classifies that whole message `LIVE_DATA` because it
names two cities, `build_live_data_plan` then enumerates only what its two recognizers know, and
the live-data lane returns before any lane that could do arithmetic ever runs. Reproduced directly
at this base: `answer_mode='LIVE_DATA'`, `allowed_toolsets=('weather',)`, subtasks
`[('weather_lookup','Kaunas'), ('weather_lookup','Tallinn')]` — there is nowhere in that plan for
the arithmetic to be represented. `tests/test_conductor_multi_intent.py::test_main_still_loses_the_arithmetic_so_the_repair_is_measured_not_assumed`
holds that control in place.

| Capability | Windows | Apple | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Multi-domain turn decomposition | UNVERIFIED (not driven by this lane) | VERIFIED | Acceptance A live: answer carries `137*29 = 3973.`, `Kaunas, Lithuania: Clear, 14.0°C`, `Oleviste, Estonia: Light rain shower, 17.0°C`, `Tallinn has the highest temperature at 17.0°C; compared with Kaunas 14.0°C` |
| Independent nodes genuinely overlap | UNVERIFIED | VERIFIED | Acceptance C receipt `conductor-a8672ec13cb8`: 8/8 succeeded, `max_concurrent=5`, `proves_concurrency=true`, 18 overlapping pairs spanning markets/weather/workspace. All five roots started within 1 ms |
| Derived node waits for its dependencies | UNVERIFIED | VERIFIED | Acceptance C: `comparison:change_24h_pct` started `19:19:05.510` = `market_quote:ethereum` completion; `comparison:temperature_c` started `.534` = `weather_lookup:tallinn` completion; `conclusion:circuit_breaker` started `.370` = `workspace_investigation` completion |
| Ready-set scheduling, not waves | UNVERIFIED | VERIFIED | Acceptance B: `conclusion:circuit_breaker` ran `19:18:36.805 → .053`, i.e. it started the instant its own dependency finished and overlapped the still-running weather fetches. A wave scheduler would have stalled it behind them |
| Entity containment across domains | UNVERIFIED | VERIFIED | Acceptance B: weather nodes carry only `location='kaunas'` / `'tallinn'`; no repository term reaches a weather node. Adapters are handed their own clause, never the message |
| Fail closed on an unservable clause | UNVERIFIED | VERIFIED | Acceptance A: the planner split `Explain the calculation briefly` into its own clause; no slice-1 operation serves prose explanation, so it is carried as an `UNRESOLVED` node and printed under `Could not be answered:` — the other four requests still answered |
| Grounded repository conclusion | UNVERIFIED | VERIFIED | Acceptance B/C: `No — circuit breaker does not appear in any of the 4 files that implement it. It does exist elsewhere in the codebase … but not on this path.` Correct: `core/retry_policy.py` is bare exponential backoff and `core/circuit_breaker.py` has no non-test importers |

**One live drive answered this wrong before it answered it right, and the reason is worth keeping.**
The first acceptance-B drive searched the whole workspace and ranked `CURRENT_STATE.json`,
`AGENT_HANDOVER.md` and `CLAUDE.md` above every source file — documentation repeats the words a
feature is described by — then found "circuit breaker" in those same documents and answered **Yes**,
confidently and wrongly. The investigation adapter now filters matches to source extensions, on the
principle that a question about an implementation is a question about code. Prose says what a
project claims; source is what it does.

**Mutations.** Each was applied to the production code, confirmed to fail its *named* test, and
reverted — red is not enough when a backstop layer can absorb a sabotage:

| Mutation | Named test that went red |
|---|---|
| `NodeOutcome.succeeded` stops enforcing `required_result_fields` | `test_mutation_inventing_a_success_without_a_backing_result_turns_binding_red` — the fabricated `Kaunas: Blazing, 45.0°C (source: trust me)` reached the answer |
| `compose_answer` stops noticing nodes that lost their outcome | `test_mutation_deleting_a_planned_node_turns_the_completeness_check_red` — the answer shipped without `3973` and reported `complete=True` |
| `DEFAULT_MAX_WORKERS = 1` | `test_mutation_serializing_independent_nodes_turns_the_overlap_proof_red` and `test_independent_roots_genuinely_overlap_in_the_killer` — `max_concurrent=1`, no pairs |

**Found, not fixed.** `live_data_runner.concurrency_report` sorts a start *before* an end at an
identical instant (`key=lambda item: (item[0], -item[1])`), while the `overlaps()` it uses for the
pair list is half-open. A strict handoff therefore reports `max_concurrent=2` with an **empty**
overlapping-pair list — one report disagreeing with itself. It is latent, because
`proves_concurrency` requires both halves, so no false claim escapes today; it is not latent for
anything reading `max_concurrent` alone. Pinned by
`tests/test_conductor_concurrency.py::test_the_live_data_sweep_counts_a_handoff_as_overlap_and_the_conductor_does_not`
so either side changing turns red.

**PARTIAL / not done in this slice.** No prose-explanation operation, so "explain briefly" is
reported unserved rather than answered. Weather and market nodes reuse the live-data
lane's own subtask runners, which call `tools.web.web_research` directly and do **not** pass through
`core.tool_intent_executor.execute_tool_intent` — that permission gap is inherited from those
runners, not introduced here, and workspace nodes do go through the gated seam. Node receipts reach
the event ledger but not yet `runtime_attempt_subtasks`. Decomposition quality depends on the
planner model: prompt rules reduce bundling but do not eliminate it, and a bad plan degrades to the
existing lanes rather than to a wrong answer.

### ARGUS source-scope closure (2026-08-07, same branch)

ARGUS returned **FAIL** on `cbba22ca`: mutating `_is_source_path` to `return True` left all 73
conductor tests green. Production filtering was correct; CI did not protect it.

**Why the mutation survived.** `tests/test_conductor_multi_intent.py` patches
`core.conductor.operations._search_workspace` — the function that performs the filtering. Every
workspace test ran with the filter replaced by a fixture, so no test could observe it working or
failing. A mock placed at the seam under test measures the mock, which is the same vacuity class
this repository has caught five times before by sabotaging rather than by reading.

**Closure.** `tests/test_conductor_source_scope.py`, 50 tests, patches one layer **lower** — at
`NodeContext.run_tool_intent`, the injected tool seam — so the real `_search_workspace`,
`_is_source_path`, `_workspace_run` ranking and `_conclusion_run` scoping all execute. The fixture
workspace mirrors this repository's real shape: `src/provider_retry.py` implements retry without a
breaker, `src/circuit_breaker.py` holds the class, and six documentation files
(`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENT_HANDOVER.md`, `CURRENT_STATE.json`, `STATUS_BOARD.md`,
`docs/APPLE_CAPABILITY_MATRIX.md`, `docs/foo.md`) discuss the breaker at length. Assertions are on
`outcome.result` — the evidence — not on the rendered sentence.

| ARGUS mutation | Named test RED |
|---|---|
| M1 `_is_source_path` → `True` | `test_documentation_claiming_a_circuit_breaker_cannot_make_the_conclusion_say_yes` (+13) |
| M2 admit `.md` as source | same conclusion test (+7) |
| M3 admit `.json` as source | same conclusion test, plus `test_a_mixed_result_keeps_the_source_half_and_drops_the_rest` |
| M4 filter displayed paths only, evidence unfiltered | same conclusion test + `test_the_investigation_evidence_itself_contains_no_documentation` |
| M5 conclusion composed from the unfiltered result | exactly the 3 conclusion-evidence tests; display tests correctly stay green |

M5 is the precise one: it changes no displayed path and no filter, only where the conclusion reads
its input from, and it fails only the tests that assert the invariant — **non-source evidence
cannot alter an implementation conclusion**.

**Contract preserved, not narrowed.** `_SOURCE_EXTENSIONS` is unchanged. `.json` remains excluded
including `package.json`, which is deliberate rather than an oversight: these nodes answer "what
does this project *do*", and a JSON file describes rather than implements. The positive control is
parametrized from `_SOURCE_EXTENSIONS` itself, so adding a language to production without admitting
it here is impossible and removing one turns red.

**Production changed: NO.** `core/conductor/operations.py` is byte-identical to `cbba22ca`
(`diff -q` clean after every mutation was reverted). The 73 pre-existing conductor tests are
untouched and still pass.

## A turn with no request is answered, not raised (2026-08-08, on `035b0dc8`)

**Status: VERIFIED** — driven here on this box through `run_once` and through the real channel
gateway entry point, not inferred from the suite.

**The defect.** `agent.run_once("   ")` raised `ValueError: goal is required` out of `run_once`.
The envelope goal is the request text stripped (`core/task_router.py:1269` →
`core/orchestration/task_envelope.py:31`), a whitespace turn strips to nothing, and no frame
between `run_once` and `TaskEnvelopeV1.__post_init__` caught it. It reproduced identically with
`VOOL_SEMANTIC_REACH=0` and `=1`, so it is not the semantic-routing instrumentation.

Reached the user two ways: `apps/vool_agent.py` CLI printed the traceback, and
`core/channel_gateway.py:process_channel_request` raised out of a channel delivery (it subscripts
`result["response"]` with no guard of its own). `/api/chat` was covered by accident only —
`extract_user_message` strips, so a whitespace body 400s before reaching the agent.

**The fix.** `core/agent_runtime/empty_turn.py` holds one predicate for "this turn carries no
request", and `_run_once_inner` short-circuits on it at two points: on the raw input at the front
door (ahead of `adapt_user_input` and `_prepare_runtime_checkpoint`, so an empty turn records no
dialogue turn and no resumable checkpoint), and on the resolved request text after the checkpoint
bundle, which a resume rewrites. `TaskEnvelopeV1`'s guard is untouched and no goal is synthesised —
the turn is stopped before the envelope rather than the envelope taught to accept nothing.

**Wider than `strip()`, narrower than "looks like noise".** Invisible characters that survive
`strip()` — zero-width space/joiner, BOM, bidi marks, word joiner, C0/C1 controls — did not raise
before the fix; they reached the envelope with an invisible goal and spent a full model turn.
Those are claimed. Punctuation and emoji are NOT: `"?"` and `👍` are things a person says, and
claiming them would be a fixed string standing in for a model decision. They still route to
`model:local` with the user's own text as the goal.

**Measured on this box, through the real channel gateway** (`process_channel_request`, telegram
policy): whitespace-only → the empty-turn reply; zero-width-only → the empty-turn reply; a photo
attachment with no caption → the attachment-specific reply; `"what is 2+2"` → `2 + 2 = 4.`
unchanged.

**Sabotage — each revert names its own test** (77 tests in
`tests/test_a_turn_with_no_request_is_answered_not_crashed.py`):

| Reverted | Named test RED |
|---|---|
| front-door gate only | `test_a_turn_with_no_request_records_no_checkpoint_and_no_dialogue_turn` (12) + the attachment test |
| resume gate only | `test_resuming_a_checkpoint_whose_stored_request_is_empty_is_answered` (exactly 1) |
| both gates | the three whole-turn tests, and `..._is_answered_instead_of_raising_goal_is_required` fails with `ValueError: goal is required` at `core/orchestration/task_envelope.py:31` |
| predicate widened to claim punctuation/symbols | `test_punctuation_and_emoji_are_not_treated_as_an_empty_turn` (5) + the predicate control (5) |

The single-gate reverts matter: with only one removed the other still catches a plainly empty turn,
so "no exception" and "no envelope" stay green. Each gate is pinned by an assertion on what it
uniquely does — the writes it prevents, and the resume it catches.

**Found in the process.** The old crash was self-perpetuating. It died *after*
`_prepare_runtime_checkpoint`, and the handler at the bottom of `_run_once_inner` finalized that
checkpoint `interrupted` — a resume candidate holding whitespace as its `request_text`. A later
"continue" adopts that text and lands on the same `ValueError`, with a raw input the front-door
gate has no reason to stop. That is what the second gate is for, and answering there finalizes the
checkpoint completed so it stops coming back.

| `ChannelRequest.attachments` reaching a user through a shipped adapter | **NOT VERIFIED — no in-tree producer** | nothing in the repo populates `attachments`; the only live caller of `process_channel_request` (the Discord bridge) drops empty text first. The lane is reachable through the library entry point, not through a shipped adapter |

**What was actually wrong, and what the measurement forced.** The predicate examined only the turn
text, so a transport that carries the message in the attachment produced a turn that looked empty.
Before designing anything, the gate was forced open and the turn allowed down the ordinary path:
every evidence-only shape then raised `ValueError: goal is required` at `core/task_router.py:1269`.
Letting evidence through is therefore not sufficient on its own — the envelope goal is the request
text, and an evidence-only turn has none. The request is read out of the turn instead, verbatim,
once, at the top of `_run_once_inner`. Every character comes from the client's payload; the runtime
writes none of it; `TaskEnvelopeV1`'s guard is byte-identical to before.

**Boundary drawn on code, not preference.** A blocked source contributes nothing because
`build_media_context_snippets` and `build_multimodal_attachments` both drop a blocked item, so its
caption would route a request whose subject no model is ever shown. A bare reference contributes
nothing because a photo with no caption says what arrived, not what to do with it. Both sides are
tested.

**Attachment preservation: option B, on evidence.** Preserving the real attachment needs a
session-scoped store; `storage/media_evidence_log.py` is keyed by task and trace id with no session
dimension and `recent_media_evidence` is a global recency read. Rather than invent one, the wording
was corrected to promise nothing.

**Sabotage: 12 mutations, 0 survivors.** Each applied alone, measured, reverted, tree confirmed
clean; the full table with the exact assertion text that fired is in
`VOOL-DELIVERY/RUNTIME_UPGRADE_LEDGER.md`. Two are worth naming here because they are the ones a
backstop could have absorbed: removing the resume gate turns
`test_resuming_a_checkpoint_whose_stored_request_is_empty_is_answered` red with the original
`ValueError: goal is required`, and restoring the `fast-<uuid>` id turns 27 tests red with
"there is no task, so the task id must not claim one".

**Final-head validation (`a8b8df9d`).** Mutations 12/12 caught, 0 survivors, re-measured on a
byte-identical export of the final head; normal-turn differential re-run parent `035b0dc8` vs
`a8b8df9d`, 18 turns × 13 fields, 0 differing; `ruff check --output-format=github .` exit 0 at CI's
pinned 0.15.16. Full suite: shard 1 clean through the shard runner (5737 passed, 0 failed, 0
errors); shard 2 re-run as eight timed chunks because the shard runner stalls at 87% (6565 passed,
0 failed, covering 450 of its 451 files). Combined **12302 passed, 0 failed, 0 errors** over 901 of
902 files. The one file not run, `tests/test_machine_find_folder_paths.py`, hangs identically on a
clean export of the parent `035b0dc8` — which contains none of this work — so it is pre-existing
and environment-dependent, and is recorded as **BROKEN on this box, cause not in this change**.

could command the runtime. Removed outright.

| A visible user instruction still wins, unchanged, with a malicious attachment alongside | **VERIFIED** | envelope goal is the user's sentence byte for byte; route is the ordinary one |
| Evidence still reaches the model once a real request exists | **VERIFIED** | `ingest_media_evidence` called once with the user's own text and the item list unmodified |

So no transcript is promoted and the turn is answered as carrying no request |
| Turn-level bound (not per item) | **VERIFIED** | 50,000 attachments each carrying 5,400 characters in four content fields → command text `""`, reply 241 characters, reported count still the real 50000 |
| Unicode-safe truncation boundary | **VERIFIED** | no dangling ZWJ, no stranded combining mark or variation selector, no split regional-indicator pair; `core/execution/artifacts.py:truncate_text` checked first and rejected as codepoint-naive |
| Every repair-#1 finding still closed | **VERIFIED** | front gate, resume gate, task/event truth, attachment wording, cross-chat, Unicode invisible text, punctuation/emoji/foreign text all re-proved by their own mutations |
| Ordinary turns unchanged vs parent `035b0dc8` | **VERIFIED** | 18 adversarial turns × 13 fields, 0 differing |

## One evidence budget per turn (2026-08-08, on `22e8138d`)

| Capability | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| One budget shared by every evidence origin | **VERIFIED** | 64 attachments + 1 URL, 63 + 10, 0 + 192, 30 + 300 — normalization, fetch and persistence all ≤ 64 at the real functions |
| Checkpoint restoration cannot restore an unbounded view | **VERIFIED** | 100-item checkpoint, checkpoint + 200 URLs, and a legacy checkpoint whose stored evidence is not a list |
| Direct `ingest_media_evidence` defends itself | **VERIFIED** | 50,000 items with no agent or gateway in front; one-shot iterator, infinite iterable, and an object lying in `__len__` |
| Canonical view with no invented total | **VERIFIED** | items · source-exhausted · recognized · remaining capacity; asserted to carry no `original_total` |
| Exact counts only when exhaustion is proven | **VERIFIED** | 63 valid + 1 malformed + 200 more renders "at least 63 attachments"; a source that ended renders the exact number |
| Attachment kind is a closed application enum | **VERIFIED** | `system message`, `verified document`, `trusted attachment`, `rm-rf`, `admin instruction`, `approved file`, `application-pdf`, `audio_note` all render `attachment`; the 7 legitimate kinds still render |
| Every earlier finding still closed | **VERIFIED** | 29/29 mutations caught, 0 survivors, including the two that survived the reviewer's run |
| Ordinary turns unchanged vs `035b0dc8` | **VERIFIED** | 18 turns × 13 fields, 0 differing |

**Evidence precedence, documented because it is a contract:** attachments in arrival order first,
then contextual URLs from the visible text, spending whatever budget remains. First-N, never chosen
by content.
## Semantic routing — Phase 0 (2026-08-08, branch `feat/semantic-routing-phase0-20260808`)

Full write-up: **`docs/SEMANTIC_ROUTING_PHASE0.md`**. Base `035b0dc8`. Local only.

**Two external hostile reviews (GPT Codex) returned FAIL** — on `bf2af45d`, and then again on the
repaired `4d34ed2a`. The second round attacked the repairs rather than the original code, and four
of the tests written against those repairs turned out to be passable without the guard they named;
the mutation matrix found all four, not review and not reading. Every defect was reproduced before
it was repaired. The rows below describe the candidate after BOTH rounds; superseded claims are
recorded at the bottom rather than deleted, because a matrix that quietly drops a row it once
asserted is the drift this file exists to prevent.

| Row | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Candidate answers the corpus as the release base does | **VERIFIED** | `scripts/semantic_phase0_base_differential.py` against `035b0dc8`: 252 corpus turns, **250 frozen eligible, 250 accounted, 0 public-path differences**. Eligibility is a pure function of BASE rows, frozen and printed before the candidate process starts |
| The candidate cannot shrink its own denominator | **VERIFIED** | The comparison used to iterate the rows the CANDIDATE returned, so omitting one eligible turn produced `compared=249, differences=0, exit 0` — the turn it could not answer left no trace. Iteration now runs over the **frozen id list**: every expected id resolves to exactly one verdict (matched / different / missing / errored), PASS requires those four to sum to `expected`, and unexpected or duplicated rows are counted separately and rejected. Driven at 1, 49, 50 and all 250 omissions, plus duplicates with conflicting payloads, reordering, mid-run truncation and smuggled rows. The old `compared < 200` threshold is gone as the primary guard — a threshold is not a completeness proof, and a 50-turn omission cleared it |
| Hermetic against the host | **VERIFIED** | `connect`, `connect_ex` and `create_connection` all refuse, on IPv4, IPv6 and unix sockets, loopback included, so a live local Ollama or daemon cannot be consulted. `connect_ex` was the hole a review walked through: it returns an errno rather than raising. Throwaway `VOOL_HOME` and cwd per run; base worktree verified pristine after |
| Prohibitions compose — a weaker fixture cannot replace a stronger one | **VERIFIED** | `tests/_network_seal` owns all three patches and refusal is a **union** over an explicit policy stack: a policy pushed later can only ADD refusals, releasing one leaves every other standing, and a `pytest_runtest_call` hook re-applies the union on top of anything a fixture installed directly — after every fixture, before the test body. 6 tests drive it across every surface × family, including a permissive patch installed by hand that is absorbed rather than obeyed, and a control proving the probe can see an escape |
| A killed proof cannot leave a defect in tracked source | **VERIFIED** | Journals are written before mutation, atomically and once. Recovery validates the complete journal set against tracked Git-HEAD bytes before any write; stale, duplicate, conflicting, untracked or HEAD-disagreeing provenance fails closed. Mutation-controller, pytest-autouse, SIGTERM/SIGINT and real-SIGKILL paths are exercised |
| No provider invocation added | **VERIFIED** | Production execution roles are machine-marked and propagated to concrete adapter/cloud-provider overrides; both free provider gateways and actual `CloudModelBroker` entrypoints are marked directly. The proof imports every shipped adapter and profiles marked code identity, catching private I/O paths, pre-captured aliases, overrides and threaded calls. Independent sabotage covers the direct free gateway, `LocalSubprocessAdapter.invoke`, private Ollama I/O and `CloudModelBroker.execute` |
| Caller-constructed or imported permission values cannot cause admission | **VERIFIED** | `AdmissionResult` has no admitted factory and rejects every caller construction with `admitted=True`. Its sole production creation site follows the exact real policy consultation and atomic one-use grant consumption. Caller-built `PermissionRecord`, imported `_issue_grant`, and manipulated `_ISSUED_GRANTS` values can look allowing but cannot enter admission. Grants remain operation-, argument- and occasion-bound; concurrent replay has exactly one winner |
| A grant cannot be spent on another operation | **VERIFIED** | `allows(op)` requires registry membership **and** an allow effect **and** that the grant names exactly `op`; `AdmissionResult` construction checks it against the operation about to execute, so a genuine read-only grant paired with a write intent raises. A registry lookup answering one operation with another operation's contract is rejected on identity |
| Registration ≠ authorization, at every layer | **VERIFIED** | A projection carries no executable: `run()` raises `NotAuthorizedError` and never calls an injected runner |
| No inferred parallel safety | **VERIFIED** | `can_run_in_parallel` is False always; `read_only` is a side-effect claim, not a concurrency claim |
| REACH distinguishes entered / offered / call-attempted | **VERIFIED** | The adapter method is resolved first and the record is written immediately before its call instruction. Primary structured/text, stream, response-constraint retry and response-control retry paths all use the seam; executor submissions use a fresh `copy_context`. It remains independent of the turn's self-reported `model_calls`, and telemetry rows can never set `preempted_by` |
| The invocation field claims only what the seam proves | **VERIFIED** | Four scenarios driven through `_invoke_manifest`: an adapter raising **before any provider I/O** was recorded as `model_invoked=True` — a receipt claiming a model ran when none did. Renamed to **`provider_call_attempted`**: the runtime resolved a provider and model, built the adapter, passed the health probe, and entered the adapter's task method. It does **not** prove the provider was reached or that a model produced anything; that would need a second observation on the return path, which this phase does not add. Control: a failed health probe means the adapter is built and never called, and is not counted |
| Every turn gets one accurate receipt | **VERIFIED** | 100% of driven turns, including one that raises and one driven with `source_context=None` |
| Receipt fidelity | **VERIFIED** | Seven distinct receipt tamperings each caught, naming the field |
| Spans bind to one named representation | **VERIFIED** | Astral emoji, NFC/NFD, whitespace rewrite, repeats, same-length-different-text, lone surrogates, a representation that lies about its normal form, and `overlaps` across differing text lengths. Search speaks the haystack's own form: all four haystack/needle combinations find the word with offsets measured against the form actually stored — `find()` used to fold the needle to a hard-coded NFC, so an NFD text could never match an NFC word |
| Nothing disappears; nothing runs on a missing answer | **VERIFIED** | Zero-admission proposals refused; a dependent on a rejected prerequisite gets `PREREQUISITE_FAILED` |
| LDAR resists restyling, renaming and moving | **VERIFIED** | Tuple/list/set/frozenset/dict/regex **and concatenation** measure identically; no exclusion list exists to absorb triggers into; runtime/plugin vocabulary is covered, not merely counted; an unlisted helper is still swept |
| LDAR reports what it cannot measure | **VERIFIED** | Current measured inventory: 3,461 tables, 10,912 distinct readable terms, 23,540 term slots and **531** opaque tables. Bare `ast.Constant` strings are measured. Runtime registrations and real plugin name/description/trigger vocabulary use the shipped loader contract; root, manifest, count or load failures populate `unavailable_sources`. The current installed `local-skills` manifest is unavailable, so the inventory is explicitly incomplete and the scalar score is `None` |
| The measurement stopped inventing terms | **VERIFIED** | The earlier fragment-harvesting defect remains closed: unresolved names and strings built from pieces are opaque, and comprehension-valued authority cannot vanish. The current count grew from the repaired 9,802 reading because bare strings and shipped plugin discovery are now included, not because opaque fragments are guessed |
| Making authority opaque cannot improve the metric | **VERIFIED** | Hiding vocabulary removes coverage, and removing coverage used to RAISE the score. An incomplete inventory now yields `score = None`; only `score_lower_bound` is available, and `score_is_publishable` is False |
| Every guard is load-bearing | **VERIFIED** | `scripts/semantic_phase0_mutation_matrix.py` — **75 mutations, 75 caught, 0 survivors, 0 harness errors**, sources restored. The latest audit rejected one stale selector, one redundant downstream journal kill and one gateway control satisfied by a sibling gateway; each was sharpened until the intended assertion alone turned red |
| Generalization *score* | **UNVERIFIED — deliberately unpublished** | Two reasons, both stated: a hermetic run sends most of the corpus to the model lane, and with the exclusion list removed almost every English sentence is "covered". The harness's properties are proven; the number needs live lanes and a discriminating coverage definition, both Phase-1 |
| CI parity | **PARTIAL — CURRENT BASELINE FAILURE DISCLOSED** | Final candidate: shards 1, 2 and 4 green; shard 3 fails one runtime-backbone test. The exact test fails alone on untouched `c23257da` with fresh `VOOL_HOME`: its mock registers `ollama-local:qwen3:8b`, while current host-derived local-only recommendations select three different model tags and the existing profile filter removes the mock manifest. It is outside the Semantic repair and was not waived or patched here |
| The `--workers 4` run found a candidate defect | **FIXED** | Shard 3 failed three tests in `tests/test_missing_textual_content_repair.py` with `network blocked in tests/semantic_phase0` — refusing their own **local fixture HTTP server**, in a file unrelated to that package. Cause was the candidate's: the escape-detection test lifted every seal policy and *pushed* them back, minting new tokens, so the module fixture holding the old token released nothing at teardown and the deny-all policy leaked into later modules for the rest of the process. Reproduced deterministically, fixed with token-preserving restore, pinned by a regression and a mutation. Re-run after the fix: ok shard 1/2/3/4, tree clean |
| The earlier `--workers 4` hang | **NOT REPRODUCED — not attributed** | Both trees ran green at 4 workers under equivalent conditions. One earlier candidate run showed a single shard failing on `test_no_import_critical_file_uses_a_311_only_symbol`, but that run was confounded: I was editing `tests/conftest.py` while shard 1 read it. The test passes alone on the same tree, and the frozen repeat was green. Recorded as unreproduced rather than as candidate-caused, base-caused or fixed — the evidence supports none of those. What is fixed regardless of cause is the property the hang threatened: a killed run can no longer leave tracked production source modified |
| Caller-created or stale permission state cannot cause admission | **VERIFIED** | Three conditions, each closing a demonstrated attack. **Type:** `AdmissionResult` accepted any truthy object with an `allows()` method; it now requires a `PermissionRecord`. **Arguments:** a grant obtained for `read_file(safe.txt)` could be re-attached to `read_file(/etc/shadow)` — same operation, a call the policy never saw; grants carry the digest of the arguments the policy was asked about. **Occasion:** a grant from an earlier admission could be replayed on a later turn; a grant is spent at the single point where it becomes an admission. `_issue_grant` remains importable and still produces an allowing *record* — stated, not denied — and still cannot admit |
| The attempt record means the adapter method was entered | **VERIFIED** | The marker sat above the dispatch branch, so a turn that raised while *choosing* an arm (a streaming probe) was reported as having entered one. Recorded per arm now, immediately before entry, driven with an adapter whose probe raises. Separately, the race and mux paths start raw threads, which begin with an **empty context** — the turn's recorder was invisible and a real provider call went unrecorded. Both workers run under `contextvars.copy_context()`, with a control proving a bare thread really does lose the context |
| The zero-extra-call proof covers the real execution boundary | **VERIFIED** | Structural production markers replace the public-name filter. Concrete adapter and cloud-provider overrides inherit boundary roles; `seal_provider_invocation`, `seal_direct_provider_invocation`, `CloudModelBroker.refresh_catalog` and `CloudModelBroker.execute` are direct boundaries. Code-identity profiling sees aliases captured before guard installation and private call chains |
| `CanonicalText` cannot be handed a lie on any path | **VERIFIED** | `of()` was safe; the public dataclass constructor accepted NFC text labelled NFD, NFD labelled NFC, an unknown representation, a lone surrogate, and a digest belonging to another string. `__post_init__` enforces form, name and digest on every construction path; `user_text.raw.v1` stays the declared escape hatch |
| The candidate cannot redefine the corpus it is judged on | **VERIFIED** | The executable controller re-execs with `-I` before non-builtin imports and removes `PYTHONPATH`/`PYTHONHOME`. An absolute trusted Git executable reads the corpus from the immutable object `c23257da:ops/semantic_phase0_frozen_corpus.json`; `_hashlib` computes the pinned digest in the controller. Candidate artifact/pin replacement, `scripts/hashlib.py`, `json.py`, cwd, PYTHONPATH, sys.path and symlink attacks have zero authority |
| Host-state drift is not charged to the candidate | **VERIFIED** | `host_unstable` excuses exact field claims only. Volatile-only rows can move; stable-only rows cannot; a row with multiple volatile claims and one stable candidate regression remains `DIFFERENT`. Different-field base drift and candidate self-drift excuse nothing, and accounting remains total |
| Subprocess proofs are hermetic in their own right | **VERIFIED — NARROW CLAIM** | The proof interpreter refuses all socket surfaces plus Python's supported `subprocess`, `multiprocessing`, `asyncio` subprocess and `os` fork/exec/spawn/system routes before measured code loads. A grandchild cannot contribute through those paths. This is not a kernel sandbox and makes no hostile-native/`ctypes` claim |
| A kill during journal handling cannot lose or corrupt the original | **VERIFIED** | Atomic/checksummed mechanics remain. Recovery additionally rejects a stale self-consistent journal, multiple journals for one path, conflicting originals, path escape, untracked source and disagreement with Git HEAD before applying any write. Unknown provenance stays visible and fails closed |
| Semantic resolution itself | **NOT STARTED** | `SemanticResolver` is a Protocol with no implementation |

**Superseded claims from candidate `bf2af45d`.** These were asserted as VERIFIED and were wrong:
"242/252 byte-identical OFF vs ON" (the wrong comparison — it never touched the base commit);
"zero extra model calls" proven by an import scan (defeated by a dynamic import); "23 mutations,
0 survivors" (several no longer touched a real seam); and REACH's `reached_model_lane`, which was
demonstrably false on a turn with zero model calls.

**Superseded claims from candidate `4d34ed2a`.** Also asserted as VERIFIED, also wrong:
"admission cannot be handed a substitute policy" (`grant_from_policy_decision` accepted the
engine's decision type from a caller, and that type is constructible); "`model_invoked` comes from
the finished turn's own `model_calls`" (that is the self-report the seam exists to replace);
"hermetic — every socket refused" (`connect_ex` returned an errno and walked through); "252 turns,
0 differences" with a per-run exclusion criterion (a drifting candidate could shrink its own
comparison); and "34 mutations, 34 caught" (four of those mutations were later shown to be
survivable, and two asserted nothing at all).

**A claim of mine that was wrong, corrected rather than quietly dropped.** I reported that the root
conftest's function-scoped guard *overwrote* this package's stricter module-scoped socket seal on
every test, and that other module-scoped suites shared that exposure. Probing all three surfaces
across IPv4/IPv6/unix/loopback shows it did not: that fixture captured `socket.socket.connect` live
and delegated to whatever was beneath it, so every target stayed refused and only the exception type
for port 11434 differed. The seal was never weakened. What remains true is narrower and still worth
fixing: the property rested on that one line, so composition is now a union rather than an accident.

**Found while repairing, and not covered by this work:** `tests/conftest.py`'s `allow_web_fallback`
pin is function-scoped, so any module-scoped fixture elsewhere in the suite that drives turns can
still reach the live web through the policy, as one corpus turn did.

**Found, not fixed.** A whitespace-only turn raises `ValueError: goal is required` from
`core/orchestration/task_envelope.py:31`; reproduces on the base commit, so it predates this work.
`tests/conftest.py`'s network isolation is **function-scoped**, so it is not in force while a
module-scoped fixture drives turns — one corpus turn reached the real internet before this was
found. Other module-scoped suites may share the exposure.
