**Regression, exact failure names.** Per-file isolation, the measurement that is
reproducible: every file that ever appeared as a difference, same command and venv on both
trees — base **3 failed / 328 passed**, candidate **3 failed / 328 passed**, failure sets
identical name for name. **Zero new failures.**

The full-corpus ordering is not a stable measurement, and the proof is at base: the whole
429-file corpus run **twice at the untouched base**, same code and command, gives
**58 failed** then **67 failed** — base disagrees with itself by 9 names, and those nine are
exactly the family a candidate run had reported as "new". Bisecting the corpus prefix
isolates it to a two-file pair that reproduces **8 failed / 44 passed at BOTH trees,
byte-identical**. Pre-existing order-dependence in the repository, not integration damage.

**Three bridges, identified separately.** The first comparison found 6 genuine new failures
in isolation; none was waved through as inherited. Each bridge makes a SECOND decision site
ask a rule the FIRST site already applies: the demand path now asks the rule `plan_turn`
uses before splitting an all-ordinary multi-part turn (gated on two authorities, because
the plain-task predicate alone also catches turns the demand path must own); the workspace
finalize veto is asked only when the read lane actually claims part of the turn; and the
conductor stands down only when a mixed-demand lane exists to stand down to. Every bridge
was reverted and bit, naming its own tests, with every file restored byte-exact.

**Gates.** `git diff --check a2308a26..HEAD` zero output. Ruff on the 82 touched files: 12
findings, all pre-existing (the same files carry 15 at base — a strict subset, zero new).
Secret scan over 47,393 diff lines: 0 hits.

**Cross-lane:** shared runtime across `core/`, `adapters/`, `tools/` — KAS re-verifies on
Windows after merge. Nothing in the Windows lane's matrix was read or edited.

## Safe bug reporter — privacy-preserving issue reporting, 2026-09-01, `build/safe-bug-reporter-20260901` (base `b3f5117f`)

Opt-in pipeline, off unless invoked: bounded capture → deterministic model-free redaction →
allowlist reconstruction → outbound secret scan → local/model-free reproduction synthesis →
exact-outbound-bytes preview with field/attachment removal → consent-hash-bound approval →
GitHub submission through the repo's one network door (`named_background_effect_scope
"bug_report.submit"`) → hash-chained metadata-only receipts. Wired end-to-end as
`/api/bug-report/{draft,preview,approve,submit,status,receipts}` (owner-local, standard POST
guard suite) and `vool bug-report {draft,preview,approve,submit,status,receipts}` (CLI).
Cloud restructuring of already-sanitized material exists behind
`VOOL_BUG_REPORT_CLOUD_SYNTHESIS` (default OFF, output re-scanned, sections restricted to
what the local rendering built). GitHub is fully mocked in every test; no real issue exists.

| Capability | Before (at `b3f5117f`) | Now | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sanitized LOCAL draft before anything leaves | nothing existed | **VERIFIED** (tests; mocked transport) | 131 tests green across 9 files incl. `tests/adversarial/test_bug_report_adversarial.py`; draft file on disk proven secret-free (`test_draft_is_created_locally_and_sanitized`) |
| Exact outbound bytes == approved bytes | — | **VERIFIED** (mocked transport) | sha256 of bytes received by the fake transport equals the previewed/consented hash (`test_submission_requires_approval_and_sends_exact_preview_bytes`, `test_submit_sends_the_consented_bytes_not_a_newer_preview`) |
| Approval bypass | — | **VERIFIED refused** | 9-case bypass matrix in `tests/test_bug_report_approval.py` (no consent, wrong hash, forged consent, disk tamper, cross-report, destination swap) |
| Failed submission keeps draft + safe retry | — | **VERIFIED** | `test_failed_submission_retains_the_local_draft`, `test_retry_after_failure_submits_the_same_bytes_without_reapproval` |
| Receipt truth (what left, not content) | — | **VERIFIED** | `test_receipt_records_what_left_but_not_content` + chain tamper detection; receipt rows carry hashes/sizes/names only |
| Live submission to a real GitHub repo | — | **UNVERIFIED (deliberate)** | Local-only rule: no credential spent, no issue created. Adapter uses the same door pattern as `self_update.check`; first live submission is the operator's step |

Sabotage matrix (mutate → named test fails → restored byte-identical): 8 guards proven —
vendor-secret layer, home-path layer, log shape allowlist, capture caps, approval binding,
fingerprint dedup, draft retention on failure, receipt metadata-only. Lint: lane files
`ruff check` clean; touched legacy files carry only the 5 base findings (strict subset of
base, zero new). Secret scan over the staged diff: only the synthetic adversarial fixtures.

## Safe bug reporter, amendment 1 — the flow inside the real chat UI, 2026-09-02, same branch

`Report a problem` now exists where a user actually meets a failure: on the failed task card
(`reportProblemForFailedRun`), in the sidebar footer, and in Settings beside build info. The
modal walks turn selection (failed turns from `/api/bug-report/candidates`, sanitized
server-side from `runtime_session_events`; conversation content never consulted) → diagnostics
checkboxes → expected/actual/repro editing → the exact outbound preview with redaction summary,
destination, fingerprint and attachment list → per-field/attachment removal → approval bound to
the exact preview hash → explicit submit → draft/submitting/submitted/failed states with safe
retry and the issue link. `POST /api/bug-report/update` edits drafts and clears consent (any
edit invalidates approval); `VOOL_BUG_REPORT_GITHUB_API_ROOT` points the adapter at a
self-hosted root (used by the local fake in the proof; door, scope and credential rules
unchanged).

| Failure keeps draft; retry works | mock-proven only | **VERIFIED in-browser** | fake armed `fail_once` → failed screen states the draft is retained locally → Retry → submitted |

Page JS remains console-free (test-enforced). Raw diagnostics live only in the app's own local event store — the source candidates sanitizes |

## M3 — an unsupported current claim can never be published (2026-09-01)

Branch `build/grounding-m3-publication-gate-20260901`, base `b3f5117f`. Shared runtime — KAS
inherits it and re-verifies on Windows. Local only; the operator's `:4187` was checked (nothing
listening) and never used; no local model was launched at any point.

| Capability | Apple/Linux | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A current-information turn completes a typed lifecycle before its bytes ship | **VERIFIED (live)** | `REQUIRED → RETRIEVED → BOUND_TO_SYNTHESIS → CLAIM_SUPPORTED → PUBLISHED`, enforced at `core.finalization.finalize_answer` — the one authority every served answer traverses through all six transport doors. On `b3f5117f` that seam consumed no grounding fact at all: it took `source_context` and deleted it on the next line, and M2's `evidence_synthesis_binding` stamp had zero production readers. Measured through the seam at base: `current_information_required=True`, evidence bound, M4 `coverage=none` with 3 unsupported claims — and the fabricated headlines committed byte for byte (`sha256 e331f55b…` on both sides). On this branch the same probe prints GREEN. |
| The committed fabrication cannot reach the wire | **VERIFIED (live, 3/3)** | Isolated daemon `:4313` (`~/vool/grounding-m3-home-fab`, mesh 49333, `remote_only`), real keyed Brave (4 sources, `lifecycle=succeeded`), completion fixed at the wire. State `refused` at `claim_supported`; no fabricated detail in the served bytes, the committed transcript or the conversation store; Activity carries the same verdict. |
| A grounded answer publishes untouched, with its links | **VERIFIED (live)** | Browser round **b4**, real Chromium through the shipped composer and the shipped model picker, the product's own paid-spend dialog answered: Brave keyed → `evidence_bound_to_synthesis` → `openrouter-byok:amazon/nova-micro-v1` → all five stages → `published`, links to `docs.python.org/3/whatsnew/3.14.html`, every committed URL a real anchor, DOM and store agree. **18/18 bullets.** |
| A typed observation lane mints its own support and is never gated | **VERIFIED (live, 3/3)** | Daemon `:4312` with every Ollama endpoint AND the OpenRouter transport on dead ports: the live-quote lane still answered from a real CoinGecko observation — `runtime_composed`, `support_origin=typed_observation`, no model call, no local provider used. |
| DIRECT and timeless answers are untouched | **VERIFIED** | Smalltalk, arithmetic, a file read and a timeless fact all publish byte-identically and the commit carries **no lifecycle at all** — the difference between "not applicable" and "not reached" is visible, and no claim matcher runs. |
| Partial truth: supported claims ship, unsupported ones are named | **PARTIAL** | Proven at the real commit seam in-process (test pack + publication-probe group 4: the supported line survives WITH its link, the invented line is withheld and named). **Served, the case was not reached** — the fixture's mixed completion never arrived at the gate with a supported half, so no served demonstration of the partial path exists. Stated rather than glossed. |
| Activity/API show every stage or the exact failed one | **VERIFIED (live)** | The commit envelope carries `grounding_lifecycle`; Activity carries `grounding_required` and `grounding_publication` rows, and the served drives assert the Activity row repeats the commit's verdict rather than offering a second opinion. |

**Five defects the live drive found that no test showed**, all fixed here: the gate could not
resolve its own turn (a served context carries the bare request uuid while the A0 door binds
`req:http:<uuid>`); the workflow-planner tool loop answered current questions with four real
Brave sources and no binding at all; a step bound by its one-line operator summary instead of the
tool's full response text refused a real, correctly grounded answer; `/api/chat/history` kept the
refused fabrication while the wire served the refusal; and a FAILED model call was counted as
authorship, so the runtime's own failure notice was rewritten.

**Two pre-existing findings recorded, not repaired here:** Brave returns results unrelated to the
query for several topics on this rig (developer docs for a volcano question), and "show me recent
news coverage about X" routes to `machine.inspect_specs` and answers with the host's hardware.

| `native.cmd_comma_bound` | same | **VERIFIED (in-process, real Cocoa)** — pywebview builds custom menu items with an EMPTY key equivalent; the post-start binder sets `,` + Command on a real `NSMenu` built the way pywebview builds it | `test_cmd_comma_is_bound_on_a_real_cocoa_menu` |
| `native.settings_menu_in_the_app_menu` | same | **PARTIAL** — the item is merged into the macOS application menu through pywebview's `__app__` sentinel, and the code degrades to no menu (never to no window) when the API is absent. Not seen on a real menu bar: no `.app` was built, and `vool_window.py main()` was not run because its supervisor stops a pre-existing runtime and would take the operator's daemon with it | `test_the_menu_degrades_to_nothing_when_the_api_is_missing` |
| `settings.keys_are_their_own_group` | same | **VERIFIED (corrected here)** — keys were first filed under Models & Providers. One store holds `llm.cloud.*` (8), `search.web.*` (7) and slots that are neither, so they are not a model setting; they have their own group, grouped by family, with the add-form selector built from both server catalogues | `test_keys_are_their_own_group_because_the_store_holds_more_than_models`; `shots/keys.png` |
| `settings.runtime_minted_keys_are_not_removable` | same | **VERIFIED (hazard removed)** — the store also holds `blackbox.cas.keys`, the AES-256-GCM keyring for the Blackbox CAS with no plaintext fallback. The first key list gave it a Remove button; one click would have made every sealed blob unreadable, on a key the user never supplied. Listed and marked `system` now, with the Remove path returning before the button exists | `test_a_runtime_minted_key_is_listed_but_never_removable`; `shots/keys.png` |
| `settings.name_and_signature_are_memory_not_settings` | same | **VERIFIED (corrected here)** — `migrate_legacy_profile_fields` moved `user_address` and `email_signature` into the Operator Profile and empties the JSON fields at every boot. Controls on that legacy shim duplicated the Memory list, and the signature control wrote a field the next boot empties — it would have read empty while the value stayed in effect. Both rows removed; `email_signature` is out of the prefs authority; the write-set==read-set invariant is now a test, and re-adding the field turns it red | `test_the_prefs_endpoint_reads_back_exactly_what_it_accepts` (sabotage-verified) |
| `settings.answer_language_reaches_the_policy` | same | **VERIFIED (new control over an existing policy)** — the scoped Operator Profile `language` item, written through `/api/profile/remember` and cleared through `/api/profile/forget`. Live: the next turn derives `{expected_language: lt, reason: operator_preference}`; `answer in Polish` still overrides it (`explicit_output_request`); a chat-scoped item wins in that chat and `lt` holds elsewhere; an unresolvable stored value is skipped rather than guessed; clearing returns to inherited behaviour. The picker imports the policy's own 50-language table, so it cannot offer one the policy cannot read | `drive_answer_language.py` (12 checks); `shots/answer-language.png` |
| `settings.answer_language_claims_nothing_extra` | same | **VERIFIED (copy)** — the control states that it does not translate the interface (English only; no i18n machinery exists) and does not make the model equally good in every language or change tool and web-search behaviour | `shots/answer-language.png` |
| `settings.search_key_signups_come_from_the_catalogue` | same | **VERIFIED** — the "getting a web-search key" list renders each provider's own `signup_url` and `free_tier` from `core/search_providers.py`, so nothing is a claim this page invented; a provider whose free tier ended reads as such (Brave: "card required (free tier ended Feb 2026)") rather than as an offer | `shots/keys.png` |
| `settings.legacy_panel_removed` | — | **UNVERIFIED / not done** — the in-chat settings modal is no longer reachable from the UI, but its markup and JS remain in `core/vool_chat_page.py`. Removing them is ~800 lines in a file the chat-repair lane is actively working in; handed off rather than done here | `SETTINGS_CENSUS.md` |

## Desktop pet — transparency and free desktop movement (2026-09-07, `fix/desktop-pet-repair-20260907`, base `07f69998`)

**Method:** `tools/pet_native_proof.py`, on **two attached displays** — a 2× primary at (0,0) 2240×1260
and a **1× secondary at x = −1920**, i.e. a monitor left of the primary with mixed scaling. Every row
below is read back from the window server (`-[NSWindow level]`, `-[NSWindow frame]`,
`+[NSWindow windowNumberAtPoint:belowWindowWithWindowNumber:]`) or measured in pixels from a screen
capture of a backdrop the harness owns. No row is inferred from CSS or from a bridge returning `ok`.
Raw: `evidence/pet/{baseline,repaired}/report.json`, `evidence/pet/sabotage/sabotage.json`.

| Row | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Detached pet opens a real native window | **VERIFIED** | Production flags were rejected by pywebview (`ValueError: #00000000 is not a valid hex triplet color`) before any window existed — the pet could never leave the app. After the fix the real library accepts them, and `detach_companion` called from a worker thread (the bridge's own path) adopts a real NSWindow. |
| No rectangular backdrop around the pet | **VERIFIED** | Widest run of the ground-bar colour inside the pet's box: **264 px @2× / 132 px @1× → 0 px** on both displays. Caption card: **82 / 38 px → 0 px**. `evidence/pet/repaired/screen{0,1}_{light,dark,otherapp}.png`. |
| True transparent window and web content | **VERIFIED** | `isOpaque=false`, `hasShadow=false`, `background_alpha=0.0`; all four corner probes equal the backdrop over a light background, a dark one, and a **separate process's** window, on both displays. |
| Transparent margins do not block clicks | **VERIFIED** | The OS routes a click 6 pt inside the pet's corner to the **other application**, on both displays. Before: to the pet. |
| Pet dragging still works | **VERIFIED** | Same call with the cursor on the character routes to the **pet**, on both displays — the inverse defect (whole window click-through) is not present. |
| Does not obstruct dialogs | **VERIFIED** | Level read back = **3 (NSFloatingWindowLevel)**, `above_modal_panel: false`. Before: **25 (NSStatusWindowLevel)**, above `NSModalPanelWindowLevel` (8). |
| Native movement across monitors | **VERIFIED** | 8 `setFrameOrigin:` → `frame` round-trips across **both physical displays**, all exact, including negative-x origins (−1912, −1048, −184). |
| Pet does not steal the keyboard | **VERIFIED** | `focus=False` → `canBecomeKeyWindow` false; re-show uses `orderFrontRegardless` instead of pywebview's `show()`, which calls `activateIgnoringOtherApps:`. |
| Position survives a monitor going away | **PARTIAL** | Recovery, straddling, the top-edge fling and an empty display list are asserted as **geometry** (simulated topology). Physical **movement** across monitors is VERIFIED above; a physical **hot-unplug** was not performed. |
| Spaces / full-screen policy | **UNVERIFIED** | `CanJoinAllSpaces \| FullScreenAuxiliary \| Stationary` is set and the bits are read back (273). Whether the pet *appears* over another app's full-screen space was not exercised. |
| Dragging by a real mouse gesture | **UNVERIFIED** | Every position result is a real OS window move read back from the window server; driving a gesture needs synthetic events and therefore Accessibility permission, which was not requested. |
| Pet overhead | **VERIFIED** | The cursor-tracking timer: 206 ticks, **1.39 ms mean** at a 60 ms interval, and it is **stopped** whenever the pet is hidden. Measured from the tracker's own counters, not claimed to be free. |
| Packaged app boots with the repair | **VERIFIED** | `0.5.0-closed-test+macos+03a6111ace1d`, `source_tree_clean: true`. An earlier launch of this build opened its native window, loaded the chat page and reached the detach path — which is how two defects invisible to the suite were found (script-mode import order, and the asynchronous Cocoa window registration). |

**Two side effects this lane caused on this box, recorded so nobody rediscovers them:** launching the
test build **loaded a local model** (a 5.4 GB `qwen3:0.6b` runner, 3 s after boot, despite
`VOOL_REGISTER_INSTALLED_OLLAMA_MODELS=0`; unloaded afterwards), and it **coincided with the
operator's running VOOL.app restarting** — `_single_instance()` flocks
`~/Library/Application Support/VOOL/window.lock`, one path per user, shared by every build on the
machine along with `app.log` and `open.log`. Close the main app before running a second build.

## Two-intent conjunction routing — the live-data lane no longer swallows a demand it does not serve (2026-09-07, `fix/two-intent-routing-20260907`, base `f94d7652`, tip `4e223fe7`)

**Method:** served source daemon from this checkout on a sealed fixture transport (quotes, a news feed,
its pages; every other host refused), assertions read from `runtime_session_events` — which sub-requests
opened and what ran under each — never from the prose; the wallet lane's real-network probe re-run on
five wordings; in-process grain family; sabotage with sha256-checked restores; the identical neighbouring
pack run at BASE for attribution. Record: `validation-logs/two-intent-routing-20260907/TWO_INTENT_ROUTING_FIX.md`.

| Row | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| "get me latest on Iran and oil prices pls" dispatches BOTH halves | **VERIFIED** | Base f8937f80/f94d7652: one whole-turn request, live-data plan, "get me latest on Iran — not dispatched". After `70e91563`: sub-request `get me latest on Iran` → news retrieval (google_news_rss) + model lane; `and oil prices pls` → typed plan, Brent served; no "not dispatched" (`logs/green_served_final_4e223fe7.log`, `logs/two_intent_probe_postfix.stdout.log`). |
| The class, not the wording: reversed order and unrelated topics/assets | **VERIFIED** | Served: `update me on Ukraine plus bitcoin price`, `oil prices and the latest on Iran`, `tell me about Sudan and the silver price` — each opens the unserved half as its own sub-request with a model call or retrieval under it (5/5). Probe: `tell me the latest about Mali and the gold price` likewise. In-process family of 17 (6 clean, 6 sloppy, 4 reversed): 32/32, RED 16/16 at base. |
| One-request coordinations stay one typed plan | **VERIFIED** | `gold and silver price`, `gold, silver and bitcoin price`, `get me the price of gold and silver please`, `weather in Rome and gold price`, `convert 100 usd to eur and then to gold`, `weather in Rome, in celsius please`, `1000 EUR to RUB, gold with it` — one execution unit before and after; the R1g gauntlet's 23 pinned counts unchanged. |
| No lane pre-empts a conjunction no lane reads | **VERIFIED** | `latest on Nvidia and the euro rate` stays whole, reaches a retrieval/model call, never reports "not dispatched" (served control). |
| Sabotage names the test | **VERIFIED** | 5/5 caught: every-headless-fragment-rides (16 family tests), drop-the-binder (4 one-request controls), invert-the-predicate (26), drop-the-continuation-shape (4 incl. the gauntlet's `cur-chain`), lexical classifier (5). `logs/sabotage/sabotage_report.json`. |
| A raising family binder cannot change a route | **VERIFIED** | Found by `tests/test_live_data_fail_closed_invariant.py` in the packs (the only red the fix caused); `4e223fe7` keeps the pre-binder grain when a binder raises; the invariant file is green again. |
| Neighbouring packs (51 files) | **VERIFIED** | post-fix 8F/1092P → identical run at BASE 7F/1061P (the same seven, pre-existing) → final tree 7 failed / 1061 passed (6m29s) -- the identical seven pre-existing reds; logs/packs_final_4e223fe7.log. |
| Frozen selection (`run_selection.sh`) | **VERIFIED** | 1 failed / 1113 passed / 2 skipped (7m32s); the one red is tests/test_runtime_event_text_rendering.py::ThreeLaneFailureStreamTests::test_each_failed_lane_names_its_own_cause_in_the_stream, the same single red every previous selection run carried (combined_45a22cf9, devauth_07f69998, wg_20e5b9ce); the 37 new tests passed inside it (validation-logs/comparison-truth-overnight-20260906/logs/two_intent_4e223fe7.log + .junit.xml). |
| "+" / "&" / "n" as coordinators; anaphoric second demand ("... and the weather there"); a coin-index alias binding prose ("a haiku about rain") | **BROKEN** (recorded, not repaired) | The mint reads only and/also/plus/then/commas, so `get me latest on iran + oil prices` mints ONE unit and the live lane still claims whole; an anaphoric fragment rides by design and the clock lane claims the fused unit; the recognizer seam is the coin index. Next seam for the first two: `answer_coverage._unit_spans` and the whole-turn claim law at MINT grain. |
## macOS architecture compatibility — native Apple Silicon without Rosetta (`fix/mac-architecture-compatibility-20260907`, base `45a22cf9`, 2026-09-07)

Driven on Apple M4 / macOS 26.5.1 (build 25F80). Corrected artifact
`0.5.0-closed-test+macos+arm64+c0aa516cbadc`, DMG sha256 `f559712d…97ddbf`.

| Capability | Windows | Apple | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| The shipped `.app` launches NATIVE arm64, not under Rosetta | N/A (Rosetta is an Apple-silicon concept) | **BROKEN at base → VERIFIED FIXED this lane** | Root cause was NOT an Intel file — all 96 Mach-O files in the bundle carry arm64. `Contents/MacOS/VOOL` is a bash SCRIPT, so LaunchServices has no Mach-O header and **forges** `LSArchitecturePriority=(x86_64, arm64)` — visible as `more flags: forged-arch-priority` in `lsregister -dump` and **absent from the on-disk Info.plist** (`plutil -extract` exits 1; `grep -c` = 0). A/B through LaunchServices, single variable: no key → `uname=x86_64 proc_translated=1`; `LSArchitecturePriority=[arm64]` → `uname=arm64 proc_translated=0`. Repeated with the REAL shipped Info.plist → `proc_translated=1`. The `P_TRANSLATED` reader was validated with a positive control first (`arch -x86_64 /bin/sleep` → translated=YES). Corrected artifact driven live via LaunchServices (isolated `HOME`, port 49733): `/healthz ok`, commit `c0aa516cbadc`, **every pid `translated=no`, executable slices `arm64`**. |
| The bundled `ollama` runs its arm64 slice, not its Intel slice | N/A | **BROKEN at base → VERIFIED FIXED this lane** | The bundled `ollama` is universal (x86_64+arm64) — not itself a defect. Under the forged priority it was *selected* as x86_64: the REAL shipped binary run as a child of the launcher read `p_flag=0x00034004 translated=YES`; with the fixed plist, `p_flag=0x00004004 translated=no`. This is the "component" in the macOS string `body.launch.embedded.app.notification` = "This version of “%@” **includes a component** that will not work with a future release of macOS", extracted from `Ecosystem.framework/…/Localizable.loctable` on this host. `ecosystemd` records actual Rosetta **execution** events and explicitly excludes universal apps, so a slice census alone can never see this. |
| The advertised minimum macOS is one the bytes can actually reach | N/A | **BROKEN at base → VERIFIED FIXED this lane** | `LSMinimumSystemVersion` was a hardcoded `12.0` nothing derived. Measured: `vool-devauth` required **macOS 26.0** (compiled by `swiftc` with **no `-target`**, inheriting the build host) and `ollama` requires **14.0** — so on macOS 12/13 the app could not start and the helper was a dyld failure rather than a typed "unavailable". Floor is now `14.0`, derived from measured deployment targets; the Swift helper takes `-target <arch>-apple-macos<floor>` (`vtool -show-build`: `minos 26.0` → `minos 14.0`). |
| Architecture is declared at build time, never inherited from the build host | N/A | **VERIFIED this lane** | `--arch` / `--min-macos` with validation; embedded CPython requested by architecture (`cpython-<v>-macos-<uv arch>-none`); the staged interpreter's slices re-read with `lipo` rather than trusting uv; wheels resolved with `--python-platform` so a host cache cannot contaminate the bundle; `arch` + `macos_min_version` stamped into `BUILD_MANIFEST.json`. Cross-architecture builds are **refused** — the bundle's own interpreter must run on the build machine or verification proves nothing. |
| A build-time gate refuses an artifact that disagrees with what it advertises | N/A | **VERIFIED this lane** | `installer/bundle/macos_arch_gate.py` runs inside `verify_bundle`. On the artifact **as shipped** it fails with 3 real defects and exact paths (forged launch contract; `vool-devauth` 26.0 > 12.0; `ollama` 14.0 > 12.0). On the corrected artifact: **PASS**. Sabotage: each of the four checks removed in turn reds exactly the tests naming that cause (launch-contract → 2, slice → 2, deployment → 1, symlink-escape → 1); controls return to 17/17. A universal dependency carrying the correct slice **passes** — asserted, because an x86_64 slice beside arm64 is not a defect. Every fixture is compiled by the test with `clang`; **no delivered artifact was read, mutated or stripped**. The gate also caught a defect in its own wiring on the first real build (a defaulted floor) — a missing floor is now an error. |
| Machine-readable architecture census of the bundle | N/A | **VERIFIED this lane** | `installer/bundle/macos_arch_census.py`, pure-stdlib Mach-O parser: per-file slices, deployment targets, linked dylibs, escaping symlinks, dSYM separated. **Validated against `file(1)`: identical file sets.** The "238 Mach-O files" figure is `file(1)`'s per-**slice** output lines: 66 universal × 3 + 30 thin × 1 = 228 lines for **96 distinct files**. Corrected artifact: 83 extensions, 10 dylibs, 3 executables; arm64 deployment targets `11.0` ×94 and `14.0` ×2; **0** symlinks escaping. |
| Intel (x86_64) support | N/A | **NOT BUILT / NOT TESTED — explicitly not claimed** | No Intel artifact exists and none is claimed. Chosen approach is **separate per-architecture artifacts, not universal2**: the bundle embeds a python-build-standalone CPython (no universal2 distribution exists) and 83 native extensions from per-architecture wheels; lipo-ing only the launcher over an arm64-only Python environment would claim Intel support and fail at the user's first import. Prerequisites, exact: an **x86_64 build runner**; `uv python install cpython-3.11-macos-x86_64-none`; an x86_64 wheel set (`--python-platform x86_64-apple-darwin`, already wired); an x86_64 `ollama` (vendor binary already universal — no work); and **genuine Intel hardware to test**. A Rosetta run will not be labelled Intel support. Already proven cross-buildable from this host: the Swift device-auth helper (`--arch x86_64 --min-macos 12.0` → real `x86_64` Mach-O, `minos 12.0`). |
| macOS 14.0, the declared floor | N/A | **NOT TESTED** | No host at that version. The floor is derived from measured deployment targets — evidence about the bytes, not a boot on 14.0. |
| The reported macOS notification itself | N/A | **NOT CAPTURED** | No delivered notification was observed. The trigger analysis rests on the OS's own `Ecosystem.framework` strings, `ecosystemd`'s `rosetta_*` tables, and reproduced translation. No OS warning state was cleared; Rosetta was neither installed nor removed. |

**Rosetta timeline (Apple, checked 2026-09-07):** macOS 26.4+ may notify on Rosetta-reliant launches; **macOS 27** is the final release supporting Rosetta; **macOS 28+** keeps it only for certain older unmaintained games. Sources: `developer.apple.com/news/?id=w5ngl9k2`, `support.apple.com/en-us/102527`.

**Neighbours:** 123 passed across the eight packaging families. The wallet + device-auth family is 32 failed / 465 passed / 1 skipped — **failure sets identical at base `45a22cf9`** (diff empty both directions), so zero regressions from this lane.

**Cross-lane: shared files.** `installer/bundle/build_macos_app.sh`, `core/wallet/device_auth.py` and `tests/test_macos_app_packaging.py` are also touched by the pet and wallet lanes — hand-merge, per-hunk map in `docs/MACOS_ARCHITECTURE_COMPATIBILITY_20260907.md` §9. `LSRequiresNativeExecution` was tested and **does not work** for this case; `LSArchitecturePriority` is the key that does. **KAS: nothing here transfers to Windows** — Rosetta and LaunchServices are Apple-only.

### Intel artifact — amendment, same lane, 2026-09-07

An **x86_64 artifact now exists**, built from the same commit as the arm64 one (`8baa9d6b1584`):
`0.5.0-closed-test+macos+x86_64+8baa9d6b1584`, `VOOL-x86_64.dmg` sha256 `137c61cf…f584e1`.

| Capability | Windows | Apple | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| A genuine Intel (x86_64) artifact exists | N/A | **BUILT — NOT TESTED** | Every Mach-O carries x86_64 (71 thin + 16 universal, 0 missing), 0 unresolved load paths, embedded CPython 3.11.15 `x86_64`, `vool-devauth` `x86_64` `minos 14.0`, `LSArchitecturePriority=[x86_64]`, architecture gate PASS at `--arch x86_64`. **It has never run on an Intel Mac.** It was started on this Apple Silicon host **under Rosetta** — `/healthz` ok, chat door 200, every pid `translated=True`, which is what an Intel binary on an ARM host must do. That is coherence, not Intel verification, and it is labelled as the separate experiment the mission asks for. |
| The artifact declares its own verification status | N/A | **VERIFIED** | The manifest carries `build_host_arch`, `built_under_translation` and `natively_verified` in the bundle itself: arm64 → `natively_verified: true`; x86_64 → `built_under_translation: true, natively_verified: false`. A reader of the artifact alone can tell them apart. Cross-arch builds are opt-in (`VOOL_ALLOW_CROSS_ARCH_BUILD=1`) and the build says out loud that the result is not natively verified. |
| The bundle is assembled from wheels, never compiled from source | N/A | **VERIFIED — FIXED here** | The first Intel attempt made uv fall back to an sdist and run `maturin`/`cargo` to build `cryptography` on the build host: arbitrary build code during packaging, irreproducible, needing an undeclared Rust toolchain. `--no-build` forbids it; wheel-only resolves for both architectures (`cryptography 48.0.1` on x86_64). A missing wheel is now a legible resolve-time failure instead of a silent source build. |
| `--arch` reaches every architecture-derived value | N/A | **VERIFIED — FIXED here** | Found twice: values derived from `TARGET_ARCH` are computed near the top of the build script, before `--arch` is parsed. The build id was one; the uv interpreter/wheel spellings were another, and that one sent `--arch x86_64` to uv as the host's `aarch64` request — uv returned an arm64 CPython for an Intel build, caught only by the staged-slice check. Both recomputed after parsing, with a test asserting the general property. |

**Still missing for Intel, exactly one thing:** genuine Intel hardware (or a native x86_64 macOS 14+
runner) on which to launch `VOOL-x86_64.dmg`. Until then the Intel row stays **BUILT, NOT TESTED**.
